# Study 2 source notes

Public pages only, for the pre-registered Study 2 in `BACKTEST-2.md`. Nothing here is a ranking result. No Qloo or LLM ranking call
was made. Show lists are compiled later and get their own section below.

## Eligibility check (14-18 Oct 2026)
- Every page was read with one fetcher that enforces robots.txt (RFC 9309 matching). It reads the rules for its own token and for `*`,
  and also every group that names an Anthropic agent (ClaudeBot, Claude-User, Claude-SearchBot, Claude-Web, anthropic-ai); all of them
  must allow the path. It waits at least 2.5 s between requests to a host (6 s on web.archive.org until 16 Oct; see the next item),
  honours any Crawl-delay, stops a host for good at its first 403 or 429, and refuses ticketing, aggregator, social and setlist sites
  outright.
- The rule for web.archive.org changed on 16 Oct 2026, after the archive answered a 429 (20:17 UTC) and the fetcher stopped it: a
  first 429 now paused the archive for one hour, and its requests were at least 15 s apart (from 20:30 UTC); reads resumed at
  21:18 UTC. From 17 Oct (03:00 server time, UTC+3) the gap is 30 s, Retry-After is honoured and a 429 ends the night. Before that,
  a second 429 (16 Oct 21:38 UTC, 00:38 on 17 Oct server time) stopped the archive for good. That stop left 41 scouted venues
  undecided, all but two (Lido Berlin and Woodward Theater) for want of archive copies (`candidates.md`, "Where the check stopped"). The
  gap is enforced between request starts under a per-host lock; `requests.log` stamps each line when the request ends, so two log
  lines can be closer than the gap after a slow answer.
- Archive copies from 17 Oct 2026 on: every web.archive.org request after the stop came from one deterministic client,
  `prefetch.py`, which follows a fixed plan of CDX queries and copies and sends each request through polite.py. It runs only between
  20:00 and 08:00 server time and stops itself at 07:45; requests to the archive are at least 30 s apart; Retry-After is honoured (a
  503 pauses the archive); a 429 or a 403 ends the night; and the saved bodies are capped at 200 MB in all (no request starts unless
  a full 8 MB body still fits). The first night ran from 17 Oct 20:03:37 to 18 Oct 07:45:21 server time, when the window ended, and
  made 1,401 requests for 179.9 MB. The second night started on 18 Oct at 21:27:37 with 102 tasks and stopped at 22:18:49 ('plan
  done'); the client was started again at 22:19:22 with 105 tasks and stopped at 22:55:54 ('plan done'). Then the prefetch state file
  held 1,575 requests and 192,058,589 bytes in all, and 103 of its 105 tasks were complete. The two open tasks,
  `elig:themagicbag-com:1` and `settle:oldtownschool-org-gap`, got no answer to a CDX query (22:19:52 and 22:20:23) and queued
  nothing; they are retried on a later run. Two tasks added to the prefetch plan at 23:06, `settle:cassiopeia-berlin-de-club` and
  `settle:cassiopeia-berlin-de-sommer`, have not run yet. The prefetch log has no 429 and no 403, and two pauses after a 503
  (18 Oct 00:14 and 21:27 server time). The settle runs of 17-18 Oct and the Off Broadway compile of 18 Oct made no request of their
  own: they read only the copies this client had saved (the presenter check that followed the compile made the last 12 lines of the
  log, 18 Oct 22:59-23:01 server time, to press and company pages, 2 of them refusals). These requests are in `requests.log` too and
  are counted in the totals below: after 16 Oct 23:25 UTC the log has the same 1,575 web.archive.org requests, and 2 refusals logged
  while a 503 paused the archive.
- User-Agent: `Mozilla/5.0 (research; dark-night-desk)`. GET only.
- 18,664 requests in all, logged from 14 Oct 19:29 UTC to 18 Oct 20:01 UTC (the last line counted is at 20:01:17 UTC, 23:01 on 18
  Oct server time), to 1,804 hosts: the scouted venues' sites (including the 15 Oct retry of hosts that failed the first sweep and
  the checks of 16 Oct), their terms pages, platform terms, the Internet Archive (2,338 requests) and press pages. 1,896 of them are
  robots.txt reads. 307 of the 18,664 log lines, 78 of them for web.archive.org, are refusals that the fetcher logged without
  sending the request (197 because robots.txt was unreachable or answered 5xx, 78 while the archive was paused or stopped, 26
  robots.txt disallows, 4 on hosts stopped after a 403 or 429, 2 with an empty URL); 18,357 requests were sent. Up to 16 Oct 23:25
  UTC (the last line of the earlier count, 23:25:08 UTC, 02:25 on 17 Oct server time) the log held 6,577 lines to 646 hosts, 761 of
  them for web.archive.org and 737 robots.txt reads, with 222 refusals (76 for web.archive.org) and 6,355 requests sent; up to 16
  Oct 06:09 UTC it held 3,568 lines, 138 of them refusals. Per-venue counts for the venues in `venues.csv` are below.
- Venues that sent a 403, a bot check or a robots.txt refusal were not read in any other way. One exception was found afterwards and is
  closed by Amendment 14: Lodge Room's own show archive sits behind a bot check, while its events feed was open and was read during the
  check. Lodge Room is not used, and nothing read from it is used.
- During early scouting (14 Oct 2026), before this fetcher was written, 21 requests to five hosts went to paths their robots.txt
  disallows, and one more read a web.archive.org copy of such a page. The causes were chiefly a robots parser bug (wildcard rules) and
  a generic user agent that skipped the rules written for Claude's agents. None of that data was used: the bodies are quarantined
  pending deletion, and none of the five venues is in `venues.csv`. A few hosts were fetched before their robots.txt was read; it
  allowed those paths. A few pages of one site whose terms forbid automated reading were read; nothing was saved or used. The fetcher
  used since then reads the rules for ClaudeBot, the other Anthropic agents and `*` separately, and all of them must allow a path.
- One narrow exception was added to the fetcher's refusal list on 15 Oct: Etix's own policy pages
  (`www.etix.com/ticket/online3/...privacy|purchase|terms|legal...jsp` and `hello.etix.com/`), so that the terms of the Rockhouse Partners
  sites could be judged. Etix listings and ticket pages are still refused. 3 page requests and 2 robots.txt reads were made.
- One web.archive.org copy of The Garage's gig listing (Glasgow, 13 Mar 2026) is a hosting check page served to the Archive's crawler.
  Under reading 5 the venue is not used and its other copies are not used either; our own live requests to it got no check.
- Verdicts for every scouted venue: `candidates.md`. Readings of §3: Amendment 14.

## Platform and archive terms (read 14-16 Oct 2026)
- **Fatsoma** (https://www.fatsoma.com/policies): "Website" takes in "such other URL or URLs as Fatsoma may ... decide by means of which
  Fatsoma may provide the Service"; clause 5.1.6 forbids copying content. Unclear whether it reaches the venue sites it builds, so they
  are not used (Deaf Institute, Gorilla).
- **Music Glue** (https://www.musicglue.com/policies): its "Website" expressly includes pages hosted or powered by Music Glue. Not used
  (Hug & Pint).
- **Squarespace** (https://www.squarespace.com/terms-of-service, effective 27 Mar 2025; https://www.squarespace.com/acceptable-use-policy,
  updated 24 Feb 2020): the Services are defined openly ("including without limitation"), the visitors of a customer's site are called
  users of the Services, and Acceptable Use Policy 1.6 forbids access "by any means other than our publicly supported interfaces (for
  example, scraping, spidering or crawling)". Unclear, so not used (The Lilypad, Hyde Park Book Club, Button Factory, Théâtre Fairmount).
- **Weebly** (https://termsandprivacy.weebly.com/tos-en.html, updated 27 Nov 2023), host of Make-Out Room: "THESE TERMS APPLY SOLELY TO
  THE SERVICE. ... WE HOST WEBSITES FOR CERTAIN THIRD PARTIES ("THIRD PARTY SITES") ... YOUR ACCESS OR USE OF ANY THIRD PARTY SITE IS
  GOVERNED BY THE TERMS APPLICABLE TO SUCH THIRD PARTY SITE." The venue site has no terms, so it passes.
- **Wix** (https://www.wix.com/about/terms-of-use, last revised 9 Jul 2026), host of The Grand Social: the terms forbid access to
  "User Content", the content of its customers' sites, "through any means or technology (e.g. scraping and crawling), other than our
  publicly supported interfaces". They call a customer's visitors "End Users", but no sentence leaves those sites to their own terms.
  Unclear, so not used (The Grand Social), as with Fatsoma and Squarespace. Pages already read from it are not used (Amendment 14).
- **Webflow** (https://webflow.com/legal/terms, effective 15 Nov 2023; https://webflow.com/legal/aup, effective 6 Oct 2026), host of
  Horseshoe Tavern, Cinetol, The Pour House and Off Broadway: ToS 4.4, "Webflow does not have a direct relationship with any of your
  End Users"; the Acceptable Use Policy forbids scraping only of material "outside of what is intentionally made available". The
  terms bind Webflow's customers, not the visitors of their sites, so they do not decide the venue, as with Weebly. Read on 15 Oct
  2026 (3 requests with robots.txt).
- **Rockhouse Partners / Etix.** 18 scouted venue sites say "Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company", linked to hello.etix.com;
  some (Old Rock House) give Etix's own pages as their privacy and purchase policy. Those pages
  (https://www.etix.com/ticket/online3/privacy-policy.jsp, .../purchase-policy.jsp) return only a script shell that loads a bot-defence
  check, so their text cannot be read, and hello.etix.com links no terms of use. Whether Etix's terms reach the visitors of these sites
  is unclear, so they are not used (Larimer Lounge, Kung Fu Necktie, Old Rock House and 15 more; list in `candidates.md`), as with
  Fatsoma. Read on 15 Oct 2026.
- **Ticket widgets.** The Wardrobe's event pages embed a See Tickets price widget, and Lizard Lounge's an Eventbrite one. The listings
  are the venues' own WordPress pages and feeds; no widget content and no ticket site is read.
- **Hosting providers** (GoDaddy, Pantheon, CarrierZone) bind their account holders, not visitors, and are not the site's terms.
- **Internet Archive** (web.archive.org). Its Terms of Use (dated 31 Dec 2014) bind anyone who reads an archived page on its site, so they
  bind us directly. The live page https://archive.org/about/terms.php is a JavaScript shell; its text comes from
  https://archive.org/metadata/ia-page-terms, read on 15 Oct 2026, and matches word for word the server-rendered copy of 4 Nov 2024
  (https://web.archive.org/web/20241104205241id_/https://archive.org/about/terms.php). They have no clause on robots, crawling, scraping
  or request rates, and web.archive.org's robots.txt allows the snapshot and CDX paths used. The change notice
  (https://blog.archive.org/?p=9040, 30 Dec 2014) shows that the bans on copying offsite and on commercial use were taken out. What
  remains: access is "for scholarship and research purposes only", use is "limited to noninfringing or fair use", no personal data is
  collected about anyone, and the Archive's servers are not interfered with. This back-test is pre-registered research published with
  the Archive cited; its rows are show dates and titles (facts) and hold no data about private persons; requests to web.archive.org
  were at least 6 s apart at the start of each request, and at least 15 s apart from 16 Oct 20:30 UTC (of the 685 requests sent, 36
  got a 503, 23 timed out or failed and 2 got a 429; 76 more were refused by the fetcher and not sent). So the terms are met. They do
  not replace the venue's own terms, which are judged as for a live site. Used for The EARL, Bottom of the Hill, VEGA, Club Passim,
  Horseshoe Tavern, The Pearl, Badehaus, Tolhuistuin, Privatclub, The Burren, MilkBoy Philadelphia, PhilaMOCA, Yoshi's, Blue
  Strawberry and Off Broadway, and in part for Brudenell, The Wardrobe, Le Ministère, The Castle Hotel, Night & Day Cafe, Bitterzoet,
  Kings and The Pour House. From 17-18 Oct also used for Center Stage, Martyrs', Old Town School of Folk Music, Reggies Chicago,
  Beachland Ballroom & Tavern, Music Box Supper Club and Amager Bio & BETA, and in part for Green Mill.

## Second pass (19 Oct 2026)
- Sample: the 931 lines listed in `results/backtest2-check-sample.json` (a random 10% of rows, seed 3, as BACKTEST-2 "Data handling" says). Each row was checked against the saved copy it was compiled from (no network), field by field: date, start, title, performers, canceled, drop_reason and presented_by. A flag from the first checker was re-read by a second one before it was kept.
- Result, in `checked_by` (`second-pass-2026-10-19:ok`, or `mismatch(<fields>)`): 925 ok, 6 mismatches. The rows are left as compiled, so the run uses the data the pass measured. The six: Bitterzoet 4 Aug 2026 (start is the doors time, 19:30; the page gives the main act at 20:30); Urban Spree 25 Apr 2026 (canceled: the page says the show is postponed); Beachland Ballroom 28 Jul 2026 (presented_by: the page names Grog Shop as presenter); Make-Out Room 9 Aug and 17 Sep 2026 (title and start: each row is a description paragraph of a show that has its own row, so it is an extra row); Tranzac Living Room 28 May 2026 (presented_by: three of the four names are partners, not presenters).
- No other row was touched by the pass; every other field reads back unchanged.

## Names withheld (19 Oct 2026)
- BACKTEST-2 records only performers' billing names. Eight more titles named private persons (memorials, birthdays, a retirement, a private booking); the name is now "[name withheld]" in each, as in the 529 rows. Where the source URL's path carried a private name (Bitterzoet 5 Apr, Rickshaw 4 Apr, Tranzac Main Hall 23 Sep, and Tranzac Main Hall 28 Sep, whose title "private Memorial" names no one), the path's last part is "[slug withheld]". No date, room or count changed. Two of the eight rows are in the second-pass sample and were checked before the name was withheld.

## Venues in `venues.csv`
### Bitterzoet (`ams-bitterzoet`, Amsterdam)
- Source: Own WordPress site. sitemap.xml lists 1,015 /event/<slug>/ pages, and each page prints the show date in its header line. The /agenda/ page lists only coming shows (about one week, about 9 rows; each row gives date, start time and price), so S1 was read from web.archive.org copies of it. The WP REST /wp-json/wp/v2/event and /event/feed carry only the post publish date, not the show date; REST was used to pick S2 pages, never to date them.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://www.bitterzoet.com/agenda/ for ts 20260418102932, 20260423171942, 20260517054900, 20260610213724 (S1); https://www.bitterzoet.com/event/<slug>/ (S2, and one S1 page); https://www.bitterzoet.com/wp-json/wp/v2/event (titles and publish dates only). bitterzoet.com redirects to www.bitterzoet.com.
- Dates checked: kevin-atwater: 'donderdag 3 december 2026', 'deuren open 19:30', 'Zaal open: 19:30, Hoofdprogramma: 20:30', published 2026-06-18, so the publish date is not the show date. rum-punch-5: 'zaterdag 25 juli 2026'. Wayback agenda 2026-04-18 row: 'zaterdag 25 april 2026 / 19:30 / €28.70 | The Barr Brothers'. Review re-reads: jealousy-4 'zaterdag 29 augustus 2026' (doors 23:30), jiva 'donderdag 3 september 2026' (doors 23:30), byond-normal 'donderdag 10 september 2026' (doors 23:59), songtravestival 'zondag 10 mei 2026' (doors 19:00). These are show dates. Starts in S2 run from 19:00 to 23:59, so the 00:01-04:59 rule (Amendment 3) never applies.
- robots.txt (16 Oct 2026): allowed. https://www.bitterzoet.com/robots.txt: 'User-agent: * / Disallow: /wp-admin/ / Allow: /wp-admin/admin-ajax.php / Sitemap: ...'; no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every URL used; web.archive.org was allowed. Every venue page answered 200 or 404, with no 403 and no bot check. web.archive.org later answered 429 (to a request made for another venue's check in the same run) and polite.py stopped that host; this is the archive's rate limit, not the venue's answer (Amendment 14 does not apply to it).
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): copyright-only. The footer on every page says only '© 2026 bitterzoet' (a bare notice, which Amendment 14 says is not terms). There is no terms, privacy or disclaimer link. The /info/ 'Huisregels' are door and conduct rules (age, weapons, drugs, cameras) with no clause on copying or reuse. Checked: https://www.bitterzoet.com/; https://www.bitterzoet.com/info/; https://www.bitterzoet.com/bitterzoet-huren/; https://www.bitterzoet.com/terms/ (404); https://www.bitterzoet.com/terms-and-conditions/ (404); https://www.bitterzoet.com/legal/ (404); https://www.bitterzoet.com/privacy-policy/ (404); https://www.bitterzoet.com/privacy/ (404); https://www.bitterzoet.com/algemene-voorwaarden/ (404); https://www.bitterzoet.com/sitemap.xml (no terms or privacy page listed)
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress with a custom theme (built by the design studios Stuurmen and PUHA); an Ecwid store plugin serves the shop only. The only 'cloudflare' string is cdnjs.cloudflare.com script loading, not a challenge. Not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Weebly or a Rockhouse/Etix site. Tickets through outside sellers (fourvenues, tickets.paradiso.nl, bitterzoet.weticket.io, weeztix and others).
- Operator (§1): a private owner (named in a 2020 AT5 article, https://www.at5.nl/artikelen/203033/zware-tijden-voor-bitterzoet-de-bedrijfsleider-is-nu-stratenmaker, and on the venue's Wikipedia page, https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bitterzoet, as sole owner since 2020, marked 'citation needed'). No legal entity or KvK record was found, so the current owner is otherwise not identified. The venue says it books no bands itself and rents itself out ('Bitterzoet boekt zelf geen bands maar verhuurt zich bijna dagelijks voor cdreleases, showcases en concerten', https://www.bitterzoet.com/info/), and lists its own programming and bookings contacts at @bitterzoet.com, so there is no outside house booker and the owner is the operator. Outside presenters: Paradiso (Stichting Paradiso, a foundation, not Live Nation or AEG) has the ticket link on 8 of the 32 S1 agenda rows (9 of the 41 distinct agenda rows seen, with the live agenda of 16 Oct) and on none of the 20 S2 pages (their own ticket links: 14 bitterzoet.weticket.io, 5 Bitterzoet's own account on fourvenues.com, 1 weeztix); club nights are run by several outside promoters (Jealousy, TR!BE, Do You Remember, Wasserette and others). No company presents more than half of the counted shows, so none is a house booker (Amendment 14). Fourvenues links appear on 27 of the 53 counted rows, but every one goes to Bitterzoet's own account there (www.fourvenues.com/bitterzoet/...), so it is a ticket channel, not a presenter. In the review's web searches, Mojo Concerts (Live Nation NL) appears only as presenter of one ADE 2024 showcase night, and Live Nation's site lists one show there; no public source names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake or takeover.
- Rooms: one. The agenda's only filters are type (alle / clubnacht / concert) and month, not a room, and event pages have no room or location field.
- Coverage: S1 >=33, S2 >=20, both lower bounds. S1: the 32 distinct rows of the four web.archive.org copies of /agenda/ (18 Apr to 17 Jun 2026) plus one own event page (songtravestival, 10 May). S2: 20 own event pages dated 9 Jul to 19 Sep 2026 (17 picked by REST publish date Jul-Aug, plus jealousy-4, jiva and byond-normal); vroege-vogels-25 (2 Oct) is outside S2. Amendment 4 words remove 0 rows; the two S1 'WK 2026: ...' football screening rows count because no listed word is in their titles (without them S1 is 31). No canceled, postponed or off-site row inside S1 or S2. Counting stopped at the venue host's request budget and when web.archive.org stopped answering, before the 2026-07-11 copy; the REST feed lists about 70 events published Jun-Sep and the venue programmes almost nightly. S2 sits exactly at 20, so the role rests on all 20 pages; the compile confirms it (Amendment 12). About half of S1 is concerts and half club nights (13 of the 32 agenda rows have doors at 23:30); the S2 sample is club nights (19 of 20 have doors 22:00-23:59, one is an early 19:00 Wasserette edition; the pages carry no type field).
- §5: other Amsterdam venues in other clusters were checked in the same run: Cinetol and OCCII came out eligible, Tolhuistuin pool-only; Melkweg and Paradiso are not used. §5 holds if Cinetol or OCCII is listed. Under the literal reading of Amendment 1, Paradiso is an operator of Bitterzoet, and Tolhuistuin (where Paradiso organises the Club concerts) and Cinetol (Paradiso on 8 counted rows, 'i.s.m. paradiso') would share that cluster; OCCII would still keep §5.
- Capacity: none published by the venue. Paradiso's location page (https://www.paradiso.nl/en/info/hoe-kom-ik-bij-de-verschillende-locaties-van-paradiso; not fetched, its robots.txt disallows ClaudeBot) gives 350 in a search snippet, but Paradiso is not the operator, so the room is "size unknown" (Amendment 8).
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.bitterzoet.com 40 (4 of them the review's re-reads of four event pages), bitterzoet.com 4, web.archive.org 6 naming bitterzoet.com (see requests.log)

### Cinetol (`ams-cinetol-*`, Amsterdam)
- Source: HTML of the venue's own past-events archive (/archief, a Webflow CMS list, 40 rows per page) with weekday, day.month and year per row, plus the own event pages /events/<slug>, which give the full date, a LOCATION field (room), doors and show times. The archive keeps all past shows; pages 1-5 reach from 16 Oct 2026 back to 17 Feb 2026. Ticket links (cinetol.stager.co, WeArePublic, Subbacultcha, TicketSwap) were not used. web.archive.org copies were not used.
- Read: https://www.cinetol.nl/archief?5bc40fc8_page=N for N = 1..5 (read by the first check and again by the second check) -> https://www.cinetol.nl/events/<slug> (17 event pages in all); also /, /programma, /over-cinetol, /faq, /verhuur, /huisregels, /privacyverklaring
- Dates checked: Event pages match the archive rows and give show dates and show times. /events/afar: '30 September 2026 LOCATION: Zaal doors: 20:00 show: 20:30' (archive Wed 30.09 2026). /events/mckay: '23 July 2026 ... doors: 19:30 show: 20:00' (archive Thu 23.07). /events/zalf: '30 June 2026 LOCATION: Zaal doors: 20:00 show: 20:30' (archive Tue 30.06).
- robots.txt (16 Oct 2026): allowed. https://www.cinetol.nl/robots.txt answers 200 with an empty body (allow all); no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed / and every page read. cinetol.nl redirects to www.cinetol.nl.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): none-found. No terms of use. The footer links only 'huisregels' (house rules for visitors) and 'privacyverklaring' (no use-licence section). The only conditions named are KHN's 'Uniforme Voorwaarden Horeca', for restaurant reservations. No ©, copyright, reuse or personal-use text on the home, archive, FAQ or rental pages. No VNPF visitor terms (the reason Melkweg is not used) are linked or mentioned. Checked: https://www.cinetol.nl/ (footer); https://www.cinetol.nl/huisregels; https://www.cinetol.nl/privacyverklaring; https://www.cinetol.nl/faq; https://www.cinetol.nl/archief; https://www.cinetol.nl/terms (404); https://www.cinetol.nl/algemene-voorwaarden (404)
- Platform: Webflow (meta generator 'Webflow', cdn.prod.website-files.com), which passes as read for Horseshoe Tavern. No Rockhouse Partners/Etix, Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma or Music Glue markers. Every page answered 200 except three 404s (/sitemap.xml, /terms, /algemene-voorwaarden); no bot check and no 403. Tickets go through cinetol.stager.co and outside sellers.
- Operator: Stichting Kunst- en Cultuurhuis Cinetol, a foundation (site footer; /privacyverklaring names Stichting Cinetol as controller). Booking requests go only to info@cinetol.nl and are passed to the house programmers (/faq); /over-cinetol names a board, directors and an in-house programmer (private persons, named on that page). No public source names a Live Nation or AEG owner or booker. Named co-presenters ('i.s.m.', 'presents') are on a minority of the 139 counted S1+S2 rows: Paradiso 8 (4 / 4), Thunderboom, De Appel, Afrogrooves and Schorem 2 each, every other one 1. So the house books the rooms and no outside company joins a cluster (Amendment 14). Under the literal reading, a company that `presented_by` records for a counted row is an operator of that row's room; Paradiso is also an operator of Bitterzoet and the house booker of Tolhuistuin's house room, so the Cinetol room with such a row (Lamb, 1 Sep, is in the Zaal) would join that cluster. 'i.s.m.' means 'in cooperation with', not 'presents'.
- §5: Amsterdam has eligible venues in other clusters (OCCII, Bitterzoet; Tolhuistuin pool-only). Melkweg and Paradiso are not used.
- Rooms: the LOCATION field of each event page, a CMS reference list separate from the title. Values seen: 'Zaal'; 'Etage' alone (felix-ross, 19 Jun); 'Zaal' + 'Etage' (26 Sep) and 'Zaal' + 'Tempel' (27 Jun), which go to the Zaal as the first listed; 'Volkshotel' (another building, `off-site`). 'Tempel' is the neighbouring building (a former theosophical temple, per /over-cinetol), so a row that lists it first would be `off-site`. The archive listing does not show the field and its filters (month, genre, year) cannot place a row, so each row's room comes from its event page; rows with no room go to the house room. Capacity: Zaal 150 (standing; 70 or 80 seated), Etage 60 ('tot 60 pers.'; 50 standing), the figures https://www.cinetol.nl/verhuur publishes.
- Coverage: S1 81, S2 58 for the building (every row of /archief pages 1-5 at its listed local date; recounted by the second check with the same result). 3 S1 'workshop' rows removed (Amendment 4), none in S2; 1 S1 row dropped as off-site ('Live Resort @ VOLKSHOTEL', 3 May); no row marked canceled or postponed. Not exact: the off-site check opened only 17 event pages. By room, lower bounds from those 17 pages: Zaal 6 / 9, Etage 1 / 0, no room 0 / 0. The word rule reads the title only: 'Wintertijd' (30 May) has 'film screening ... + queer karaoke' in its subtitle line and counts (S1 would be 80 if the subtitle were read). The Zaal's own count is at most 81 / 58, so it was pool before compiling.
- Role corrected (17 Oct 2026, Amendment 12): the compiled `shows.csv` (archive pages 1-5 and every event page they list) gives Zaal 74 / 57, Etage 2 / 0 and the house room 3 / 0, so the Zaal is a test room. Three more event pages give LOCATION 'Tempel' alone (23 Jun, 25 Jun, 28 Aug) and are `off-site`. One row gives LOCATION 'Tolbar' (16 Apr), which the venue's /tolbar page names as Cinetol's own eetcafe, so it goes to the house room.
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.cinetol.nl 42, cinetol.nl 3, web.archive.org 3 (scouting, 15 Oct: two CDX queries and one copy of the homepage, not used for counting)

### OCCII (`ams-occii`, Amsterdam)
- Source: Own HTML year archive of the venue's self-hosted WordPress site (Events Manager plugin): one page with 138 rows for 2026, each with weekday and date, doors time, title, a genre line and a link to /events/<slug>/. A Yoast event sitemap is the backup route. Event pages give the full date with year and the doors and show times.
- Read: https://occii.org/events-archive-2026/ (one page, read on 15 Oct 2026); event pages https://occii.org/events/<slug>/ for date and location checks (8 pages)
- Dates checked: The archive's weekday and date agree with 2026 on all 138 rows. /events/wdzkerk-heydarian/ 'Thursday, April 02, 2026 ... Doors open: 20:00 Showtime: 20.30'; /events/kb_-kuntari/ 'Friday, June 12, 2026 ... Doors open: 20:00 Showtime: 20.30'; /events/ragdawl-presents-toendra-helen-jewett-crashing/ 'Thursday, September 10, 2026 ... Doors open: 20:00 Showtime: 20.30'. These are show dates, read at the Europe/Amsterdam local date.
- robots.txt (16 Oct 2026): allowed. https://occii.org/robots.txt: User-agent * disallows only the WooCommerce log and upload paths, /*?add-to-cart= and /wp-admin/; the Yoast block has an empty Disallow. No Anthropic-agent group. polite.py allowed every page read.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): silent. The site's general terms (Stichting In It, Chamber of Commerce no. 41211866) are sales terms only: 'These terms and conditions apply to all quotations, offers, activities, orders, agreements and deliveries of services or products by or on behalf of OCCII.' The English and Dutch texts cover prices, delivery, retention of title and force majeure, with no clause on intellectual property, copying, reproducing, crawling or personal use. The footer has only a bare notice, '© CopyWhatever' (not terms, Amendment 14). The house rules and the Code of Conduct cover behaviour in the building. No VNPF visitor terms are referenced. Checked: https://occii.org/terms-conditions/; https://occii.org/terms/ (same page); https://occii.org/terms-of-use/ (404); https://occii.org/legal/ (404); https://occii.org/privacy/; https://occii.org/policy/; https://occii.org/code-of-conduct/; https://occii.org/faq/; https://occii.org/ (footer)
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress (Beaver Builder theme, Events Manager Pro, WooCommerce for its own ticket sales through PayPal/iDeal, Yoast, Site Kit). No Fatsoma, Squarespace, Wix, Weebly, Music Glue or Rockhouse Partners markers; no bot check (the saved bodies carry no challenge or captcha markers).
- Operator: Stichting In It, a non-profit foundation with ANBI status, runs OCCII in the De Binnenpret complex and programmes it itself (the /info page says 'OCCII has a staff of two who coordinate the program'; the /diy page sends bookings to its programme coordinator and says 'we don't need booking agencies, big labels, or promoters'). The building owner is named by no public source and is recorded as not identified. No Live Nation or AEG stake, booking or ticketing link was found. Local collectives' series (12FU, Pretty//Ugly, Geregeld Ontregeld, KB_ and others) each present only a few nights.
- Rooms: one. OCCII is a single hall; the archive and event pages have no room field or filter (the line under an event title names a series or presenter, not a room). Kinderpret, the Tuesday and Wednesday afternoon children's theatre series that OCCII also hosts (/info), is not listed in this archive, so it adds no rows. Capacity 225: the largest figure the venue publishes ('We have an capacity of 225 people', https://occii.org/faq/; the /diy page says 200).
- Coverage: S1 43, S2 26 (exact, every 2026 row of the own archive). Off-site rows dropped: 3 in S1 (OCCII ON TOUR at Willem de Zwijgerkerk on 2 Apr and OCCII POËZIE there on 10 May; Kerkje van Oostum, Groningen, on 4 Apr) and 1 in S2 (23 Aug, De Kromme Haring Brewery, Utrecht, also matched by 'market'). No canceled or untitled rows; the 10 Sep '[cancel: toendra]' row counts, since only one support act withdrew. KaraOCCII and CiNEMOCCii rows carry no Amendment 4 word in the title and count under the literal rule. Read by meaning, S2 would fall to 19 (18 without KRONKEL FESTIVAL, a multi-venue festival that lists OCCII among its locations), because several counted S2 rows are karaoke, film, an art expo, drinks, a summer-break placeholder or a duplicated festival header. S1 stays well above 20 read by meaning: 40 without the two figure-drawing nights and the community drinks, 37 if the line-dancing night, the drag night and the cabaret club night also go. `shows.csv` confirms the role (Amendment 12).
- Requests during the eligibility check: occii.org 34 (12 on 15 Oct, 22 on 16 Oct; requests.log), web.archive.org 0

### Tolhuistuin (`ams-tolhuistuin-*`, Amsterdam)
- Source: The venue's own HTML agenda (/agenda) on its self-hosted Craft CMS site. The page carries an inline JSON 'all-items' array with title, eventStartDate, eventType, a separate 'location' field (the room) and a paradisoEvent flag. The live page lists only coming rows and past event pages mostly return 404 (the sitemap.xml is an empty sitemapindex), so S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of that same own page.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://tolhuistuin.nl/agenda for ts = 20260325011843, 20260402165353, 20260415105802, 20260513194409, 20260712113128; live https://tolhuistuin.nl/agenda (15 Oct 2026; coming rows only, none of them counted). The copy 20260617202058 got a 429 from web.archive.org (the archive's rate limit, not the venue), and the fetcher stopped that host, so there is no copy between 13 May and 12 Jul and none after 12 Jul.
- Dates checked: Listing date = date on the venue's event page: Shakey Graves, listing 2026-08-28, page 'vrijdag 28 aug, 26 ... Tijd 19:30' (https://tolhuistuin.nl/evenementen/shakey-graves); Jaguar Sun, listing 2026-09-08, page 'dinsdag 08 sep, 26 ... Tijd 20:15' (https://tolhuistuin.nl/evenementen/jaguar-sun). Each copy's first row falls on or after the copy's date (the 15 Apr copy starts 2026-04-15), as upcoming show dates do. These are show dates. No start falls between 00:01 and 04:59.
- robots.txt (15 Oct 2026): allowed. https://tolhuistuin.nl/robots.txt returns 404, so every path is allowed; polite.py allowed every URL read on 15 and 16 Oct. web.archive.org allowed. The agenda page holds a Cloudflare Turnstile script tag (for forms), but every page answered 200 with full content and no challenge, so there is no bot check.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): none-found. There are no website terms of use. The 'Algemene voorwaarden' page holds only visitor house rules ('Huis- en Tuinregels'); its only related line bans filming on site for professional use without permission. The privacy page, cookie page, FAQ, press page, contact page and the footer hold no ban on copying, reproducing or republishing, no personal-viewing licence and no clause on automated reading. Checked: https://tolhuistuin.nl/ (footer); https://tolhuistuin.nl/algemene-voorwaarden; https://tolhuistuin.nl/privacy-beleid; https://tolhuistuin.nl/cookies; https://tolhuistuin.nl/faq; https://tolhuistuin.nl/pers; https://tolhuistuin.nl/contact; https://tolhuistuin.nl/terms (404)
- Platform: Self-hosted Craft CMS (not Fatsoma, Squarespace, Wix, Music Glue, Weebly or Rockhouse Partners). Ticket links in the counted rows: tickets.paradiso.nl (116, all on Paradiso rows), the choir's own site (37), tickets.parallel.am (18, Club nights), Paylogic (17) and small sellers; no Ticketmaster or Live Nation links.
- Operator: Tolhuistuin is run jointly by Stichting Tolhuistuin (a non-profit ANBI foundation), THT Horeca BV and Stichting Paradiso (https://tolhuistuin.nl/over-ons). The building is a former Shell research centre (over-ons); a search snippet of the venue's history page (https://tolhuistuin.nl/en/geschiedenis-van-tolhuistuin, not fetched) says Shell sold the site to the Gemeente Amsterdam in 2009; the current owner is not identified. House bookers by room (Amendment 14, counted S1 + S2 rows): Paradiso presents 117 of the 130 rows with no room (paradisoEvent true, CMS organiser id 5377, 'Met huisgenoot Paradiso' on its event pages), so it is the house booker of the house room; it presents 117 of the 262 counted rows of the venue (45%). Parallel, whose partner text in the agenda source reads 'Parallel presenteert dynamische clubnachten, concerten, exposities, kunstinstallaties en spoken word', is the CMS organiser of 19 of the 21 counted Club rows and is named in the description ('AT PARALLEL') of 19, 20 of 21 in all, so it is the house booker of the Club. The FAQ also names Paradiso as organiser of the concerts in the Club ('Paradiso organiseert de concerten in de Club', https://tolhuistuin.nl/faq), so Paradiso is recorded as a named booker of the Club too. Stichting Wijsneus is the CMS organiser of the one series in the Jagerstudio (5 of 5 counted rows). In the other rooms no organiser presents more than half (37 of 44 IJzaal rows are one recurring choir series with no organiser named), so their operator is Stichting Tolhuistuin. No saved page names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake, booking deal or presenter for Stichting Tolhuistuin, Stichting Paradiso or Parallel, and no saved page names Mojo Concerts. All rooms are one building and one cluster (`ams-tolhuistuin`); none of these companies books or owns a room in `venues.csv`. The compiled rows (with the CMS organiser field read as the presenter) give each room's majority organiser, and `venues.csv` names it as the operator of the room it house-books (Amendment 14): Parallel for the Club (0.957 of its counted rows), Paradiso for the house room (0.902) and Podcastnetwerk for the Tuinhuis (0.588; on the counts above no organiser had more than half of the Tuinhuis rows). The Jagerstudio row names Stichting Tolhuistuin with Stichting Wijsneus as its house booker, and the other rooms keep Stichting Tolhuistuin. The cluster stays `ams-tolhuistuin`.
- Rooms: the 'location' field of each agenda row (IJzaal, Club, Tuin, Tuinhuis, Zonzij, Jagerstudio, Galerij). Rows with no location, which are Paradiso's concerts (paradisoEvent true, location empty) and a few others, go to the house room `ams-tolhuistuin-house`. The paradisoEvent flag names no room, so those rows are not placed in the Club. Club rows say 'at Parallel' in their description, but the location field puts them in the Club and the saved pages give Parallel no other address, so they are not off-site; if compiling shows Parallel at another building, they are dropped as `off-site`. One row has no location in the 25 Mar copy and 'Tuin' in the two later copies; it goes to Tuin. Capacity: the venue publishes 'de grootste zaal tot 550 gasten staand' (https://tolhuistuin.nl/verhuur) without naming the room, so no room has a figure (Amendment 8: size unknown).
- Coverage (recounted 17 Oct from the five saved copies, keyed by the row's CMS uid and local date): S1 >=171, S2 >=91 for the venue. Keying by title and date, as first done, counts 5 rows twice whose title was changed between copies (174 / 93). 1 off-site S1 row (location 'Externe locatie') and 4 S2 'workshop' rows (Amendment 4) removed; no title marked canceled or moved. By room: house 85 / 45 (Paradiso 74 / 43), IJzaal 33 / 11, Club 16 / 5, Tuin 12 / 18, Tuinhuis 14 / 3, Zonzij 6 / 7, Jagerstudio 5 / 0, Galerij 0 / 2. No named room reaches 20 / 20. The house room does, but it holds the rows whose room is not given, so it is a pool room by Amendment 14; every room is pool and the venue is listed as pool. Rows added after 13 May (for S1) and after 12 Jul (for S2) cannot be seen, so these are lower bounds (Tuin needs 8 more S1 rows, IJzaal 9 more S2 rows, to become a test room on `shows.csv`). 4 S1 rows (2 house, 2 Tuinhuis) were listed in one copy and missing from a later copy dated before the show; without them the venue has 167 / 91 (house 83 / 45, Tuinhuis 12 / 3). Counted rows include non-music event types (workshops, recurring rows, children's activities); rows typed music alone give 91 / 54.
- Requests during the eligibility check: tolhuistuin.nl 19, web.archive.org 8

### Center Stage (`atl-centerstage-*`, Atlanta)
- Source: The venue's own self-hosted WordPress site: a JSON feed /wp-json/centerstage/v2/events/?venue=<room>&page=N with event_date, show_time, show_status, promoter, a venue_room field and an external_venue field, and one /events/<slug>/ page per show. The live site keeps only coming shows (the feed starts at 16 Oct 2026, past event pages answer 404, and the live events-sitemap.xml holds 125 entries, 55 of the 325 archived event slugs), and the WP REST types list no events post type, so S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of those same own pages: single event pages, one feed copy and one home page copy.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/events/<slug>/ (120 copies, ts 20260301 to 20261011, all status 200; 104 distinct pages, 16 of the copies are ?doing_wp_cron variants of the same pages), picked from the CDX query https://web.archive.org/cdx/search/cdx?url=centerstage-atlanta.com/events/ (prefix, collapse=urlkey, from 20260301 to 20261015; 367 rows; the same query without prefix gave 0 rows); https://web.archive.org/web/20260928201145id_/https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/wp-json/centerstage/v2/events/?venue=all (the one feed copy found by a CDX query for that exact URL, from 20260901 to 20261015); https://web.archive.org/web/20260515093037id_/https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/ (home page copy, read in scouting); the scouting domain CDX of 15 Oct (from 20260301 to 20261015). Live pages read on 16 Oct 2026 for the check: the home page, ?venue=all, /wp-json/wp/v2/types, the sitemaps, /about-the-venues/, /frequently-asked-questions/, /venue-rental/, /contact/, /private-events-booking/, /directions-parking/ and one live event page.
- Dates checked: Live feed event_date 20261023 with show_time 8:00 pm for Nick Shoulders = live home page 'Center Stage Oct 23 Nick Shoulders ... Doors 7:00 pm Show 8:00 pm'. Archived home 2026-05-15: 'Vinyl May 30 Nice Dream ... Show 8:00 pm'. The archived S1/S2 event pages use the live template: The Magic of Motown, 'Friday, June 05th, 2026', 'Doors: 7:00 pm Show: 8:00 pm' (https://web.archive.org/web/20260312020556id_/https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/events/10218/); Congress The Band, 'Friday, September 18th, 2026', 'Doors: 11:00 pm Show: 11:59 pm', so 18 Sep. Dates carry doors and show times, so they are show dates. No start in S1 or S2 falls between 00:01 and 04:59 (Amendment 3); the only 'am' time is a placeholder page dated 1970-01-01, which gives no row.
- robots.txt (15-16 Oct 2026): allowed. https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/robots.txt (Yoast): 'Crawl-delay: 10' on the first line, before any group, then 'User-agent: * / Disallow:' (empty) and a Sitemap line; no Anthropic group. A Crawl-delay outside any group belongs to no user agent, so the fetcher kept its 2.5 s gap. polite.py allowed every URL read; web.archive.org was allowed. Every venue-host request answered 200 or 404, never 403, and no saved body carries a bot check.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): none-found. No terms or legal link in the header or footer and no copyright notice. The page sitemap lists only the home page, private-events-booking, contact, venue-rental, about-the-venues, FAQ and directions-parking, and all of them were read. The check's review scanned the saved live bodies, and the settle's review all 122 archived copies; neither found terms, copyright, 'All rights reserved', copy, reproduce or personal-use text. Checked: https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/; https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/page-sitemap.xml; https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/frequently-asked-questions/; https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/contact/; https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/about-the-venues/; https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/venue-rental/; https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/private-events-booking/; https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/directions-parking/; https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/terms/ (404); https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/terms-of-use/ (404); https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/terms-of-service/ (404); https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/terms-and-conditions/ (404); https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/legal/ (404); https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/privacy-policy/ (404)
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress (theme centerstage2020, plugin event-fetcher, custom REST namespace centerstage/v2). No Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Webflow, Rockhouse Partners/Etix or Cloudflare markers in the live bodies (check and its review), and no Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Etix or Rockhouse text in any of the 122 copies (the three 'rhp' string hits are Facebook share ids); no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer. Tickets through Ticketmaster (AXS on one off-site page): ticket vendors, not owners or bookers.
- Operator (§1, Amendment 14): Owner: a private family owns the building (named on https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/about-the-venues/: 'The family preserves the Midtown Atlanta property as a home to performing arts'); no owning company is identified. House booker: Rival Entertainment, named by the venue ('Center Stage is operated by independent Atlanta promoter Rival Entertainment. Since 2004, Rival has produced concerts and festivals', same page; box office, rentals and feedback go to rivalentertainment.com addresses, /contact/), and it presents more than half of each room's counted rows: Center Stage Theater 13 of 25, The Loft 8 of 15, Vinyl 23 of 30 (44 of the 70 counted rows), so it is the house booker of all three rooms (Amendment 14). The site lists the venue and Rival as members of NIVA, indiepromoter.org and liveindependent.com; a Wikipedia search snippet says Rival, an independent Atlanta promotion company formed in 2004, has run the venue since 2009. Web searches in the check and its review found no Live Nation or AEG owner, stake, house-booking or talent-buying deal; the Live Nation-linked 'Rival' in the press is a ticketing startup that Ticketmaster bought in 2020, another company. Outside presenters on counted rows ('presented by' line of the page, promoter field of the feed): Outback Presents 5 of 25 Center Stage Theater rows (all with Rival); Bowe Inc with Reaction Presents ('Bowe Inc.' or 'Reaction' on one row each) 1 of 25 Center Stage Theater, 2 of 15 The Loft and 3 of 30 Vinyl rows; The Masquerade 1 Center Stage Theater and 1 The Loft row; Zero Mile 1 Vinyl row (with Rival); one row each for Jetlife with Clockwork Music, Icon Concerts, LYFANDFRIENDS, CoreATL, Furtado Global with Speakeasy, Shaky Knees and 'Promoted By Venue' (Center Stage Theater), Beats + Ballads (The Loft) and Starringo (Vinyl). None presents more than half of a room's rows, so none joins a cluster or decides §1. 9 counted rows name no presenter; one of them names only two private persons as presenter, which is not written. Shaky Knees (1 S2 row, 18 Sep) is a festival brand whose ownership is to be checked against `ln-aeg.csv` for the literal reading.
- Rooms: the room element of each event page (div.csa-venue-column with class has-center-stage-background-color, has-the-loft-background-color or has-vinyl-background-color, and a logo with alt 'Center Stage', 'The Loft' or 'Vinyl'), the h4.event-venue of each home carousel slide and the feed's venue_room field (center_stage, the_loft, vinyl), all separate from the title (Amendment 14, rooms); the site's room filter (All / Center Stage / The Loft / Vinyl / Other Venues) calls the same field. 'Center Stage' is the source's label for the Center Stage Theater room. Rows with no room go to `atl-centerstage-house`; the only such rows are 'Other Venues' rows (black-squares logo or external_venue), which are off-site. One 30 Sep row is Vinyl in the 12 Jul and 5 Aug page copies and The Loft in the 28 Sep feed copy; the latest copy wins, so it is The Loft. The about page names the main room 'Center Stage Theater', so the room for rows with no room carries the building name. Capacity: Center Stage Theater 1,050, The Loft 650, Vinyl 300, the figures https://www.centerstage-atlanta.com/about-the-venues/ publishes ('Center Stage Theater, the historic 1050-capacity original theater', 'The Loft, an industrial 650-capacity live music and event space', 'Vinyl, a 300-capacity staple'; re-read in the saved 16 Oct copy).
- Coverage: settled on 17 Oct 2026 from copies fetched that day. The 16-17 Oct check had left Center Stage undecided, because polite.py had stopped web.archive.org after a second 429. Lower bounds, not exact, on the settle `shows.csv` (73 rows, 70 counted): Center Stage Theater S1 >=15, S2 >=10; The Loft >=12 / >=3; Vinyl >=15 / >=15; house 0 / 0; building S1 >=42, S2 >=28. An independent review recounted with its own parser and got the same 73 rows, rooms, canceled and off-site flags, and found title, date and room of 8 random rows in the cited bodies. Removed: 1 canceled (The Loft, 23 Aug; the ticket button of the 21 Aug copy reads 'Cancelled'), 2 off-site (6 May, an 'Other Venues' page with no room and an AXS ticket link; 21 Jul, a Cobb Energy Centre slide on the home carousel), 0 untitled, 0 by the Amendment 4 words; comedy and talks without one of the words in the title count. No room meets 20 / 20 by itself, so every room is pool; the rooms together meet coverage, so the venue is eligible and listed as pool (Amendment 12; reading 8 of `candidates.md`). Vinyl needs 5 more rows in each period to become a test room. 67 counted rows come from single event-page copies and the 5-show 'Featured Events' carousel of the 2026-05-15 home copy (its 'Upcoming Shows' list is loaded by script and is empty in the copy), and 3 from the 2026-09-28 feed copy (20 rows from 28 Sep to 11 Oct), the only copy that covers its dates in full (28-30 Sep). No full listing copy covers 1 Apr to 27 Sep. The prefetch picked event pages alphabetically, up to the f's: of the 325 event slugs in the CDX files, 104 were read and 221 were not (the review's count; the settle report says 201). Each unread page can hold one show in any room, and shows with no archived page cannot be seen. The feed CDX query asked only for the exact ?venue=all URL and only from 2026-09-01 (requests.log; the manifest does not record its range), so ?venue=<room> and page=N copies were not looked for, and the domain CDX lists no wp-json copy; earlier feed copies were neither found nor ruled out; the domain CDX also lists /category/<genre>/ copies (Mar-Jul) whose content is unknown. The review's corrections: the events CDX is collapsed by urlkey, so most pages were read at their first capture, and 54 of the 67 counted rows not from the feed copy (S1 32, S2 22) have later captures dated before the show that were not read, so a later cancellation or date move would be missed and the room counts are not strictly lower bounds against cancellations; the building's S2 falls below 20 only if 9 of those 22 S2 rows fail, against 3 cancel marks seen in 120 page copies and 1 in 20 feed rows. 'Concrete Boys' (Center Stage Theater, same tour line) on 23 Apr and 26 May under two slugs may be one moved show (then Center Stage Theater S1 14, building S1 41), and the 6 May off-site row rests on the 'Other Venues' logo, the empty room class and the AXS link (in the house room it would make the building S1 43). None of these changes the verdict. One page copy (20260519091600) carries the placeholder date 1970-01-01 and describes an off-site festival in October; it gives no row.
- Coverage, compiled (18 Oct 2026, Amendment 12): the compiled `shows.csv` (73 rows, the settle's rows: 65 from event-page copies, 5 from the 2026-05-15 home copy and 3 from the 2026-09-28 feed copy; read from saved bodies, no request) gives Center Stage Theater 15 / 10, The Loft 12 / 3, Vinyl 15 / 15 and the house room 0 / 0 (its 2 rows, 6 May and 21 Jul, are `off-site`); building 42 / 28. Removed: 1 canceled (The Loft, S2), 2 off-site, 0 untitled, 0 by the Amendment 4 words. Every room stays pool, as `venues.csv` has it, and the dry run on the merge draft (`results/backtest2-dryrun.json`) finds no coverage problem here. One correction to the settle: the saved non-HTML domain CDX (69edb2e116f4, 380 rows) lists only the 20260928201145 feed capture, so earlier feed copies are ruled out, not unknown. Still not read (report.json, gaps): 221 event pages, 57 later captures of period pages that were read and 23 /category/ captures; no full listing copy covers 1 Apr to 27 Sep (180 nights), so the counts stay lower bounds. The 23 Apr and 26 May 'Concrete Boys' rows are written as the source gives them (possibly one moved show).
- Presenters, compiled (18 Oct 2026): Rival Entertainment presents Center Stage Theater 13 of 25 counted rows, The Loft 8 of 15 and Vinyl 23 of 30; the dry run gives these as house-booker shares 0.52, 0.533 and 0.767, each matching the operator in `venues.csv` (Amendment 14). Other names on counted rows: Center Stage Theater, Outback Presents 5 (all with Rival) and 1 each for Bowe Inc, Reaction Presents, The Masquerade, Jetlife, Clockwork Music, Icon Concerts, LYFANDFRIENDS, CoreATL, Furtado Global, Speakeasy, Shaky Knees, 'Promoted By Venue' and Dalton Carpet One (17 Jul, from the title '1FW SUMMERSTAGE 2 Presented by Dalton Carpet One', beside 'Promoted By Venue'; the settle had only the latter); The Loft, Bowe Inc 2, Reaction Presents 2, The Masquerade 1 and Beats + Ballads 1; Vinyl, Bowe Inc 2, Bowe Inc. 1, Reaction Presents 2, Reaction 1, Zero Mile 1 (with Rival) and Starringo 1. Names are kept as written, so 'Bowe Inc' / 'Bowe Inc.' and 'Reaction' / 'Reaction Presents' stay apart. 61 of the 70 counted rows name a presenter; of the 9 that do not, 1 names only private persons (not written). LN/AEG by the presenter check (`ln-aeg.csv`): Shaky Knees, live-nation (Shaky Knees Fest, LLC is on Live Nation's FY2025 Exhibit 21.1), on 1 counted row (Center Stage Theater, 18 Sep); Zero Mile, aeg, on 1 counted row (Vinyl, 15 Jun) and on the 6 May `off-site` row, which does not count. Outback Presents is not Live Nation or AEG by the check: AEG's reported stake in it is a minority one, and the check notes that the verdict should be reviewed if the aeg group is to count minority stakes. Icon Concerts (LiveCo, backed by Waterland Private Equity) and the other identified names are not Live Nation or AEG; CoreATL, LYFANDFRIENDS, Speakeasy, Reaction, Reaction Presents and Starringo were not identified. Under the literal reading of Amendment 1 the dry run drops all four Center Stage rooms on §1.
- §5: Atlanta has 529 (`atl-529`, booked in house) and The EARL (`atl-earl`, house booker Triple D's, see that section) in other clusters. Rival Entertainment books no other room in `venues.csv`, so its cluster (`rival-entertainment`, named after the house booker as `rising-sun-presents` and `modo-live` are) holds Center Stage alone. Under the literal reading of Amendment 1 the presenters above have not been compared with those of 529 and The EARL (529's feed has no promoter field; The EARL's compiled rows name Triple D's on 91 of 102). §6 and Amendment 11: Atlanta is not Minneapolis-St Paul, and Center Stage is not Hideout or Black Cat.
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.centerstage-atlanta.com 25 (16 Oct UTC: 2 robots.txt reads, 20 requests for the check, 2 of them script files and 8 answered 404, and 3 pages read by its review), centerstage-atlanta.com 1 (robots.txt, 15 Oct), web.archive.org 3 (scouting, 15 Oct: two domain CDX queries and the 2026-05-15 home copy). For settling, web.archive.org 124 on 17 Oct 2026 (18:25 to 19:26 UTC: 3 CDX queries, 1 feed copy and 120 event-page copies, all answered 200); the settle and its review made no request (requests.log).

Juni 2026', 2026-06-12T19:45, as in the 2026-05-11 copy. Cadet Carter: 'Fr. 18. September 2026', 2026-09-18T20:00, as in the 2026-07-06 copy (the page now marks it ABGESAGT). All 24 sampled S2 event pages give the same date as their copy's row. No start falls between 00:01 and 04:59.

No terms of use, and no clause on copying, reproduction, Urheberrecht, use licence, scraping or automated access on any page read; a second check of the Kontakt/Impressum, Datenschutz, Jugendschutz and Galerie pages, one event page and the live homepage found none either. The only © marks are photo credits on single events ('Foto: © ...'), bare notices that Amendment 14 says are not terms. Not Squarespace, Wix, Webflow, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Weebly or Rockhouse Partners. The Datenschutz page says Cloudflare may show a captcha to traffic it rates as dangerous; no challenge and no 403 was met on any request (all 200, apart from the 404 of the REST endpoint). Tickets go through many outside sellers per promoter (Eventim, Ticketmaster, DICE, Eventbrite, Reservix, TixforGigs, promoters' shops); no ticket site is read.

- Coverage: S1 83, S2 about 44 (union of five web.archive.org copies of the own homepage, which together list every day of 1 Apr to 30 Sep without a gap; the live copy starts at 16 Oct and adds no S1/S2 row; rows deduplicated by WordPress post id, latest copy before the show date). S1: 88 rows listed; 4 carry a canceled or rescheduled notice in the copies (one of them, 12 Apr, was moved to 23 Nov at another building, Mikropol), and 1 more is an older second post of the show rescheduled from 2 Jun, so 83. A 13 Apr show whose text says it was moved to that date is counted. S2: 48 listed minus 1 canceled in the copies = 47; 3 of 24 sampled live event pages are now marked ABGESAGT, so about 44; S2 >= 21 verified on the venue's own event pages (6 of them DJ party nights, which Amendment 4 does not remove). The other 23 S2 rows and all S1 rows were not rechecked on live pages, and no copy after 6 Jul exists, so later cancellations and S2 rows added later are not seen. 0 rows removed by Amendment 4 words. Re-count of the saved copies on 17 Oct gives the same 83 / 47 / 44.
- Operator (§1): Milchmädchen Musikkultur GmbH (HRB 143155 B, Berlin), named as 'Betreiber' on the Kontakt page; its managing directors are private persons. Booking is in house: 'Info & Booking' on the Kontakt page uses the venue's own address and phone. Web searches found no Live Nation or AEG owner, stake or booker. Ticket links are per promoter (Eventim, Ticketmaster, DICE, Eventbrite, Reservix, TixforGigs, promoters' shops), with no exclusive Live Nation or AEG ticketing. Semmel Concerts Entertainment GmbH (CTS Eventim group, not Live Nation or AEG) presents 2 counted S1 shows.
- Presenters: the "Örtlicher Veranstalter" line names a company on 77 of the 127 counted rows (36 different names); the largest, Trinity Music GmbH, has 21 (about 17%), Loft Concerts 7 and the operator itself 2. No company has more than half, so booking is the house's (Kontakt page: "Info & Booking" at the venue's own address). Some rows also carry "präsentiert von" lines naming media partners (13 counted rows).

The following General Terms and Conditions of Business shall be valid for the entire business relationship between Urban Spree GmbH ("Shop") and the purchasing person ("Orderer")'. Concert Room, Biergarten and Back Garden appear only in description text. Capacity: no figure published by the venue (third-party figures only), so none is recorded.

Press (the-berliner.com) says it has been run by its founders since 2012; web searches for Live Nation, AEG, takeover or ownership news found no Live Nation or AEG owner, stake or house booker. No house booker is named; a search snippet credits an independent collective with curating, which is unverified. Presenters: on the 16 detail pages read (a systematic sample of the 63 counted rows, every fifth row plus 3), the Promoter field names 13 companies: Greyzone Concerts 3, Loft Concerts 2 (one of them Berlin Breakout 2026), Urban Spree itself 2 (one with Libertine Records), every other one 1. No single company is shown to present more than half, so none is a house booker (Amendment 14). The Promoter field gives `presented_by` for the literal reading, where Loft Concerts (also 7 counted Privatclub rows) joins Urban Spree and Privatclub (`candidates.md`).

No Amendment 4 word in any title or, for the 25 titles cut off with an ellipsis, in the URL slug (0 removed in S1, 0 in S2). The Events filter (art openings, a guided tour, a book release, a few parties: 5 S1, 2 S2) was not counted; with it the counts are 41 / 29. Berlin Breakout 2026 (2026-06-13) gives the Urban Spree address but is billed as a festival across three venues on the RAW-Gelände, so part of it may be off-site; without it S1 is 35.

### Arts at the Armory (`bos-armory-*`, Boston)
- Source: Own WordPress site with the Events Manager 7.4.3 plugin. The homepage month calendar (?mo=M&yr=2026) still serves past months and lists every event of a day as its own em-cal-event row (date, time, title, event id); each day's title attribute lists the same events, so nothing is cut. The same page carries a preview block for every event (data-event-id) with the event's date, time and category list. No web.archive.org requests (archive reads were stopped in polite.py).

Apr. 08, 2026 at 7:00 pm'. Get To the Gig Presents Bad Time Records Tour '26: calendar 2026-06-14 6:00 PM, page 'Sun. Jun. 14, 2026 at 6:00 pm', doors 6:00, show 6:30. These are show dates.
- robots.txt (17 Oct 2026): allowed. https://artsatthearmory.org/robots.txt (Yoast/WPForms, 392 bytes) disallows only /wp-content/uploads/wpforms/, /*blackhole and /?blackhole; there is no Anthropic group. The homepage carries a ?blackhole= trap link, which was not followed. polite.py allowed every page read; a second fetch by the checker gave the same file.
- Terms (17 Oct 2026): none-found. Footer: 'Privacy Policy Proudly powered by WordPress The Center for Arts at the Armory'; no © or 'All rights reserved' notice and no use licence. The privacy policy (Jan 2024) covers only logs, cookies, e-mail and embeds. None of the 25 saved bodies has text forbidding copying, reproducing or republishing, or limiting use to personal viewing. Checked: https://artsatthearmory.org/ (footer); https://artsatthearmory.org/privacy-policy/; https://artsatthearmory.org/about/; https://artsatthearmory.org/events/perfume-genius/; https://artsatthearmory.org/terms/ (404); https://artsatthearmory.org/terms-of-use/ (404); https://artsatthearmory.org/terms-of-service/ (404); https://artsatthearmory.org/terms-and-conditions/ (404); https://artsatthearmory.org/legal/ (404)
- Operator (§1): The City of Somerville owns the building (acquired by eminent domain in June 2021). The Center for Arts at the Armory, a non-profit, is the anchor tenant and runs the Performance Hall and the Cafe (https://artsatthearmory.org/about/). Booking goes through the venue's own Booking Inquiry Form (https://artsatthearmory.org/venue-rental/performance-hall/), and its own ticket links go to Eventbrite. Most nights are rentals produced by outside groups. Outside presenters among counted rows: The Bowery Presents 4 in S1 + 2 in S2, Live Nation Presents 1 in S1 (a comedy-tour title), Get to the Gig 5 + 7 (one with MassConcerts), and a promoter trading under its owner's name 4 + 0; none presents more than half of the counted shows, so none is a house booker (Amendment 14). Ownership evidence rests on the venue's own site; no press or company-record search was made.
- Rooms: the Events Manager category field, not the title, names the room: the calendar's event previews, the event pages and the iCal CATEGORIES line carry 'Performance Hall', 'Cafe' or 'B5 Lavender Room' beside genre categories such as Music or Dance (Amendment 14, rooms). The site lists an event's categories in alphabetical order, so a row given both Cafe and Performance Hall (6 counted rows: 5 in S1, 1 in S2, conventions and fairs) goes to the Cafe, the first one listed. Rows whose categories name no room go to `bos-armory-house`, a pool room of the same building. B5 Lavender Room (`bos-armory-b5`) has only two S1 rows, both 'Somerville Poetry Workshop', which the word 'workshop' drops, so it is pool. The Mezzanine is part of the Performance Hall (venue-rental page).
- Coverage: S1 107, S2 97 for the building (every em-cal-event row of the Apr-Sep 2026 month calendars, counted three times by separate scripts). Raw rows S1 122, S2 115. Removed by Amendment 4 words: S1 14 (workshop 2, comedy 12), S2 14 (comedy 10, workshop 1, class 1, open mic 2). Canceled: S1 1, S2 3. Off-site: S2 1 ('One Night Art School @10 Prospect St.', whose page says it is not happening at Arts at the Armory). Per room, placed by the category list in the saved month calendars: Performance Hall 54 / 47, ROOTED Cafe 53 / 49, no room 0 / 1 ('Watch THE FINAL GAME at Arts at the Armory!', category Community only), B5 Lavender Room 0 / 0. Performance Hall and ROOTED Cafe each meet coverage by themselves, so both are test rooms of one cluster (`bos-armory`); `shows.csv` confirms or corrects this (Amendment 12). No single company presents more than half of either room's counted shows (Performance Hall: Get to the Gig 12 of 101, the most; ROOTED Cafe: no outside presenter above 1). About a third to two-fifths of counted rows are music; the rest are dance socials and classes (West Coast Swing Training is 26 of the Cafe's 102), comedy and improv without the word, storytelling, circus, theatre, conventions, fairs and receptions, which count under the Amendment 4 words.
- §5: the Boston metro has other eligible venues in other clusters (`bos-burren`, `bos-passim`, `bos-rockwell`).
- Capacity: Performance Hall 495 ('Its capacity is a maximum of 495 standing', https://artsatthearmory.org/venue-rental/performance-hall/); Cafe 52 ('It has a capacity of 52', https://artsatthearmory.org/venue-rental/cafe/).
- Platform: self-hosted WordPress 7.1.3 with Events Manager 7.4.3 (Nexcess hosting), not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue or a Rockhouse/Etix site. Tickets through Eventbrite and promoters' links.
- Requests during the eligibility check: artsatthearmory.org 26 in the first check, plus 1 robots.txt re-fetch by the checker (27 lines in requests.log). The per-room counts were made later from the saved bodies, with no new request.

- Source: Static hand-built HTML events calendar on the venue's own https://burren.com/music.html. Each day has a heading ('MONDAY MAY 18') and each row has Room, Time, BAND (title) and Text cells. The live page lists only coming shows (2026-10-12 to 12-22), so S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of that same own page. The CDX lists 27 status-200 captures of music.html from 2026-03-08 to 10-10 (26 of www.burren.com/music.html, 1 of burren.com/music.html).
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/20260521025549id_/https://burren.com/music.html (one capture, 169 rows from May 18 to Sep 19); CDX queries for burren.com/ (prefix) and burren.com/music.html; the live https://www.burren.com/music.html for date checks only
- Dates checked: All 169 rows of the May 21 capture sit under day headings whose weekday matches the 2026 calendar (0 mismatches). 'TRIVIA NIGHT - Trivia night in the Burren Backroom every Monday!' sits under MONDAY MAY 18 / 25 and JUNE 1; 'COUNT-OFF ENTERTAINMENT - COLLEGE BAND THURSDAYS!' sits under THURSDAY MAY 21. On the live page, 'Down in Davis Presents DOLLY PARTON SING ALONG BRUNCH (Postponed from Sept 25)' sits under Sunday Oct 18 with a daytime time. The headings are show dates. Every row has a show time (most 7pm-10pm; daytime rows too, five of them back-room S1 rows starting 10:30am-11:30am, one of which is the dropped KIDS OPEN MIC); none falls between 00:01 and 04:59.
- robots.txt (15-16 Oct 2026): allowed. https://burren.com/robots.txt and https://www.burren.com/robots.txt return 200 with an empty body, so every path is allowed. There is no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed music.html and web.archive.org.
- Terms (15-16 Oct 2026): none-found. No terms, legal or privacy page; the homepage, music.html and more.html carry no terms link and no copyright notice. The footer reads only 'THE BURREN | 247 ELM ST. DAVIS SQ. SOMERVILLE MA 02144 | 617 776 6896'. Checked: https://burren.com/; https://www.burren.com/music.html; https://www.burren.com/more.html; https://burren.com/terms (404); https://www.burren.com/terms (404); https://www.burren.com/terms-of-use (404); https://www.burren.com/terms-of-service (404); https://www.burren.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://www.burren.com/legal (404); https://www.burren.com/privacy (404); https://www.burren.com/privacy-policy (404). A second check the same day found no copyright or terms text in the saved live music.html or the May 21 capture, and no bot check (pages returned 200; missing paths returned the host's plain 404 page).
- Operator: private owners (a couple of musicians, named in https://www.burren.com/more.html; open since January 1996), house booking ('All other band inquiries including our night time cover band bookings' go to house staff, more.html). more.html names an individual as the contact for the Backroom Series, Americana sessions and open mic; a press article names him and his outfit 24 Hour Concerts / 24 Hour Music as running the series since its launch in 2011 (more.html: launched in 2011 with WGBH). 50 of the 93 back-room rows in the May 21 capture (49 of the 77 counted ones) carry a tickets.24hourmusic.com link. A ticket link is not a presenting credit and no row names 24 Hour Concerts as presenter, so no company is shown to present more than half of either room's shows (Amendment 14); if `presented_by` on `shows.csv` shows that it does for the back room, it becomes that room's operator. It is not Live Nation or AEG, and no saved page of the other listed Boston venues names it as a presenter. In-page ticketing goes only to tickets.24hourmusic.com, with no Ticketmaster, AXS or Live Nation link. No public source links the venue or 24 Hour Concerts to Live Nation, AEG, Crossroads Presents or Bowery Boston, and 2026 Somerville press (search snippets) reports a lease extension to the end of 2026 and a cancelled tower project, not a sale. Other presenters named on rows: Count-Off Entertainment (college band nights) and Down in Davis.
- Rooms: the Room cell of each row (<td class="Room">, separate from the BAND title): THE FRONT ROOM and THE BACK ROOM. Every row in the capture names one of the two, so no row goes to a house room.
- Coverage: Lower bounds from one web.archive.org capture (2026-05-21): S1 >=112 (front room 57, back room 55), S2 >=41 (front room 19, back room 22). S1 covers only May 18 to Jun 30 (Apr 1 to May 17 not read); S2 is only what a May copy listed of the summer. Raw rows S1 126, S2 43. Removed by the Amendment 4 words in the BAND title: S1 14 (7 'TRIVIA NIGHT', 6 'COMEDY NIGHT', 1 'KIDS OPEN MIC'), S2 2 (1 comedy, 1 trivia). 'closed' appears only in the description of the IRISH SESSION rows ('Weekly closed session with ...'), not in the title, so they count. Back-room S2 includes 4 rows titled 'TBD', which the rule keeps; without them the back room has >=18. No canceled or off-site rows; one S2 back-room row (Jul 31) is a show 'Rescheduled from May' and is counted. Back room is a test room (>=55 / >=22). The front room (S2 >=19 on this capture) was pool; on the compiled rows (549 rows, back room 135 / 114, front room 131 / 112) it meets 20 / 20 by itself, so it is a test room in `venues.csv` (Amendment 1, rooms; the role check of Amendment 12). web.archive.org is stopped in polite.py since a second 429 (16 Oct 21:38 UTC, 00:38 on 17 Oct server time), so the other captures (every 1-3 weeks across both periods) were not read.
- Platform: hand-built static HTML (table layout, music.html / more.html / food.html); the 404 page is the web host's generic page. No site builder, no Rockhouse Partners / Etix footer, no 'Powered by' line.
- Capacity: no figure published (more.html: 'The Back Room is available for private parties & special events. For parties of 30 or more...', not a capacity).
- Requests during the eligibility check: burren.com 3, www.burren.com 12, web.archive.org 3, theburren.com 2 (redirects to celtic.com, another site; not used)

Event pages give the location 'The Louisiana, Bristol, GB', the date and the start time. The sitemap lists the same 153 Apr-Sep 2026 event URLs. Re-admitted on §5 in the merge (`candidates.md`); `candidates.md` had it "not used" on §5 alone.
- Read: https://www.thelouisiana.net/events/2026/04 to https://www.thelouisiana.net/events/2026/09 (6 pages); https://www.thelouisiana.net/sitemap.xml; event pages https://www.thelouisiana.net/events/2026/04/glasshouse, /events/2026/07/rodadesamba, /events/2026/08/acidfest-bristol (date checks); /about, /contact and one /news page (operator and terms checks). No web.archive.org copy was used.
- Dates checked: Glasshouse: listing 'Wednesday, 1 April 2026' = event page 'Wednesday, 1 April 2026 7:30pm'; Roda De Samba: 'Sunday, 12 July 2026 3pm'; ACIDFEST BRISTOL: 'Thursday, 27 August 2026 7:15pm'. Each matched its listing date, so these are show dates. None of the 3 starts lies between 00:01 and 04:59 (the contact page gives a 10:30pm curfew Mon-Sat, 10:15pm Sunday).
- robots.txt (14 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://www.thelouisiana.net/robots.txt: 'User-agent: *' disallows only /connectors/ /core/ /manager/ /setup/; Sitemap declared; no Anthropic group (per the 15 Oct record; the robots.txt body was not saved, 230 bytes, status 200).
- Terms (14 Oct 2026 UTC; re-checked 17 Oct 2026 on the saved bodies under the 16 Oct readings): none-found. The only notice is the footer on every page, '©2026 The Louisiana Bristol // The King Of Pixels // Faultless Design' (a bare notice plus the web designers' credits, which Amendment 14 says is not terms). A scan of all 20 saved bodies for reproduc, republish, personal use, non-commercial, all rights reserved, scraping, crawling, automated, copyright, 'terms of' and 'without permission' found nothing. The six standard terms paths are 404 (one identical 'Page Not Found' page). Checked: https://www.thelouisiana.net/ (footer); https://www.thelouisiana.net/about; https://www.thelouisiana.net/contact; https://www.thelouisiana.net/terms (404); https://www.thelouisiana.net/terms-of-use (404); https://www.thelouisiana.net/terms-of-service (404); https://www.thelouisiana.net/terms-and-conditions (404); https://www.thelouisiana.net/legal (404); https://www.thelouisiana.net/privacy-policy (404)
- Platform: self-hosted MODX site (robots.txt paths /connectors/ /core/ /manager/ /setup/; MODX extras pdoTools and phpThumbOf under /assets/components/), designed by The King Of Pixels and Faultless Design. Not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma or Music Glue, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer or any Etix link in any saved body. Third-party hosts in the bodies are only social links (X/Twitter, Facebook, Instagram, YouTube, Patreon), the two designers' own sites, Google Tag Manager, Google Maps (a map link on event pages, a map embed on the contact page), Google reCAPTCHA and the data-vocabulary.org breadcrumb markup; no ticketing host appears. No bot check and no 403: every venue-host request was a 200 except the six 404 terms guesses; the reCAPTCHA guards only the contact form, not the listings.
- Operator: a family (private owners; the 15 Oct record says they are named in press, and no press page was saved). The about page: 'The Louisiana is a 140-capacity, family-run venue' and 'A Proudly Independent UK Music Venue'. No company owner is identified, and no public source read names a Live Nation or AEG owner or stake. Booker (Amendment 14): the venue names no booking company ('While external promoters book the national bands that perform at The Louisiana, we also make sure the venue is a platform for homegrown talent'); booking enquiries go to the venue's own contact form, phone and mail address. Tickets: 'You can purchase tickets via Bristol Ticket Shop or online. We don't have tickets in-house'; no ticket seller is linked on the saved pages. The listings' promoter field, counted on the 153 S1/S2 rows: every row has one; 103 different strings as written, 93 once case and 'Present'/'Presents' are made the same; the largest single company is DHP (11 'DHP Presents' and 3 'DHP Family Presents', 14 rows), then the venue itself ('The Louisiana Presents', 11 rows; the 15 Oct record's 13 is not reproduced on the saved pages), Cellar Door 7 ('Cellar Door Presents' 5, 'Cellar Door' 2), Crosstown Concerts 5, and Live Nation 4 ('Live Nation Presents' 3, 'Live Nation & SJM Concerts Present' 1). No company presents more than half of the rows (77 of 153), so there is no house booker and the operator is the owner; the venue is its own cluster. §1 passes. Under the literal reading of Amendment 1 (descriptive only) the Live Nation rows make Live Nation an operator, and the venue drops out there (`candidates.md`).
- Rooms: one (the upstairs room). No room or stage field and no room filter; the main room is on the first floor (contact page).
- Coverage: S1 85, S2 68, exact as listed (Apr 30, May 30, Jun 25; Jul 24, Aug 19, Sep 25), recounted on 17 Oct 2026 from the six saved month pages. No row carries a cancel or postpone mark, no title is empty and no title has an Amendment 4 word (the venue's own 11 Jul row 'ENGLAND VS NORWAY' has none of the words, so it counts as listed). The site seems to remove canceled shows rather than mark them, so a canceled show would not show; S2 would need to lose 49 rows to go under 20.
- §5: Bristol also has Trinity Centre (`brs-trinity`, pool-only, another cluster), so §5 passes, and Trinity passes §5 only through The Louisiana. §6: Bristol is not in Study 1.
- Capacity: 140, published by the venue (https://www.thelouisiana.net/about).
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.thelouisiana.net 21 (requests.log: 20 in the 14 Oct UTC check, robots.txt included, and 1 later /news page read the same night), web.archive.org 0

### Trinity Centre (`brs-trinity-*`, Bristol)
- Source: The venue's own Plone site. The year folder /whats-on/2026 lists every 2026 event with its local start (DD/MM/YYYY hh:mm), 213 rows. The same folder's standard Plone iCal export (ics_view, 213 VEVENTs) gives the room in its LOCATION field; 212 VEVENTs match the listing exactly on title, date and time, and one (Garden Party) differs only by a double space. No page links to ics_view; it was read only for the room field, and the 'Where' field of the sampled event pages gives the same values.
