### The Pabst Theater (`mke-pabst`, Milwaukee)
- Source: The operator's own live month-calendar JSON on www.pabsttheatergroup.com (date -> HTML entries with date, start time, title and detail link; past months are still served), plus each event's own detail page, which prints the date, the start time and a separate 'Venue' field. The calendar covers every Pabst Theater Group building and has no venue field and no cancellation marker, so a row is placed and checked only on its detail page. pabsttheater.org redirects to www.pabsttheatergroup.com. No archive copy was needed.
- Read: https://www.pabsttheatergroup.com/events/calendar/2026/4 to https://www.pabsttheatergroup.com/events/calendar/2026/9 (?v=2; six live month views, 219 unique rows across all PTG buildings); detail pages https://www.pabsttheatergroup.com/events/detail/<slug>; https://www.pabsttheatergroup.com/rentals/ticketed-event-rentals for capacity. web.archive.org: 2 requests in scouting on 15 Oct UTC (a CDX query and the 15 May copy of the venue detail page), not used for the counts; none in the checks (archive reads were stopped in polite.py).
- Dates checked: calendar and detail page agree for Geoff Tate (1 May 2026, 7:30 PM), Oklou (25 Apr 2026, 8:00 PM), Aimee Mann (27 Jul 2026, 8:00 PM) and Punch Brothers (13 Sep 2026, 8:00 PM). These are show dates with show start times; none falls between 00:01 and 04:59.
- robots.txt (17 Oct 2026): allowed. https://www.pabsttheatergroup.com/robots.txt is 'User-agent: *' with no rules; no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every page read. Every answer in both checks was 200, with no 403, no 429 and no bot check (the only 'cloudflare' text is a cdnjs font-awesome stylesheet). The scouting record's host table lists one 406 (not a 403) on a non-www wp-json URL, not a listing page; requests.log itself holds no 406.
- Terms (17 Oct 2026): silent. The 'Terms of Use' page holds only the privacy policy ('Effective: April 18, 2024 This privacy policy (this "Policy") describes Pabst Theater Group's ... policies and practices with regard to personal data'), with nothing on copying, reproduction, reuse or automated reading. The footer carries 'Copyright © 2026 The Pabst Theater Group', a bare notice (Amendment 14), and 'a carbon house experience'. carbonhouse has no terms page (/terms returns its home page), and its privacy policy 'applies to our websites located at www.carbonhouse.com', so no platform text reaches visitors of client sites. A second check re-read the saved terms body with the same result. Checked: https://www.pabsttheatergroup.com/terms-of-use; https://www.pabsttheatergroup.com/privacy-policy; https://www.pabsttheatergroup.com/ (footer); https://www.carbonhouse.com/; https://www.carbonhouse.com/privacy; https://www.carbonhouse.com/terms (home page)
- Platform: carbonhouse 'Showtime CMS' (carbonhouse is AXS Digital LLC, part of AEG's AXS group; https://news.pollstar.com/2019/09/20/aeg-buys-full-ownership-of-axs/). Not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue or Webflow, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer. Tickets are sold through AXS (AEG-owned). carbonhouse and AXS are vendors, not the owner or house booker. The separate rentals site pabsttheatergrouprentals.com is on Squarespace; its home page was read once for venue names and it is not a listing source.
- Operator (§1): Pabst Theater Group (PTG), owner and house booker. The theatre was bought from the City of Milwaukee in 2002, and in 2016 'booking and management responsibilities' moved to PTG (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pabst_Theater; its infobox gives owner Pabst Theater Group and operator PTG Live Events, while an AAA page names the Pabst Theater Foundation). PTG is co-owned by two private persons (named in a press article); one co-owner is also its talent buyer (https://prism.fm/podcast/ep-2/). PTG calls itself 'a proudly independent, local Milwaukee organization' and is a founding member of NIVA (https://www.placera.se/pressmeddelanden/associated-banc-corp-associated-bank-pabst-theater-group-kick-off-five-year-partnership-extension-with-contest-for-best-associated-bank-vip-green-row-seats-to-two-top-concerts-20260602). NIVA statements of Feb 2026 cast PTG as Live Nation's local competitor (https://www.nivassoc.org/statements/2026/live-nation-expansion-spurs-proposal-to-demolish-independent-venue; https://urbanmilwaukee.com/pressrelease/live-nation-expansion-could-erase-117-year-old-historic-milwaukee-venue-niva-pushes-back/). A web search in Oct 2026 found no Live Nation or AEG stake in, sale of or merger with PTG in 2025-2026. FPC Live (majority-owned by Live Nation; https://biztimes.com/live-nation-settlement-milwaukee-venues-impact/, https://tonemadison.com/articles/saudi-arabias-investment-in-live-nation-is-good-for-madisons-music-scene-actually/) co-presents some PTG shows, seen only off-site at Fiserv Forum (https://www.pabsttheatergroup.com/); none of the 32 Pabst detail pages read (34 detail pages in all) names FPC Live, Live Nation, AEG or Frank Productions as presenter.
- Presenters: outside presenters named on Pabst detail pages are radio stations (88Nine, WUWM, WMSE, 102.9 The Hog), ICON Concerts, Boswell Book Company, Sincerely in Song and a private person's company (named in a Pabst event page), each on a few shows; none presents more than half, so none is a house booker (Amendment 14).
- Rooms: one. The room comes from the detail page's separate 'Venue' field ('The Pabst Theater'); the /events page also has a venue filter (data-venue=1), but it lists coming events only. The Pabst Pub appears only as a rental space. Other PTG buildings in the calendar and filter (The Riverside Theater, Turner Hall Ballroom, Miller High Life Theatre, Vivarium, The Fitzgerald, Mo's Irish Pub, Fiserv Forum) are other buildings and were not checked; their rows are not counted here.
- Coverage: S1 20, S2 about 24 (not exact). Rows were first placed by the venue letter in PTG's thumbnail file names ('YYYY.MM.DD-P-...') and then on detail pages: all 32 'P' detail pages opened (37 calendar rows: 22 in S1, 15 in S2) give 'Venue The Pabst Theater', and the 2 rows with no code opened were at Vivarium and Miller High Life Theatre. Multi-day thumbnails ('YYYY.MM.DD-DD-X-...') carry the same letter; every other S1 or S2 row carries another building's letter (V, T, R, M or F), so no uncoded row is left unplaced. S1: all 22 'P' rows (21 pages) were opened; two are marked CANCELED on their pages though the calendar does not mark them ('The Musical Soiree' with three billed performers, 18 Apr; 'Crime Unfiltered True Crime: Headlines Tour', 7 Jun), so S1 = 20. That total counts both Gary Owen shows on 10 Apr (8:00 PM and a 9:45 PM late show); Amendment 12 folds repeated acts on one night in S2 only, so 20 stands, and a reading that folded S1 early and late shows would give 19. S2: 15 rows confirmed on detail pages with no CANCELED marker (4 Fresh Coast Jazz Festival rows from one festival page, two of them on 22 Aug; They Might Be Giants on two nights). Amendment 12 counts the two 22 Aug festival rows once in the run, so S2 is 23 run units on these rows; 9 rest on the thumbnail code alone (TUSK, Todd Rundgren, Marshall Tucker Band, Danny Gonzalez, Michael Feinstein, Jonathan Van Ness, Derrick Stroup, Kevin Smith, Fraggle Rock), and S2 >= 20 needs 5 of them to be live Pabst rows. Amendment 4 words remove none ('A Screening of "Say Anything" with John Cusack' and 'Airplane! Live' do not contain 'film screening'). Off-site PTG shows (Fiserv Forum and the other buildings) are not counted. About 60% of rows are music; the rest are stand-up, talks, podcasts, authors, film-with-guest, plays and family shows without an Amendment 4 word, which count under the rule. Test room on these counts; the compiled `shows.csv` must read the 9 S2 detail pages for the 'Venue' field and a CANCELED marker in the event block (the FAQ footer on every page mentions 'postponed or cancelled'), and the room becomes pool if S2 falls under 20 (Amendment 12).
- §5: the Milwaukee metro (Milwaukee-Waukesha MSA) has other eligible venues in other clusters (`mke-cactus`, `mke-linnemans`).
- Capacity: 1,341 ('Venue Capacity: 1,341', https://www.pabsttheatergroup.com/rentals/ticketed-event-rentals; secondary sources give 1,339).
- Requests: the record gives www.pabsttheatergroup.com 37, pabsttheater.org 1 (redirect), carbonhouse.com 3, pabsttheatergrouprentals.com 1 (first check, 42 in all) and 15 in the second check, counts that leave out robots.txt reads. requests.log, robots.txt reads included, has www.pabsttheatergroup.com 53 (3 on 15 Oct, 36 in the first check on 16 Oct, 14 in the second check), pabsttheater.org 2, www.carbonhouse.com 4, www.pabsttheatergrouprentals.com 2, and web.archive.org 2 (scouting on 15 Oct, see Read)

### MilkBoy Philadelphia (`phl-milkboy`, Philadelphia)
- Source: The venue's own HTML pages (home page / and /events/), which carry schema.org Event JSON-LD blocks (name, startDate in UTC, location 'MilkBoy', a description with the written show date) and per-row 'Doors/Show' times. The home page lists every upcoming show, so past periods come from web.archive.org copies of the own home page. No events feed was used.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://milkboyphilly.com/ for ts 20260316142740, 20260412160919 (S1), 20260712144507 (S2; this copy alone gives 57 S2 rows); live: https://milkboyphilly.com/ and https://milkboyphilly.com/events/ for date and room checks. The archive index also lists copies of 15 May, 21 May, 23 May and 13 Jun 2026 that were not read (the 23 May and 13 Jun requests were refused locally after the archive 429), so no copy between 12 Apr and 12 Jul was read.
- Dates checked: These are show dates. A 9 Apr 2026 row has startDate 2026-04-10T00:00Z = 8 pm 9 Apr ET, and its description says 'Thursday, April 9, 2026'. Pheedback: 2026-05-29T00:00Z, description 'Thursday, May 28, 2026'. Drayton Farley: 2026-05-24T00:30Z = 23 May local. UTC times are converted to America/New_York and 00:01-04:59 starts go to the previous night (Amendment 3). Two descriptions carry typos (City of the Sun gives the year 2025; DirtySnatcha says Aug 17 against a startDate of Aug 13); the startDate is used. Caution for compiling: 46 of the 115 Tixr events have a startDate of exactly T00:00:00+00:00 (8 pm shows). Amendment 12's midnight item reads such a value as date-only and uses the written UTC date, which would date them one day late. The S1 and S2 counts are the same either way (no such row falls on 1 Apr, 1 Jul or 1 Oct).
- robots.txt (15 Oct 2026): allowed. milkboyphilly.com/robots.txt: 'User-agent: * / Disallow: /calendar/action* /events/action* /cdn-cgi* /*? / Crawl-delay: 3'. Paths / and /events/ are allowed; polite.py allowed the venue pages and the archive copies. No bot check and no 403 from the venue host (every reply 200 or 404).
- Terms (15-16 Oct 2026): copyright-only. The only notice is the footer line '© 2025 All rights reserved. Site by Sungraphic.com' on every page, a bare notice (Amendment 14). No terms, privacy or use-licence text exists on the site, the footer has no terms link, and the sitemap lists no such page. Checked: https://milkboyphilly.com/ (footer); https://milkboyphilly.com/events/ (footer); https://milkboyphilly.com/terms (404); https://milkboyphilly.com/terms-of-use/ (404); https://milkboyphilly.com/terms-of-service/ (404); https://milkboyphilly.com/terms-and-conditions/ (404); https://milkboyphilly.com/legal/ (404); https://milkboyphilly.com/privacy-policy/ (404); https://milkboyphilly.com/privacy/ (404); https://milkboyphilly.com/accessibility/; https://milkboyphilly.com/faq/; https://milkboyphilly.com/about-us/; https://milkboyphilly.com/contact-us/; https://milkboyphilly.com/wp-sitemap-posts-page-1.xml
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress (hello-elementor theme, Elementor/ElementsKit, the 'sungraphic-events' plugin by the agency Sungraphic; hosted on Flywheel, a WP host, not a site-builder). Not Fatsoma, Squarespace, Wix, Music Glue or Rockhouse Partners (no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS' footer on the home or events pages). The only 'cloudflare' string is a cdnjs.cloudflare.com stylesheet, not a bot check. Tickets go through Tixr (most rows) and Etix (one relocated show), neither of them Live Nation or AEG; they do not host the site.
- Operator: owned by MilkBoy (the company behind MilkBoy the Studio and MilkBoy coffee/bars), held by private co-owners (named in the venue's about page and in press). House booker: Rising Sun Presents. The FAQ says 'Most of our booking is done by Rising Sun Presents' (https://milkboyphilly.com/faq/). Rising Sun Presents is a Philadelphia-area concert company formed by the ownership of Ardmore Music Hall together with Point Entertainment and Bonfire Entertainment (headline of https://www.gratefulweb.com/?p=507341, seen as a search result, not read). The 8x10's FAQ (https://the8x10.com/revival/, Baltimore) answers 'Who are the new owners?' with 'We are Rising Sun Presents, the team behind many independent venues in the Mid-Atlantic region including our flagship venue, Ardmore Music Hall'; Ardmore Music Hall's FAQ (https://ardmoremusichall.com/faqs/) sells tickets for MilkBoy and for 'other Rising Sun Presents Shows'. No public source names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake or booker for MilkBoy, Rising Sun Presents, Point Entertainment or Bonfire (web searches). A former Bonfire co-owner (named in a press article) was also a vice-president at AEG Presents Philadelphia until 2020; that press article still calls Bonfire independent, and no source discloses an AEG stake. Outside presenters (Riot Nerd, The Machine, One Night Live, Philly Music Fest, Soundroom, Outlier Group) present single nights.
- Rooms: one. Every listing card in the three copies and the live home page carries the same venue id (8435) and the same venue-name field ('MilkBoy'), and every JSON-LD row has location.name 'MilkBoy'; there is no room field or filter. The site describes one upstairs music venue above the downstairs bar and restaurant. Capacity 200: 'This location includes our 200 capacity upstairs venue' (https://milkboyphilly.com/faq/).
- Coverage: S1 >=55, S2 >=59 (union of the schema.org Event rows in the three web.archive.org copies, deduplicated by Tixr event id; S1 rows span 2026-04-01..06-28, S2 rows 07-02..09-27). Two S1 rows are taken out: Seedhe Maut, listed for 2 Jun 2026 in the March and April copies, is listed under the same Tixr event for 10 Sep 2026 in the 12 Jul copy, so it is counted once, at its new date in S2 (the June date was moved); and one row of 2026-05-05 is off-site (Amendment 1): from the 12 Jul copy on, a script on the page re-points its ticket link to an Etix page for Kung Fu Necktie, another building. One more S1 row (3 May) is listed in the 16 Mar copy and gone from the 12 Apr copy, without a cancellation mark; it is counted, and S1 is >=54 without it. Every S2 row of the 12 Apr copy dated after 12 Jul is still in the 12 Jul copy (one renamed, same Tixr event). A lower bound for shows added between copies, but not strict: a cancellation after the last copy read does not show. No Amendment 4 word in any title (0 removed in S1, 0 in S2) and no row marked canceled or postponed in the S1/S2 copies (the live pages mark cancellations in the title, '**CANCELED**', only on rows from Oct 2026 on). A second check re-parsed the saved bodies with its own script and got the same rows; the first count, 57 / 58, kept Seedhe Maut at its first date and the off-site row.
- §5: Philadelphia's only other eligible cluster is PhilaMOCA (`phl-philamoca`, re-admitted in the merge), so MilkBoy passes §5 only through it.
- Requests during the eligibility check: milkboyphilly.com 20, web.archive.org 5 (requests.log, 15-16 Oct UTC; the web.archive.org 5 are two CDX queries and the 16 Mar, 12 Apr and 12 Jul copies. Five more copy requests were refused locally by polite.py after a 429 on another venue and never sent; the 12 Jul copy among them was fetched after the archive pause and is in the 5)

### PhilaMOCA (`phl-philamoca`, Philadelphia)
- Source: The venue's own WordPress home page (https://www.philamoca.org/, titled 'PhilaMOCA: Events'). Each event card carries the show date in `<time class="event__date" datetime="YYYY-MM-DD">`, a label field (event__label: a presenter, a series, or 'Off-site event at <building>'), the title (event__title) and Doors/Show times. The live page keeps only coming shows (51 cards, 14 Oct 2026 to 20 Mar 2027). The WordPress REST type `event` gives only post dates, and the event pages carry no show date. S1 and S2 therefore come from web.archive.org copies of the same own page. No feed was used for rows.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://www.philamoca.org/ for ts 20260315095116, 20260416060200, 20260513132340, 20260608034148, 20260616215820, 20260717195418, 20260803145832 and 20260910235128. These are 8 of the 28 status-200 copies that the CDX query (https://web.archive.org/cdx/search/cdx?url=philamoca.org/&from=20260301&to=20261001) lists. Live pages read: https://www.philamoca.org/, https://www.philamoca.org/about/, /wp-json/wp/v2/types, /wp-json/wp/v2/event?per_page=3, /wp-json/wp/v2/pages, one event page, and the terms paths below. The 8 Jun, 17 Jul and 3 Aug copies were saved gzip-encoded and were read decompressed (163,250, 173,858 and 152,184 bytes).
- Dates checked: These are show dates. Each card's datetime agrees with the weekday and month/day printed beside it (e.g. 2026-05-29, 'Fri', Doors 19:30, Show 20:00, in the 15 Mar and 13 May copies). Times are local wall-clock values with no zone. Across the S1/S2 cards the latest door or show time is in the 22:00 hour, and none falls between 00:01 and 04:59 (Amendment 3), so the listed date is the night. One card gives no time. REST post dates are creation times, not show dates (e.g. 2026-10-12T19:47 for a post).
- robots.txt (14 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://www.philamoca.org/robots.txt (200, 185 bytes; the body was not saved, and the rules are as recorded in the 15 Oct check): 'User-agent: *', 'Crawl-delay: 10', and Disallow for /admin/, /includes/, /config/, /logs/, /backup/, /.env, /wp-admin/ and /wp-includes/. The home page, /about/ and /wp-json/ are allowed. polite.py allowed every URL read, and web.archive.org's robots.txt allows the snapshot and CDX paths used. There was no bot check and no 403 from the venue host. Every reply was 200, except two requests logged with no status and a 28-byte body (/terms-and-conditions, /legal; the 15 Oct record calls them timeouts), which returned 200 on retry.
- Terms (14 Oct 2026 UTC; re-checked 17 Oct 2026 from the saved bodies): none found. The home and about pages have no terms, privacy or use-licence text and no '©' or 'All rights reserved' notice. The footer holds only social links and a site-design credit. The site's REST page list (/wp-json/wp/v2/pages) has two pages, 'Events' and 'About PhilaMOCA'. Checked: https://www.philamoca.org/ (footer); https://www.philamoca.org/about/; https://www.philamoca.org/terms, /terms-of-use, /terms-of-service, /terms-and-conditions, /legal and /privacy-policy (each a soft 200 that serves a byte-identical copy of the home page, 153,069 bytes); https://www.philamoca.org/wp-json/wp/v2/pages
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress with its own theme ('philamoca2022' under wp-content/themes) and the WordPress REST API. It is not a site-builder: not Fatsoma, Squarespace, Wix, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly. There is no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer. 'ROCKHOUSE' occurs in none of the 20 saved page bodies, and every 'etix' string is either an Etix ticket link (www.etix.com/ticket/p/...) on an event card or part of 'simpletix'. Tickets go through Etix on 129 of the 152 on-site S1/S2 card rows (deduplicated by date and title). The other rows link to a presenter's own site, Eventbrite, Ticketleap, SimpleTix, Handstamp, Instagram or YouTube, one links to TicketWeb, and one (the 23 May 'PRIVATE PARTY' card) has no link. Etix does not host the site. All 8 web.archive.org copies are the full events page (title 'PhilaMOCA: Events', 50 to 63 event cards each, 152,184 to 181,335 bytes). None is a bot-check or hosting-check page, so every copy counts.
- Operator: The about page (https://www.philamoca.org/about/) says the building belongs to a private owner who rented it out in 2010 to a private person, who founded PhilaMOCA. The current manager and programmer, also a private person named on that page, took over in March 2012. Rentals go to the same in-house contact. No owner or booker company is named. No company presents more than half of the counted rows, so there is no outside house booker (Amendment 14), and PhilaMOCA is its own cluster. The largest outside presenter is R5 Productions ('R5 Productions presents' in the label field), on 13 of 69 counted S1 rows and 12 of 78 counted S2 rows (25 of 147). Next come Blood Sick Underground Cinema (3 + 3; one of the S2 rows it presents jointly with Factory 25) and the Philadelphia Alternative Arts Council (1 + 4); every other presenter has one or two rows. R5 is an independent Philadelphia promoter. The 15 Oct check cites a search snippet (https://xpn.org/aeg-bowery-presents-philly/, not saved; no request in requests.log) saying that R5 co-owns other Philadelphia rooms with Bowery Presents (AEG). No public source found discloses a Live Nation or AEG stake in R5, of 50% or any other size, and R5 neither owns nor operates PhilaMOCA. The saved bodies name no Live Nation, AEG or Bowery company. One S1 row (9 May) links to TicketWeb, a Ticketmaster site, and the off-site Keswick Theatre row (14 Aug) links to AXS. Neither is the venue's own ticketing, which is Etix. §1 passes under Amendment 14. Under the literal reading, R5 and the other named presenters become operators of this room. None of the 3 web.archive.org copies of MilkBoy read names R5, so as far as R5 goes, Philadelphia keeps two clusters.
- Rooms: one. The cards have no room or stage field, and the page has no room filter. The label field names only a presenter, a series or an off-site building. Capacity: no figure is published (none on /about/).
- Coverage: S1 >=69, S2 >=78. This is the union of the 8 copies, deduplicated by date and title, which gives 72 S1 rows and 82 S2 rows. Taken out: retitles, 1 in S1 (24 Apr: the same Etix event under two titles, in the 15 Mar and 16 Apr copies) and 2 in S2 (8 Jul and 26 Jul: the same Etix event under two titles each); off-site rows (Amendment 1), 1 in each period (25 Apr, 'Off-site event at RUBA Club'; 14 Aug, 'Off-site event at the Keswick Theatre'); and Amendment 4 title words, 1 in each period (4 Jun, 'comedy'; 1 Aug, 'market'). No card is marked canceled or postponed (the only status note is 'Sold Out'), and no title is empty. If the label is read with the title, 2 'Comedy@PhilaMOCA' rows go in each period: S1 67, S2 76. One S1 card, 'PRIVATE PARTY' on 23 May ('Invitation Only', no ticket link, in the 13 May copy), passes the word list, whose word is 'private event', so it is counted; without it, S1 is 68. The counts are lower bounds: the copies are 8 to 38 days apart and the last is from 10 Sep, so shows that were announced and held between copies, or after 10 Sep, are missed. Two corrections to the 15 Oct record: S2 is 78, not 79 (the record merged only the 8 Jul retitle), and one 'PRIVATE PARTY' card falls inside S1 (the record put them all outside S1 and S2). Many rows are film screenings and talks that pass the word list. They count, as the scope note in "Notes for compiling" says.
- Also checked: The Philadelphia metro (Philadelphia-Camden-Wilmington MSA) has one eligible venue of another cluster, MilkBoy Philadelphia (`rising-sun-presents`), so §5 passes; the pass is symmetric. Philadelphia is not in the Minneapolis-St Paul metro, and PhilaMOCA is neither Hideout nor Black Cat (§6, Amendment 11). The row was re-admitted on §5 on 17 Oct 2026, after a second check under the 16 Oct readings.
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.philamoca.org 15 (14 on 14 Oct 2026 between 20:21 and 20:45 UTC, which include robots.txt and the two timed-out terms paths and their retries, plus the REST page list at 22:06 UTC), web.archive.org 9 (1 CDX query and 8 copies). Source: requests.log. The 15 Oct record counts 14 + 9; the log has 24 lines.

### Kings (`ral-kings`, Raleigh)
- Source: The venue's own WordPress site (custom theme "Kings"). Shows are a custom 'shows' post type, not exposed in the WP REST API but listed in the shows sitemaps (wp-sitemap-posts-shows-1.xml and -2.xml, in post-ID order). The homepage calendar lists announced future shows (the 15 May copy lacks some June shows that have their own pages now, so it is not complete); each /shows/<slug>/ page prints the show date with 'Time' and 'Doors' fields. Past show pages mostly stay live, but the site reuses one page for each night of a recurring event (/shows/sub-rosa-3/, listed for 19 May in the 15 May capture, now carries Tue 20 Oct 2026), so earlier nights of recurring events survive only in archive copies of the homepage. Page 2 of the shows sitemap is served with HTTP 404 but carries the full XML (464 URLs, lastmod up to 2026-10-12); the scouting note that the sitemap ends in Jan 2025 is wrong.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/20260515224741id_/https://www.kingsraleigh.com/ (homepage calendar copy, fetched in scouting on 15 Oct and re-parsed with no new request); https://www.kingsraleigh.com/wp-sitemap-posts-shows-1.xml and https://www.kingsraleigh.com/wp-sitemap-posts-shows-2.xml; live show pages https://www.kingsraleigh.com/shows/<slug>/ chosen from the sitemap in post-ID order and for every capture row re-checked. Six more web.archive.org copies of the homepage (21 Mar to 27 May 2026) were refused by polite.py before any request was sent, because the host was stopped after a 429 at 20:17 UTC on 16 Oct (23:17 server time) on another venue.
- Dates checked: GASKET 'Sunday, May 24th, 2026', time 7:00PM, doors 6:00PM on its live page, the same as in the 15 May capture; DG the Artist 'Friday, July 3rd, 2026', 8:00PM, live and in the capture; DUEL 'Thursday, July 16th, 2026', 8:00PM, in both. These are show dates, not post dates. Rows moved since the capture: Solya (29 May in the capture, andmoreagain presents) now reads 'Friday, August 28th, 2026 ... [Rescheduled from 5/29!]', so the 29 May row is postponed and dropped; /shows/balmora/ (7 Jun, Overcome Fest presents) now serves the page with canonical /shows/holder/, still 'Sunday, June 7th, 2026' at Kings with no cancel marking; EMM (16 Aug) is marked 'Rescheduled from 1/18/26' and is held on its new date.
- robots.txt (read 15 Oct 2026, applied by polite.py on 16 Oct): allowed. https://www.kingsraleigh.com/robots.txt: 'User-agent: * / Disallow: /wp-admin/ / Allow: /wp-admin/admin-ajax.php'. No Anthropic agent group. polite.py allowed every URL read. No bot check and no 403 on any page.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): none-found. The footer has no terms, legal or copyright link and no '©' notice. The page sitemap https://www.kingsraleigh.com/wp-sitemap-posts-page-1.xml lists only home, booking, faq, private-events, about, contact and kings-20th; no terms, legal or privacy page exists, and each such path serves the homepage (canonical https://www.kingsraleigh.com/). No page read forbids copying or reuse or limits use to personal viewing. Checked: https://www.kingsraleigh.com/ (footer and links); https://www.kingsraleigh.com/terms; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/terms-of-use; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/terms-of-service; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/terms-and-conditions; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/legal; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/privacy-policy; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/about; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/faq; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/booking; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/private-events/; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/contact/; https://www.kingsraleigh.com/wp-sitemap-posts-page-1.xml
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress with a custom theme and W3 Total Cache, not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue or Weebly, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer on any saved page. The only 'captcha' text is an unused reCAPTCHA setting in the calendar plugin's config. Tickets are mostly sold through Etix ('Most of our tickets are sold through Etix.com', /faq), a ticket vendor, not an owner or booker.
- Independence: owned by private owners, a group of three local people who bought Kings in October 2023 (named in https://www.kingsraleigh.com/about and in a press article, https://raleighmag.com/2023/11/kings-new-ownership/; also https://indyweek.com/music/kings-returns-raleigh-late-summer/ and https://indyweek.com/newsletter/a-raleigh-institution-turns-twenty-five/). Booking is in-house: https://www.kingsraleigh.com/booking gives the venue's own address, booking@kingsraleigh.com. No source names a Live Nation or AEG stake, partnership or talent-buying deal; in Raleigh, Live Nation runs Coastal Credit Union Music Park and The Ritz, not Kings.
- Rooms: one. Neither the listing nor the show pages have a room or stage field or filter. No row read mentions Neptunes, the basement bar, which press says was sold separately (2022-23). Capacity: no figure from the venue (the FAQ mentions only bar stools and bench seats); a figure of 250 appears only in 2010 press.
- Coverage: S1 >=21, S2 >=26 (lower bounds from rows read). S1: 18 rows dated 15 May-28 Jun 2026 from the 15 May homepage copy (the postponed Solya row left out) plus 3 live show pages (Thagomizer 14 Jun, Neem The Animist 19 Jun, [Early] Day Aches 26 Jun); 10 of the capture rows were confirmed on live pages with an S1 date and no cancel or postpone marking (13 live S1 pages with the 3 live-only rows), the 19 and 26 May Sub Rosa nights stand on the capture only, and 6 capture rows were not re-checked live (THE ARENA 6 Jun, GAG, HONEY! 12 Jun, Long Relief 13 Jun, Document One 20 Jun, [LATE] Seven Teller 26 Jun, AZUL 27 Jun). The margin is 1. 1 Apr-14 May is unseen. S2: 7 rows from the capture (3 Jul, 16 Jul, 26 Jul, 15 Aug, 16 Aug, 14 Sep, 20 Sep) plus 17 live show pages from the sitemap, plus Solya (28 Aug, rescheduled from 29 May, sold out) and the reused Sub Rosa page /shows/sub-rosa-5/ (Tue 25 Aug), both on live pages with no cancel marking. Without the 5 capture S2 rows not re-checked live (Robopocalypse, Royale Lynn, EMM, Black Marble, Pertinence), S2 is still at least 21. About 60 May-Sep sitemap posts were not opened. Removed (Amendment 4): 0 of the counted rows; the sitemap slug emo-night-karaoke-3 has a karaoke title and its date was not read. No row is off-site. Programme is mostly live bands, with some DJ, dance and drag nights and one musical-theatre night, none carrying an Amendment 4 word.
- Presenters: 31 of the 47 counted rows name a presenter (12 of 21 in S1, 19 of 26 in S2), but no single company presents more than 4 of the 47, so none is a house booker. Named: Blind Path Booking 4 (GASKET, [Early] Day Aches with Ricochet Collective, Flesh Carving, Lucky Feeling), andmoreagain 4 (Everything Yes, Black Marble, Red Shahan, Solya), Bassbunny Productions 4 (three Sub Rosa nights, Document One), Overcome Fest 3, Crank It Loud 3, Hopscotch Music Festival 3 (the three late nights 10-12 Sep), and one row each for THE ARENA, GAG! Raleigh, PHILM, Ricochet Collective, To Be Heard Booking (with Kings), Three Lobed Recordings with WXDU 88.7 and Smashed Plastic Record Pressing, Strange World, Sorry State Records, Scorned Earth Booking, Raleigh Maison and Lincoln Theatre ('Lincoln Theatre presents Late Night Gov't Mule Afterparty', 26 Sep). None is Live Nation or AEG. None of these names appears in the saved Pour House pages or its 16 May calendar copy. For the literal reading (Amendment 14) they go into `presented_by`.
- §5: Raleigh's only other eligible cluster is The Pour House (`ral-pourhouse`), and Kings is The Pour House's only partner, so each passes §5 through the other.
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.kingsraleigh.com 53 on 16 Oct (38 in the full check, 15 in the review, all HTTP 200 except the 404-served sitemap page 2), plus 2 in scouting on 15 Oct; kingsraleigh.com 1 (robots.txt, 15 Oct); web.archive.org 0 on 16 Oct (3 in scouting on 15 Oct: two CDX queries and the 15 May copy; 6 refused by polite.py and not sent); see requests.log

### The Pour House Music Hall & Record Shop (`ral-pourhouse`, Raleigh)
- Source: The venue's own Webflow CMS site. The live /calendar lists future shows only (show-name, show-start-date, show-slug fields). Past /shows/<slug> pages stay live, with a visible date including the year and data-start (UTC ISO) and data-venue attributes. Earlier listings come from a web.archive.org copy of the own /calendar page. Show slugs for Jul-Sep came from the cached web.archive.org CDX list.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/20260516060113id_/https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/calendar (S1 16 May-30 Jun and S2 9 Jul-15 Aug); https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/shows/<slug> (22 past S2 pages, 8 Jul-27 Sep); https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/calendar (live, future shows)
- Dates checked: /shows/18-the-galentines-14-jul shows 'July 14, 2026 • 8:00 pm' with data-start 2026-07-15T00:00:00Z (8 pm EDT) and data-doors 23:00Z. /shows/keith-allen-w-royal-jelly-25-jul shows 'July 25, 2026' with data-start 2026-07-26T01:00Z (9 pm EDT). /shows/18-el-ten-eleven-18-sep shows 'September 18, 2026' with data-start 2026-09-19T00:00Z. The 16 May copy's dates (e.g. 'July 18, 2026' The Pietasters) match the slug's day and month. These are show dates. Compiling reads the visible local date and time. data-start is UTC, and 8 pm EDT shows are written as exactly 00:00:00Z of the next day; read literally, Amendment 12's "Midnight" rule would take that written date, one day late, so data-start is not used for dates.
- robots.txt (16 Oct 2026): allowed. https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/robots.txt answers 200 with an empty body (allow all); no Anthropic agent group. pourhouseraleigh.com and thepourhousemusichall.com redirect to www.pourhouseraleigh.com. polite.py allowed / and /shows/*; web.archive.org allowed.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): copyright-only. The only notice is '© 2025 The Pour House. All rights reserved.' (footer and privacy page), a bare notice (Amendment 14). The privacy policy (last updated 10 Dec 2024) has no use-licence section; it mentions 'our website terms', but no terms page is linked or exists at the standard paths. No text on any page read forbids copying, reproducing or republishing, or limits use to personal viewing. Checked: https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/ (footer); https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/calendar (footer links: /privacy, /accessibility only); https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/privacy; https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/accessibility; https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/about; https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/hours-info; https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/booking; https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/contact; https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/bar; https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/terms (404); https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/terms-of-use (404); https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/terms-of-service (404); https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/legal (404)
- Platform: Webflow (meta generator 'Webflow' on every page, cdn.prod.website-files.com), which passes (see Webflow under "Platform and archive terms" above). No 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer, no Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma or Music Glue marker. No bot check and no 403. An Opendate tag sells tickets and a Google reCAPTCHA script serves the forms; neither hosts the site or stands in front of the listings. Tickets through Opendate, See Tickets US and some eventim.us links; no Ticketmaster or Live Nation ticketing. The Shopify record-shop store and pourhousepressing.com are separate sites, not listing sources.
- Independence: owned by a private owner (named in https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pour_House_Music_Hall), owner since 2012 after the founder; no company is named. Booking is in-house: the owner is the venue's sole talent buyer (https://raleighmag.com/2018/07/09/bands-beer/), and https://www.pourhouseraleigh.com/booking takes booking requests through the venue's own form and staff. Press (https://indyweek.com/music/possibilities-pitfalls-reinventing-pour-house/) and web searches show no Live Nation or AEG stake, sale or booking deal.
- Rooms: one. Every show page carries the same data-venue value, 'The Pour House Music Hall & Record Shop'. The upstairs record shop of the same building (/about) appears only in title text ('(Record Shop) ... Listening Party'), so under Amendment 1 it is not a room of its own and its rows are not off-site. Capacity: no figure published by the venue or its operator (Wikipedia and a press article give figures; neither is a venue or operator source).
- Coverage: S1 >=36, S2 >=31 (lower bounds. S1 from the 16 May 2026 web.archive.org copy of /calendar only, covering 16 May-30 Jun; 1 Apr-15 May not read. S2: 9 rows from the same copy plus 22 different live /shows/ pages; the cached CDX list holds 73 Apr-Jun and 41 Jul-Sep slugs). 0 rows removed by the Amendment 4 words; 'Listening Party' is not on the list, so the 6 S1 and 1 S2 record-shop listening parties count (S1 30, S2 30 without them). No canceled, postponed or off-site row seen; the 45 rows from the 16 May copy were not re-checked live. One '(SOLD OUT)' title counts.
- Presenters: a few rows name an outside presenter ('TK Productions Presents: MavicMachine' 15 Aug, 'The Woods Presents: Beyblade Rave THREE' 26 Jun, '... presented by Get Sad Y'all'); each presents far fewer than half the counted shows, so none is an operator (Amendment 14).
- §5: passes through Kings (`ral-kings`), Raleigh's only other eligible cluster (see Kings).
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.pourhouseraleigh.com 44, pourhouseraleigh.com 3, thepourhousemusichall.com 2, web.archive.org 3 (15-16 Oct; see requests.log)

- Source: The venue's own HTML (a custom CMS). The homepage 'UPCOMING EVENTS' list has one row per run, with the run's first night as a full date in the aria-label and a display date range. Dated event detail pages (title 'ACT - YYYY-MM-DD', doors and show time for each set) are listed in the venue's own sitemap.xml, whose URL path carries each event's status (buy-tickets, sold-out, postponed, closed). The live /events/calendar shows only upcoming events, and /events/default/calendarJson answers only to POST (GET returns null), so neither was used. S1 and S2 were counted on a web.archive.org copy of the venue's own homepage, and S2 was checked against the live sitemap.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/20260518235851id_/https://yoshis.com/ (one copy, read during scouting and reused from the saved body); live: https://yoshis.com/sitemap.xml and https://yoshis.com/events/<status>/<slug>/detail
- Dates checked: These are show dates. KURT ROSENWINKEL: detail page 'Wed May 20, 2026 ... Doors: 7:00PM Show: 7:30PM', list row May 20. THE SPINNERS: 'July 25, 2026 Doors: 7:00PM / Show: 7:30PM' plus a 9:30PM set and two July 26 sets, list row 'Sat 7.25 to Sun 7.26'. KIRK WHALUM: 'September 4, 2026 Doors: 7:00 PM / Show: 7:30 PM' plus Sep 5 sets, list row 'Fri 9.4 to Sat 9.5'. A list row is one run, and its aria-label date is the run's first night; the three detail pages read show 1, 4 and 4 sets.
- robots.txt (15 Oct 2026): allowed. https://yoshis.com/robots.txt returns 404 (no robots.txt, so everything is allowed). polite.py allowed every yoshis.com URL requested; web.archive.org also allowed.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): none-found. The footer of every page read has only 'Privacy Policy | Refund Policy (c) 2026 Yoshi's / Bay Area Web Design' (link to websightdesign.com), a bare notice. The privacy policy covers order data and IP addresses only, with no use licence; the refund policy covers ticket refunds only; the venue page's 'PHOTO AND VIDEO POLICY' is about filming at shows, not site content. No text on any page read forbids copying or reuse or limits use to personal viewing. Checked: https://yoshis.com/terms (404); https://yoshis.com/terms-of-use (404); https://yoshis.com/terms-of-service (404); https://yoshis.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://yoshis.com/legal (404); https://yoshis.com/privacy-policy; https://yoshis.com/refund-policy; https://yoshis.com/about-us; https://yoshis.com/venue

The venue page lists a CEO and co-owner, a private owner (named in https://yoshis.com/venue); the founders were private persons (named in https://yoshis.com/about-us). One co-founder has died (https://www.ktvu.com/news/co-founder-iconic-yoshis-oakland-dies-age-82); no sale or change of owner since was found. Booking is in house: the club's own Artistic Director and a 'TALENT & MARKETING MANAGER', both with @yoshis.com addresses, and the FAQ says 'Yoshi's promotes and hosts many different types of music'. No public source names a company owner or links the club to Live Nation, AEG, Goldenvoice or Another Planet (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoshi%27s; https://www.eastbaymag.com/?p=10420). The San Francisco location on Fillmore Street 'was sold in 2014' (https://yoshis.com/about-us; https://news.jazzline.com/news/yoshis-san-francisco-jazz-club-sold/); yoshis.com is only the Oakland building at Jack London Square, which is in the San Francisco-Oakland-Fremont MSA.
- Rooms: one. Neither the list nor the detail pages have a room or stage field or filter. The restaurant and the bar & lounge hold no listed shows. Capacity 310: 'The 310 seat club, 250 seat restaurant, and 60 seat bar & lounge' (https://yoshis.com/about-us).
- Coverage: S1 >=35, S2 >=49 (rows, that is runs, in the 18 May 2026 copy of the own homepage, dated by first night; 99 rows, earliest 18 May). S1 covers 18 May to 30 Jun only: 36 rows, 1 removed by Amendment 4 ('FRAN BOOGIE: KARAOKE NIGHT', 23 Jun). 1 Apr to 17 May is not covered, because web.archive.org is stopped for good in polite.py after a second 429 and no other copy was read. S2: 55 rows, none removed by the words; 2 rows absent from the live sitemap (13 Aug and 16 Aug, possibly canceled) and 4 'PRE SHOW VIP EXPERIENCE / MEET & GREET (SHOW TICKET NOT INCLUDED)' add-on rows are taken out, giving 49 (53 with the add-ons, which carry no Amendment 4 word). Shows added after 18 May are not counted. Every S1 row still has status buy-tickets or sold-out in the sitemap. No row in the copy is marked canceled or postponed, and none is off-site; the sitemap's 'closed' entries (slugs closed-1, closed-2, ...) are not among the copy's rows. 'MAKE ME LAUGH MONDAY' (1 Jun) is probably a comedy night, but its title has no Amendment 4 word, so it counts. Programme: almost entirely music (jazz, R&B, soul, funk, Latin), with some high-school jazz ensembles and tribute acts.
- Presenters: outside presenters appear only on single shows (for example 'Presented by JaZzLine INSTITUTE', 5 Jul), far below half of the counted rows; no company presents more than half.
- Requests during the eligibility check: yoshis.com 18, web.archive.org 3 (requests.log, 15-16 Oct UTC; two CDX queries and the 18 May homepage copy, all on 15 Oct)

### Blue Strawberry (`stl-bluestrawberry`, St. Louis)
- Source: The venue's own HTML music calendar on its homepage (one page with month tabs; each row has datetime_event, performer_event and name_event fields and a /show/detail/<id> link). The live site keeps only coming shows (Oct 2026 onward), and past /show/detail/<id> pages fall back to the homepage, so S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of that same own page.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/20260517045524id_/https://bluestrawberrystl.com/ (one capture, 71 rows from 19 May to 15 Nov 2026); the domain CDX list from scouting; the live https://bluestrawberrystl.com/, https://bluestrawberrystl.com/music and https://bluestrawberrystl.com/show/detail/2184 for date, status and room checks only (past detail pages /show/detail/2014, 2032 and 1981 fall back to the homepage)
- Dates checked: The FAQ says 'Most of our shows begin at 7:30pm, with doors opening at 6pm', and most listed times are 7:30PM. Live detail page /show/detail/2184 gives 'Friday, October 30, 2026 - 9:30PM' for the row the home calendar lists at that date and time. 17 May capture rows read 'Tuesday, May 19 2026, 6:30PM', 'Wednesday, May 20 2026, 7:30PM', 'Thursday, May 21 2026, 7:30PM'. These are show times. All starts in the capture lie between 14:00 and 22:00, so Amendment 3 moves no date.
- robots.txt (15 and 16 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://bluestrawberrystl.com/robots.txt: 'User-agent: * / Disallow:' (empty, allow all). No Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every URL read.
- Terms (15 and 16 Oct 2026 UTC): silent. The Terms & Conditions page has one sentence: 'By using the site, you agree to work with Blue Strawberry along commonly accepted principles in the normal course of business.' The privacy page covers only visitors' own data. The footer reads '© 2019 - 2026 Blue Strawberry', a bare notice (Amendment 14). No text on any page read forbids copying, reproducing, republishing or automated reading, or limits use to personal viewing; a second check re-read the terms page live (200) and scanned every saved body for such words and found none. Checked: https://bluestrawberrystl.com/ (footer and FAQ); https://bluestrawberrystl.com/terms/conditions; https://bluestrawberrystl.com/privacy; https://bluestrawberrystl.com/terms (404); https://bluestrawberrystl.com/terms-of-use (404); https://bluestrawberrystl.com/terms-of-service (404); https://bluestrawberrystl.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://bluestrawberrystl.com/legal (404)
- Platform: custom-built app on the venue's own host (csrf token, its own cart and ticketing at /performer/cart and /show/ticket, a fullcalendar page at /music). Not Wix, Squarespace, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer. Third-party hosts are only Square (gift cards), Google, GTM, Google Docs and social links. No bot check and no 403 in front of the listings (a reCAPTCHA guards only the contact form); every venue-host request was a 200 except the five 404 terms guesses.
- Operator: private owners (a founder and producer and a co-investor, private persons, named in press articles found by web search, one of them https://www.stlmag.com/culture/st-louis-new-cabaret-venue-blue-strawberry/; no press page was saved). No company owner is identified. Booking is in-house: the contact page gives the venue's own booking address ('Booking: booking@bluestrawberrystl.com'), press describes the founder as the main booker, and tickets are sold on the venue's own site (no Ticketmaster, AXS or Etix). No public source names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake, booking deal or sale; the ownership press dates from 2019-2024 and no 2026 news was found either way. Outside presenters named on rows of the 17 May capture: The Midnight Company (3 rows) and Patterson Performing Arts, Inc. (1 row), both far below half.
- Rooms: one (showroom and lounge). No room or stage field and no room filter; the only filters are month tabs. 'In The Lounge - 9:30pm-1am' appears only in the name_event text of the karaoke rows (title text, as the site's own og:description reads it), and those rows are removed by the karaoke word anyway. The /music feed has no room field either (per event: id, title, show, start, allDay, ticket and detail links, description, price, cover, show_status, and event_type, which holds only REGULAR, NO-COVER or PRIVATE), and 'In The Lounge - 9:30pm-1am' sits in its show (event name) field.
- Coverage: S1 >=35, S2 >=29 (one web.archive.org capture, 2026-05-17). S1 covers only 19 May to 30 Jun (37 rows: May 15, Jun 22), minus 2 Amendment 4 rows (a Singers Open Mic, 'open mic'; a weekly karaoke night, 'karaoke'); 1 Apr to 18 May is not in the capture. S2 is what the May copy listed in advance (Jul 15, Aug 11, Sep 3), with no word hits; two of the 29 rows are early and late sets of one act on 30 Aug, which the run counts once (Amendment 12, `duplicate`), so S2 is 28 act-nights. The homepage calendar never shows a status marker, so a later cancellation would not show; 10 of the 29 would have to fall away to go under 20. The capture lists the weekly karaoke and open-mic nights only once and the Wednesday open jam (counted, no Amendment 4 word) only twice, on 20 and 27 May, while the live calendar lists all three every week. Three independent parsers (the first check, the second check and this merge check) gave the same counts on the saved capture. web.archive.org is stopped in polite.py since a second 429 on 17 Oct 2026 (server time), so the other homepage captures listed in the CDX were not read: 2, 11, 16, 17, 18 and 20 Apr and 6, 8, 9, 10 and 11 May (these would cover S1 from 1 Apr), 18 and 23 May, 8 and 17 Jun, 11 Jul and 16 Aug (these would confirm S2).
- Capacity: no figure published (the private-event size '30 to 100 people' on https://bluestrawberrystl.com/contact is not a room capacity).
- §5: St. Louis's only other eligible cluster is Off Broadway (`stl-offbroadway`, re-admitted in the merge), so Blue Strawberry passes §5 only through it.
- Requests during the eligibility check: bluestrawberrystl.com 17 without robots.txt reads (16 in the first check, 2 of them on 15 Oct; 1 in the second); with its 2 robots.txt reads, requests.log has 19 lines. web.archive.org 2, both in scouting on 15 Oct UTC: the domain CDX list and the 17 May capture (see requests.log)

### Off Broadway (`stl-offbroadway`, St. Louis)
- Source: The venue's own Webflow show list at https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/shows (grid and calendar tabs; each row has a show name, a start date, an end date, a slug ending in the show date, and doors and show times), with one page per show at /shows/<slug>. The live list holds only coming shows (Oct 2026 onward), so S1/S2 come from the one web.archive.org copy of that list in the period and from the web.archive.org CDX index of the /shows/<slug> pages, dated by the slug. Re-admitted on §5 on 17 Oct 2026, after a second check under the 16 Oct readings; `candidates.md` had it "not used" on §5 alone.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/20260516230645id_/https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/shows (the only /shows capture from 15 Feb to 14 Oct 2026, per the CDX query collapsed by day); CDX lists of offbroadwaystl.com/shows/ by prefix (from 1 Jan, collapsed by URL, 63 rows; from 17 May, uncollapsed, 85 rows); live https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/shows and https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/shows/jenna-davis-14-oct for date checks only.
- Dates checked: Live list row Jenna Davis, Oct 14, doors 7:00 pm, show 8:00 pm = event page 'October 14, 2026 ... Doors: 7:00 pm • Show: 8:00 pm' ('WED. OCT 14, 2026 Doors 7PM | Show 8PM'). In the 16 May capture, the start date of all 52 rows equals the date in its slug; one row (Twangfest 27) is a three-day run, 25 to 27 Jun, whose slug carries its first day. The 51 rows that give a time all show 7:00, 7:30 or 8:00 pm; the Twangfest 27 row gives only its dates (June 25 to June 27, 2026), so its listed start date is used (Amendment 3). Amendment 3 moves no date. These are show dates.
- robots.txt (14 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/robots.txt returned 200 with an empty body (0 bytes), which allows every path; no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every URL read; web.archive.org allowed the snapshot and CDX paths.
- Terms (14 Oct 2026 UTC): none-found. The footer links only Privacy and Accessibility and reads '© , Off Broadway' (privacy page: '© 2025 Off Broadway. All rights reserved.'), a bare notice (Amendment 14). The privacy page covers only visitors' own data; it mentions 'our website terms', but no such page exists. A scan of every saved body from the venue host and of the listing capture found no text that forbids copying, reproducing, republishing or automated reading, or limits use to personal viewing. Checked: https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/ (footer); https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/privacy; https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/booking; https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/faqs; https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/terms (404); https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/terms-of-use (404); https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/terms-of-service (404); https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/legal (404); https://www.offbroadwaystl.com/sitemap.xml (404); https://www.opendate.io/terms (ticket platform; see Platform)
- Platform: Webflow (data-wf-site and data-wf-page on every page, assets on cdn.prod.website-files.com), which passes as for Horseshoe Tavern; the footer reads 'Powered by Opendate', and tickets are sold through Opendate (the event page embeds an app.opendate.io ticket frame; no Opendate page was read beyond its terms and homepage). Not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue or Weebly, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer on any of the 12 saved venue-host bodies or the listing capture. Opendate's General Terms of Services Agreement (Last Updated March 5, 2025) opens with 'By accessing or using Opendate's website, mobile applications, or services, you ... are agreeing' and defines a 'Consumer' as customers using the Services 'to consume information', but it 'shall govern all interactions between Opendate and the Organizer', and no clause puts a restriction on a Consumer; its copying ban (Section 12, item 5: 'Copy, reproduce, display, publish ... the Services') and its other restrictions bind 'Organizers and their authorized representatives', and no clause puts a duty on people who view an organizer's listings, so it does not forbid reading or reusing them. No bot check and no 403 in front of the listings: every venue-host request was a 200 (7) or a 404 (6), and the reCAPTCHA on the pages guards only the newsletter form. The web.archive.org listing copy is the real page ('Shows | Off Broadway', 52 rows), not a check page.
- Operator: private owners (a private couple who bought the venue in 2006 and have run it since, named in https://stlouisarts.org/off-broadway/, published 2 Jul 2024 and updated 16 Jul 2025; it says a sale is 'down the road' and names no buyer). No company owner is identified. Booking is in-house: the venue's booking page reads 'Please reach out to offbroadwaystlbooking@gmail.com for booking inquiries.', and the live event page reads 'Presented by Off Broadway'. Tickets are sold on Opendate, not Ticketmaster or AXS. No public source read names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake, booking deal or sale. Outside presenters named in the 52 rows of the 16 May capture: 1% Productions (1 row), far below half. A scouting search snippet that linked Consort Productions to some shows was not confirmed by a repeat search (15 Oct record) and is not used.
- Rooms: one. The list has no room, stage or location field and no room filter; the only tabs are Calendar and Grid.
- §5: St. Louis also has Blue Strawberry (`stl-bluestrawberry`, another cluster), so §5 passes, and Blue Strawberry passes §5 only through Off Broadway. §6: St. Louis is not in Study 1.
- Coverage: S1 >=25, S2 >=27 (union by slug of the 16 May capture and the CDX index; no Amendment 4 word in any title or CDX-only slug). S1 is 27 by slug: 24 listing rows and 3 CDX-only Twangfest day pages (25, 26 and 27 Jun). The listing row 'Twangfest 27' overlaps the day-1 page, and the Anvil page of 6 Jun was captured as a 404 on the show day (20260606234352), so it may have been canceled; without both, 25. S1 covers only 16 May to 30 Jun: the earliest 2026 capture of any /shows page is 13 May, and nothing covers 1 Apr to 15 May. S2 is what the 16 May copy listed in advance (22 rows) plus 5 CDX-only slugs captured on 6 Jun; it is at least 22 without them. The uncollapsed CDX list from 17 May to 14 Oct shows no 404 for any S2 slug, but a later cancellation would not show. Note: the 15 Oct record gives the cluster as `off-broadway-stl`; the merged row uses `stl-offbroadway`, a single-venue cluster either way. Compiled (18 Oct 2026, Amendment 12; the compile report): 54 rows, S1 26 and S2 28 counted, from saved web.archive.org copies only (the 16 May listing capture and 59 /shows/<slug> page copies; the prefetch task `compile:stl-offbroadway` saved its 60 copies on 18 Oct, 22:20-22:50 server time, with 62 requests in requests.log (19:20-19:50 UTC: 2 CDX queries and the 60 copies, all 200), and the compile made no request). 0 canceled, 0 off-site, 0 untitled, 0 removed by the Amendment 4 words. One row moved from S1 to S2: Solya is 9 Jun in the 16 May listing and 9 Sep on the later 6 Jun page copy, with no cancel or postpone mark, and the latest copy wins (S1 27 to 26, S2 27 to 28 against the union above). The 'Twangfest 27' listing row (written date-only on 25 Jun) and the three Twangfest day pages are written as their own rows, as Amendment 12 has it, and Anvil (6 Jun) is written as listed with canceled 0, since a 404 capture is not a cancel mark. 1 Apr to 15 May (45 nights) is still a gap, so the counts stay lower bounds, and 26 in-period captures were not prefetched or read (later copies of pages already read, and the Anvil 404; report.json, more_captures). Presenters named on the 54 rows: Off Broadway itself 10, 1% Productions 4 (once written '1% PRODUCTIONS'), Urban Chestnut Brewing Company 3 (the Twangfest day pages) and Old Rock House 1 (written 'old rock house'); none presents more than half, so there is no house booker, and the presenter check finds none of them Live Nation or AEG. A test room on these counts, but St. Louis sorts after the cut, so the merge draft caps it and the dry run counts it as a pool room (pool, 26 / 28, 54 rows, capped), with no coverage problem.
- Capacity: no figure published on the pages read.
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.offbroadwaystl.com 13 (7 at 200, 6 at 404), web.archive.org 4 (3 CDX lists, 1 listing capture), stlouisarts.org 2, www.opendate.io 3 (all on 14 Oct 2026 UTC; see requests.log)

### Horseshoe Tavern (`tor-horseshoe`, Toronto)
- Source: HTML of the venue's own Webflow pages (homepage 'Upcoming Shows' list with weekday date and door time; schema.org Event JSON-LD with startDate and location 'Horseshoe Tavern' from Jun 2026). The live site lists only upcoming shows and past CMS items are removed (the sitemap holds 60 /event/ items, all current or future), so S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of the venue's own homepage and /events.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/{20260328052722 /events, 20260410160305 /, 20260516054217 /, 20260609123101 /, 20260726011441 /}id_/https://horseshoetavern.com/ ; live: https://horseshoetavern.com/ and /events
- Dates checked: These are show dates with door times. Robyn Hitchcock 'Saturday, April 11, 2026 / Door Time 8:00 pm' (Apr 10 snapshot). Noah Gundersen 'June 14, 2026', doors 7:30 pm, same in 3 snapshots. Hank Williams 103rd Birthday 'September 16, 2026', doors 8:00 pm, with JSON-LD startDate 'September 16, 2026' and location Horseshoe Tavern (Jul 26 snapshot). Live event page /event/hovvdy shows 'Monday, November 9, 2026 7:00 pm ... Horseshoe Tavern'.
- robots.txt (15 Oct 2026): allowed. https://horseshoetavern.com/robots.txt: 'User-Agent: * / Disallow: (empty) / Disallow: /talent/'. polite.py allowed every page read; web.archive.org also allowed.
- Terms (15 Oct 2026): none-found. The venue site has no terms and no copying or reuse text. The site is built on Webflow, which Amendment 14 does not name. As I read them, Webflow's terms do not reach people who visit customers' sites. ToS 1.1: 'These Terms of Service govern your access to and use of the Sites [www.webflow.com and associated domains] and Platform [the SaaS ... that you access or subscribe to]'. ToS 4.4: 'Any natural persons accessing or using your Website Content are deemed your "End Users." You agree that Webflow does not have a direct relationship with any of your End Users'. ToS 3.2: 'You understand and agree that third party individuals may search for, see, use, and/or re-post any Website Content that you make publicly available on the Internet via the Platform.' The AUP (effective Oct 6, 2026) 'supplements Customer's ... agreement between you and Webflow'. Its scraping ban covers only materials obtained 'from the Platform or Webflow IP outside of what is intentionally made available'. So even if it reached visitors, it would not forbid reading the published listings. Checked: https://horseshoetavern.com/ (footer: Events, News, Merch, History, Email Sign Up, Booking, Contact; no terms or privacy link; no copyright notice); https://horseshoetavern.com/terms (404); https://horseshoetavern.com/terms-of-use (404); https://horseshoetavern.com/terms-of-service (404); https://horseshoetavern.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://horseshoetavern.com/legal (404); https://horseshoetavern.com/privacy (404); https://horseshoetavern.com/privacy-policy (404); https://horseshoetavern.com/booking; https://horseshoetavern.com/contact; https://horseshoetavern.com/history; https://webflow.com/legal/terms; https://webflow.com/legal/aup
- Platform: Webflow (data-wf-site, footer says "Powered by Webflow"); most tickets sold through Tixr, a minority through TicketWeb/Ticketmaster links
- Coverage: S1 >=78, S2 >=61 (union of five web.archive.org copies of the own pages; >=77 / >=59 without three rows that vanished before their date).
- Requests during the eligibility check: horseshoetavern.com 20, web.archive.org 8, www.horseshoetavern.com 1
