The 23:59 time is a placeholder, printed on pages as 'Doors: 11:59pm' next to the real 'DOORS: 6:00PM'. It does not fall in the 00:01-04:59 window, so the date stands.
- robots.txt (14 Oct 2026): allowed. robots.txt has only a sitemap line, no rules.
- Terms (14 Oct 2026): none-found. No terms document exists; the footer word 'Terms' is unlinked. Checked: https://rickshawtheatre.com/ (footer text '© 2026 Rickshaw Theatre | Terms | Web': 'Terms' is plain text with no link); https://rickshawtheatre.com/terms (404); https://rickshawtheatre.com/terms-of-use (404); https://rickshawtheatre.com/terms-of-service (404); https://rickshawtheatre.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://rickshawtheatre.com/legal (404); https://rickshawtheatre.com/privacy-policy (404)
- Coverage: S1 55, S2 33 (all published show posts with a date in the period; an upper bound if any row is off-site, since the feed has no venue field).
- Platform: self-hosted WordPress (not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma or Music Glue).
- Requests during the eligibility check: rickshawtheatre.com 25

### Rio Theatre (`van-rio`, Vancouver)
- Source: The venue's own REST feed, the listings endpoint of the Barker cinema/events plugin (namespace barker/v1 in https://riotheatre.ca/wp-json/), which the site's JavaScript calendar draws from. Each row has a showing id, the event or movie title and link (post types barker_event and barker_movie) and start_time with a -07:00 offset. The feed still serves past showings, so the live site is the source; no web.archive.org copy was needed.
- Read: https://riotheatre.ca/wp-json/barker/v1/listings?start_date=2026-03-31&end_date=2026-07-01&per_page=1000 (S1) and https://riotheatre.ca/wp-json/barker/v1/listings?start_date=2026-06-30&end_date=2026-10-01&per_page=1000 (S2), deduplicated by showing id; event pages https://riotheatre.ca/event/<slug>/ for date and presenter checks
- Dates checked: Dervish: feed 2026-04-26 19:30, page 'Sunday, April 26 Doors 7 pm | Show 8 pm'. Sue Foley: feed 2026-07-16 20:00, page 'Thursday, July 16 Doors 7:30 pm | Show 8:00 pm'. Artcell: feed 2026-09-13 18:00, page 'Sunday, September 13, 2026 at 6:00 PM'. The feed's start_time is the show date. Every row carries -07:00 (PDT for Apr-Sep) and none starts between 00:01 and 04:59.
- robots.txt (15 Oct 2026): allowed. https://riotheatre.ca/robots.txt (Yoast): 'User-agent: *' with an empty 'Disallow:', plus a sitemap line; no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every URL read, /wp-json/ included.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): copyright-only. The only notice is '© 2026 Rio Theatre' in the footer, a bare notice (Amendment 14). The other footer text is Google's reCAPTCHA notice for the newsletter form ('This site is protected by reCAPTCHA and the Google Privacy Policy and Terms of Service apply'). There is no terms page; Ticket Info and 'The Rio's Protocols' hold only membership and house rules, with nothing on copying, reuse or automated access. Checked: https://riotheatre.ca/ (footer); https://riotheatre.ca/terms (WordPress redirects to a film page, 'Terms of Endearment'); https://riotheatre.ca/terms-of-use (404); https://riotheatre.ca/terms-of-service (404); https://riotheatre.ca/terms-and-conditions (404); https://riotheatre.ca/legal (redirects to a film page, 'Legally Blonde'); https://riotheatre.ca/privacy-policy (404); https://riotheatre.ca/ticket-info/; https://riotheatre.ca/the-rios-protocols/; https://riotheatre.ca/contact/; https://riotheatre.ca/page-sitemap.xml (10 pages, none is terms or privacy)
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress (GeneratePress child theme, Yoast, Gravity Forms, Barker events plugin). Not Fatsoma, Squarespace, Wix or Music Glue, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer. reCAPTCHA v3 sits only on the newsletter and contact forms, not in front of the listings; no bot check and no 403 (every listing request returned 200). Tickets are sold on the venue's own riotheatretickets.ca, which was not read.
- Independence: run by a private owner and partners (named in https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Theatre and https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/british-columbia/rio-theatre-1.4814198) since 2008; press reports that the owner's group bought the building in 2018 with fundraising, private investors and a City of Vancouver grant (https://www.straight.com/movies/1088491/east-vancouvers-rio-theatre-its-final-stretch-its-campaign-purchase-theatre). Tickets include membership of the Vancouver Art House Society. Booking is in house: the Rio programmes its own films and rents the room for live events ('Interested in Booking? Please email us at bookings@riotheatre.ca', https://riotheatre.ca/bookings/). No public source names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake, house booker or ticketer.
- Programme: mostly film. Of the 421 counted rows, 331 are film showings (barker_movie; S1 158, S2 173) and 90 are live or event rows (barker_event; S1 44, S2 46); about 15 of those are music nights. The other event rows are stand-up, burlesque, film festivals, football match screenings ('FOOTBALL 2026') and live D&D. Film showings are listed shows on the source and count (Notes for compiling, scope).
- Rooms: one. Feed rows have no room or stage field and the site has no room filter. The main floor (301) and balcony (108) are seating sections of one auditorium. Capacity: 409 ('Seats : 409 (Main Floor: 301, Balcony: 108)', https://riotheatre.ca/bookings/; '409 comfortable seats', https://riotheatre.ca/about/).
- Coverage: S1 202, S2 219 (exact: every row of the two feed windows, 439 unique showings from 2026-03-31 to 2026-09-30, local date per Amendment 3). Removed by the Amendment 4 words: S1 10 (8 'private event', 1 'comedy', 1 'film screening' on a movie row), S2 7 (6 'private event', 1 'comedy'). No row is marked canceled or postponed, none has an empty title and none is off-site: every row links to a riotheatre.ca movie or event page and none names another building (the site's only mention of the Park Theatre is a shared 'Rio & Park Theatre Podcast' menu link). The event rows alone give S1 44, S2 46. One S2 row titled 'Private Rental' and 9 S2 'FOOTBALL 2026' screening rows carry no Amendment 4 word and count; without them the S2 event rows fall to 36. The feed may delete canceled showings instead of marking them; every count is far above 20.
- Presenters: outside companies present a minority of live nights. On counted S1 or S2 rows: Rogue Folk Club ('Rogue Folk Club presents Dervish', https://riotheatre.ca/event/dervish/, 26 Apr), Live Nation ('Presented by Live Nation' and 'The Rio Theatre is not handling ticket sales for this event', https://riotheatre.ca/event/mo-gilligan-the-mo-you-know/, 30 Apr), Dhaka Club Vancouver ('Dhaka Club Vancouver brings you Artcell', https://riotheatre.ca/event/artcell-live-in-vancouver/, 13 Sep), JFL Vancouver (in the title of two S2 rows on 4 Sep), and, by 'Presents'/'presented by' in the feed title, The Vancouver Burlesque Co., CAFTCAD, Sparky's Film Rentals and the East Van Laugh RioT comedy series; a few more titles name individuals as presenters. A-List Entertainment presents a later listing that is not among the S1 or S2 feed rows. All event rows together are 90 of 421 counted rows, so no outside company presents more than half and none is a house booker (Amendment 14). Under the literal reading of Amendment 1 (Amendment 14, last item), Live Nation would be an operator of this room through the counted 30 Apr row, so the room would fail §1 in that report.
- Requests during the eligibility check: riotheatre.ca 30 (3 on 15 Oct, 21 in the full check and 6 in the second check on 16 Oct; see requests.log), web.archive.org 3 (two CDX queries and one calendar copy in the 16 Oct archive survey, not used)

## Eligible, after the cap (not in `venues.csv`)
These venues were settled or re-admitted on 18 Oct 2026 and found eligible, but their metros sort after the cut of Amendment 12 (the
20th test cluster in `venues.csv` order is `cph-alice`), so none of them could enter the cap. Listed, they would only feed the pools
of metros that are not tested, so they are not in `venues.csv`; their sections are kept here in file order.
Open point (Portland clusters): the proposed rows and the sections disagree. `venues-b5-rows.csv` writes Polaris Hall with operator
Mississippi Studios in cluster `true-west`, and Mississippi Studios in cluster `pdx-mississippi`; the Polaris Hall section puts both
in `true-west`, the Mississippi Studios section keeps both in `pdx-mississippi`. The two share an operator, so they are one cluster
either way (Amendment 1); whether that cluster is `true-west` rests on press, not a saved body, and is left to the lead. Portland
sorts after the cut, so the cap does not move. Portland's §5 does not depend on it: Wonder Ballroom, settled eligible on 18 Oct
2026 (section below), is a cluster of its own, so Portland passes §5 whichever cluster the four venues are put in.
### Nice'n'Sleazy (`gla-nicensleazy-*`, Glasgow)
- Source: The Events Calendar (tribe) REST feed on the venue's own WordPress site. Each event gives start_date in local time with its zone (Europe/London; BST or GMT per row), the title, and event categories, of which 'Bar' and 'Basement' (both under the parent category 'Club') name the room; the category sitemap lists the site's own filters /events/category/club/bar and /events/category/club/basement for the same two rooms (those filter pages were not read). The feed's venue and organizer fields are empty on every row. Re-admitted on §5 on 18 Oct 2026, after a second check under the 16 Oct readings (`candidates.md`, "How the rules were read"), because SWG3 became eligible on 18 Oct; `candidates.md` had it "not used" on §5 alone.
- Read: https://www.nicensleazy.com/wp-json/tribe/events/v1/events?start_date=2026-04-01%2000:00:00&end_date=2026-06-30%2023:59:59&per_page=50&page=1 to page=4 (S1; the feed's total is 182 events in 4 pages) and the same query for 2026-07-01 to 2026-09-30, pages 1 to 4 (S2; total 170), with page 5 a 404 ('event-archive-page-not-found', past the last page). The site echoes each window as 06:00 on the first day to 05:59:59 on the day after the last (its day cut-off). Also https://www.nicensleazy.com/ (home), /hire, /book, /wp-sitemap.xml, /wp-sitemap-posts-page-1.xml, /wp-sitemap-posts-tribe_venue-1.xml, /wp-sitemap-taxonomies-tribe_events_cat-1.xml, one event page (https://www.nicensleazy.com/event/upstairs-gig-killer-smile-support-07242026) and the terms paths below. nicensleazy.com redirects to www.nicensleazy.com. No web.archive.org copy was used.
- Dates checked: These are show dates. 'UPSTAIRS GIG – GOODBYE BLUE MONDAY': start_date 2026-04-01 18:00 (utc_start_date 17:00, BST), post date 2026-03-10, description 'Punk show at Nice N Sleazy on April 1st'. 'UPSTAIRS GIG – KILLER SMILE + SUPPORT': start_date 2026-08-08 18:30, posted 2026-07-24; its event page prints 'August 8, 2026 6:30 pm – 10:30 pm' and the category line 'Bar Gig'. Across the 352 S1/S2 rows no start lies between 00:00 and 07:59 (the earliest are three S2 rows at 08:00, ending 17:00; the latest starts are in the 23:00 hour), so Amendment 3 moves no row: the listed date is the night.
- robots.txt (14 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://nicensleazy.com/robots.txt and https://www.nicensleazy.com/robots.txt (each 200, 120 bytes; the bodies were not saved, and the rules are as recorded in the 15 Oct check): 'User-agent: *' with 'Disallow: /wp-admin/' only, and no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every URL read. There was no bot check and no 403 from the venue host: every reply was a 200, except the five terms guesses (404, one identical 35,600-byte 'Page not found' page) and the empty fifth S2 feed page (404 JSON). No saved body holds a challenge or captcha marker.
- Terms (14 Oct 2026 UTC; re-checked 18 Oct 2026 on the saved bodies under the 16 Oct readings): none found. The footer on every saved page (home, /hire, /book, the event page and the 404 page) holds only a 'Safe Space Policy' link, the street address and a site design-and-build credit; there is no '©', no 'All rights reserved', and no terms or privacy link. A scan of all 22 saved bodies for ©, copyright, reproduc, republish, personal use, non-commercial, all rights reserved, crawl, automated, 'terms of', 'terms and', 'without permission' and privacy found nothing; the only 'scrap' hits are 'scrappy' and a song title in event descriptions. The page sitemap lists nine pages (/, /gigs, /clubs, /news, /safe-space-policy, /hire, /bar, /home, /book) and no terms page; of these only the home page, /hire and /book were read. Checked: https://www.nicensleazy.com/ (footer); https://www.nicensleazy.com/hire; https://www.nicensleazy.com/book; https://www.nicensleazy.com/wp-sitemap-posts-page-1.xml; https://www.nicensleazy.com/terms (404); https://www.nicensleazy.com/terms-of-use (404); https://www.nicensleazy.com/terms-of-service (404); https://www.nicensleazy.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://www.nicensleazy.com/legal (404)
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress with its own theme (wp-content/themes/nns) and The Events Calendar plugin (tribe REST API, tribe_venue and tribe_events_cat sitemaps); the footer credits an independent designer and studio. It is not a site-builder: not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly, and no saved body has 'ROCKHOUSE' or 'etix'. 'fatsoma' occurs only in the website (ticket) field of 3 S2 rows (www.fatsoma.com 2, fatso.ma 1); Fatsoma sells those tickets and does not host the site. Ticket links on the 285 counted rows go to many outside sellers (Skiddle 38, RA 33, Ticket Tailor 15, See Tickets 15 plus 5 on promoters' See Tickets subdomains, Tickets Scotland 13, Eventbrite 20, Humanitix 9 and others; one RA and one Eventbrite link are written with a leading space); 84 rows have no link.
- Operator: not identified. No saved page names an owner or company: there is no company name, registration line or © notice, only the address (421 Sauchiehall St, Glasgow G2 3LG). The booker is in-house: /hire says 'We regularly program gigs, film screenings, club nights, after parties' and gives the venue's own gig-booking address (gigs@nicensleazy.com) and a separate address for club nights; no booking company is named. A 2011 press interview (https://www.theskinny.co.uk/music/interviews/20-years-of-sleaze, saved 14 Oct) describes in-house programmers booking the gigs and names no owner company. Presenters named in the counted rows' titles and descriptions ('presents', 'presented by'): PCL ('PCL Presents', a Glasgow promoter) 14 S1 rows (13 Basement, plus the 9 May all-dayer 'PCL X NICE N SLEAZY', placed in Bar) and 0 S2 rows, so 13 of the Basement's 172 counted rows and 1 of the Bar's 107 (one S2 Basement row, 20 Sep, links its tickets to PCL's See Tickets subdomain but its text names no presenter; with it PCL would be 14 of 172); Slay Sessions 6 (S1: Basement 4, Bar 2); Imposter 4; Yupty 4; SMC 3; A-TØN 3; every other presenter 1 or 2. No company presents more than half of any room's counted rows, so there is no outside house booker (Amendment 14), and the venue is its own cluster. No row's text names Live Nation, AEG, DF Concerts, Regular Music or Synergy as presenter; one S2 Basement row (22 Aug, 'DOWNSTAIRS GIG – THE JOY HOTEL') links its tickets to livenation.co.uk, two link Eventim UK and one links Universe with a Ticketmaster referral, and none of these is the venue's own ticketing. No public source read names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake or house booker. §1 passes under Amendment 14, with the owner recorded as not identified (reading 2). Under the literal reading (descriptive only), PCL and the other named presenters become operators of the rooms they present; none of them is among the presenters named in the SWG3 copies read (settle check, 18 Oct), so as far as presenters go Glasgow keeps two clusters there, but the literal reading drops SWG3 itself (Live Nation presents one Warehouse row), and Nice'n'Sleazy then fails §5 under it.
- Rooms: two, from the event categories, a field of its own (Amendment 14): Bar (the ground-floor room; titles call it 'UPSTAIRS') and Basement (the downstairs room). Rows with both categories go to the first one listed, which is Bar in the feed on all three (S1: 4 Apr 'FESTIVAL – HOUSEGUEST', 9 May all-dayer; S2: 11 Jul 'FESTIVAL – SHINEFEST'). Rows with no room category (S1 3, S2 3; one S2 title says 'UPSTAIRS', which is title text only) go to the house room, which is named after the building and stays a pool room. The category field decides the room even where the title names the other one: 4 counted rows do (S1: 3 'UPSTAIRS GIG' titles in Basement, on 12 Apr, 15 Apr and 18 Jun; S2: 1 'BASEMENT GIG' title in Bar, on 19 Sep). Placed by title instead, Bar would be 54 / 55 and Basement 91 / 79, so both stay test rooms. Capacity: Bar 130 ('Our ground floor event space has a capacity of 130'; 'Bar CAPACITY : 130'), Basement 200 ('The capacity of the downstair event space at Nice N Sleazy is 200'; 'VENUE CAPACITY : 200'), both on https://www.nicensleazy.com/hire; no figure for the house room.
- Coverage (recounted 18 Oct 2026 from the 8 saved feed pages): S1 148, S2 137 for the venue, exact as published in the feed on 14 Oct 2026 UTC (182 and 170 distinct event ids, equal to the feed's totals). By room: Bar 51 / 56, Basement 94 / 78, house 3 / 3, so Bar and Basement are test rooms. With the two-room rows left out of both rooms, as in the 15 Oct record, Bar is 49 / 55. Removed by Amendment 4 title words: S1 34 (karaoke 15, class 12, open mic 7), S2 33 (open mic 14, class 13, karaoke 4, quiz 1, comedy 1). The 'class' rows are a weekly vinyl open-decks night titled 'SPIN CLASS' (S1 11, S2 13) and one band gig, 'UPSTAIRS GIG – MIDDLE CLASS GUILT + SMUJ + NORMAL SERVICE' (7 May), which the title rule removes. No title is empty and none is marked canceled, postponed or moved; no row names another building, so no row is off-site. One S2 Basement act is listed twice on 27 Aug (19:00 and 19:30 rows); it is counted twice as listed (Basement S2 77 if merged), and the run counts it once (Amendment 12). The feed lists published events only, so a canceled show that was taken down does not show, and one left up without a mark would count. Because of the site's 06:00 day cut-off, a row starting at exactly 00:00 or between 05:00 and 05:59 on 1 Apr would fall outside the S1 query; no row in either query starts before 08:00, so none is expected. The smallest margin is Bar S1, 31 above 20.
- §5: The Glasgow metro (OECD-EU functional urban area) now has SWG3 (settled eligible on 18 Oct 2026; another cluster: SWG3 Ltd operates it, and no company is shown presenting more than half of any of its rooms' counted rows: DF Concerts is shown on 17 of the Poetry Club's 83, which is 17 of the 30 rows with a readable presenter line, with bounds 17 to 69 of 83 because 45 rows have no page capture), so §5 passes. The two give each other §5: SWG3's own §5 rests on Nice'n'Sleazy (`settle-swg3-tv.md`), so both are listed or neither is. No saved Nice'n'Sleazy body names SWG3, and the SWG3 check names no Nice'n'Sleazy company, so no operator is shown to join the two; with this owner not identified, a shared owner can be neither shown nor ruled out (reading 2). Of the other Glasgow venues checked, these are out: The Garage (bot check, reading 5), Hug & Pint (Music Glue), Òran Mór and Saint Luke's (terms), Broadcast and Mono (§4). §6: Glasgow is not in the Minneapolis-St Paul metro, and Nice'n'Sleazy is neither Hideout nor Black Cat (Amendment 11).
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.nicensleazy.com 23 and nicensleazy.com 2 (robots.txt and the redirect of /), all on 14 Oct 2026 UTC between 20:49 and 21:06, except /book at 22:19; web.archive.org 0. Also www.theskinny.co.uk 2 (robots.txt and the interview, 21:06 UTC). Source: requests.log. The 15 Oct record counts 25 for the venue, which matches.

### SWG3 (`gla-swg3-*`, Glasgow)
- Source: The venue's own /events/ listing on its self-hosted Umbraco site. Each card (article.card--event) gives the day and month-year, a start time (span.time), a separate room field (span.space) and the title (h4). Each event page gives the date, time and room (details__date) and a presenter line ('X presents', b-event-header h5). The live listing shows coming events only and past event pages answer 410 Gone, so S1/S2 rows come from web.archive.org copies of the own listing; event-page copies give only the presenter. Rows come only from listings (every event-page copy's show is also on a listing). The /events/?eventType= filters are Gigs / Clubs / Arts, not rooms.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://swg3.tv/events (or /events/) for ts 20260308045904, 20260312065642, 20260331181651, 20260414103148, 20260515073039, 20260523204731, 20260609092942, 20260728173814, 20260824234802, 20260915024758, 20261001053218 (11 listing copies, 154-214 cards each); https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://swg3.tv/events/2026/<month>/<slug>/ (120 event-page copies, for presenters); the 15 May copy saved in scouting before the check (20260515082808, same content as 20260515073039, not used twice). Live pages during the check: https://swg3.tv/, /events/, /events/2026/july/, /site-map/, /privacy-policy/, /explore/faqs/, /explore/about-us/, /explore/our-vision-plus-model/, /explore/venue-hire-spaces/, 24 live event pages dated Oct-Dec 2026 (date check and presenter sample) and 3 past event pages (410).
- Dates checked: On the live site the card date matches the event page's show date on 3 pages: Chameleons, card 2026-10-20 = 'Tuesday 20 October 2026'; Melanie C, card 2026-10-20 = 'Tue 20 Oct'; Grace Gachot, card 2026-10-17 = 'Saturday 17 October 2026'. In the 11 listing copies every card's weekday agrees with the 2026 calendar (0 mismatches, skeptic's recount). Every counted start is 10:00-23:00 local, so the 00:01-04:59 rule (Amendment 3) never applies. These are show dates (Europe/London). The 20260824234802 copy is 25 Aug in local time (00:48 BST), and the 10-01 copy lists from 19 Sep, so it shows the site as of about 19 Sep.
- robots.txt (15-16 Oct 2026): allowed. https://swg3.tv/robots.txt: 'User-agent: * / Disallow: /umbraco / Disallow: /wwwroot / Sitemap: https://swg3.tv/site-map/'; no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed /events/ and every page read; web.archive.org allowed.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): copyright-only. The footer reads only '© 2026 SWG3 FAQs Accessibility Privacy Policy' (a bare notice, which Amendment 14 says is not terms). There is no terms link and the sitemap has no terms page; the privacy policy covers data, marketing and cookies only, with no use-licence or copying clause. The skeptic scanned all saved swg3.tv bodies and all 131 copies: no copy, reproduce, republish, personal-use, scraping or permission clause and no 'terms' text. Checked: https://swg3.tv/ (footer); https://swg3.tv/site-map/; https://swg3.tv/privacy-policy/; https://swg3.tv/explore/faqs/; https://swg3.tv/terms (404); https://swg3.tv/terms-of-use/ (404); https://swg3.tv/terms-and-conditions/ (404); https://swg3.tv/terms-of-service/ (404); https://swg3.tv/legal/ (404)
- Platform: self-hosted Umbraco CMS (UmbracoForms assets). Cloudflare serves only as a CDN (email-decode script); no challenge was met live, and none of the 131 copies is a challenge, captcha, 403 or error page. No Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Webflow, Weebly, WordPress, Shopify or 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' marker. The venue's official ticket partner is Ticketmaster ('SWG3's official ticket partner is Ticketmaster.', https://swg3.tv/explore/faqs/); cards also link TicketWeb, Skiddle, Eventbrite and Universe. These are ticket vendors, not sources, owners or bookers.
- Operator (§1, Amendment 14): Owners: 'Douglas House Security (DHS) and Yardworks Ltd are owners of the site and lease the properties to the charity' (Clydeside Initiative for Arts Ltd), and 'SWG3 Ltd looks after events, bars, and day-to-day operations' (https://swg3.tv/explore/our-vision-plus-model/). SWG3 Ltd also runs its own club series, 'SWG3 Presents' (https://swg3.tv/explore/about-us/); the venue names no outside house booker. No public source names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake or house booker. Outside presenters, from the presenter lines of the 76 counted rows that have a picked event-page copy (199 counted rows in all): DF Concerts presents 23 of the 199 (The Poetry Club 17, SWG3 Warehouse 3, SWG3 TV Studio 2, SWG3 Galvanizers 1); 4TF: For The Fans 4, Belfast Brunch Co 4, Meatopia 3, Live Republic 3, 432 (as '432' and '432 Presents') 3, FLY 2, and every other company 1 (Live Nation 1, a SWG3 Warehouse row on 9 Jun; AEG 1, a SWG3 Galvanizers row on 24 May). 2 counted rows whose presenter line is only a private person's name are not written. By room: The Poetry Club DF 17 of 83 counted rows (20%); 31 have a page copy, 30 with a readable company, so 17 of 30; bounds over all 83 are 17..69, and 45 Poetry Club rows have no page capture at all, so no archive copy can close them. SWG3 Galvanizers DF 1, AEG 1 of 16 page rows (36 counted); SWG3 Warehouse DF 3, Live Nation 1 of 11 page rows (32 counted); SWG3 TV Studio DF 2 of 4 page rows (25 counted). No company is shown presenting more than half of any room's counted S1 and S2 shows, so none is a house booker and none joins a cluster. The skeptic corrected the settle report on this point: its sentence that DF is, on the evidence, the Poetry Club's house booker goes beyond the data, since Amendment 14 needs one company presenting more than half of the counted shows and the shown count is 17 of 83; the 17 of 30 share covers only rows with a picked page, which may not be a random sample. A descriptive ticket-link check (not a presenter record) points the same way: 11 of the 17 DF page rows carry a TicketWeb 'pl=gigsinscotland' link, 0 of the 14 other page rows do, and only 4 of the 52 Poetry Club rows without a picked page do. DF Concerts is listed as 'D.F. Concerts Limited, Scotland' in Live Nation's Exhibit 21.1 (FY2018 10-K, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1335258/000133525819000024/lyv-20181231xex211.htm) with no ownership percentage, so whether it counts as Live Nation is not settled; it does not decide §1 either way. §1 passes (lead reading).
- Rooms: the span.space field of each card, separate from the title (Amendment 1, reading 7). Values: The Poetry Club, SWG3 Galvanizers, SWG3 Warehouse, SWG3 TV Studio, SWG3 Galvanizers Yard, SWG3 Zinc Bar (on site) and 'Full Complex'. 'Full Complex' names the whole site, not a room, so its rows go to the house room `gla-swg3-house`, as YES's 'Full Building' row does (the settle output keeps them as their own pool room). The 2 cards with no room (1 counted S1 row) also go to the house room. A show listed under two slugs (28 May, SWG3 Warehouse; never both in one copy) is one row; a show whose page says 11 Jul and whose later cards say 24 Sep takes the latest card (S2 either way); a show moved from TV Studio (page) to Galvanizers (later card) takes the latest card. Capacity: Galvanizers Yard 'capable of hosting up to 4000 people', TV Studio 'capable of holding up to 1000 people', The Poetry Club 'Capable of holding 125 people' (https://swg3.tv/explore/venue-hire-spaces/); no figure for SWG3 Galvanizers (floor area only, '6000 square feet'), SWG3 Warehouse or SWG3 Zinc Bar, which are "size unknown" (Amendment 8).
- Coverage: settled on 17-18 Oct 2026 from saved copies, with no network request, after web.archive.org stopped the 16-17 Oct check: that check had read only the 15 May copy and counted S1 >=50, S2 35. The union of the 11 listing copies, keyed by event slug with the latest copy winning, gives S1 126, S2 73 for the building, lower bounds. By room: The Poetry Club 50 / 33, SWG3 Galvanizers 21 / 15, SWG3 Warehouse 24 / 8, SWG3 TV Studio 17 / 8, SWG3 Galvanizers Yard 8 / 7, SWG3 Zinc Bar 0 / 1, house room 6 / 1 ('Full Complex' 5 / 1 and 1 S1 row with no room). The Poetry Club meets coverage by itself and is a test room; every other room is pool. 54 rows removed by the Amendment 4 word 'bingo', all 'Bongo's Bingo' (S1 29, S2 25); no other word hit and no empty title. No row is marked canceled or postponed and none is off-site. 10 shows were listed and then left out of a later listing before their date, with no cancel mark; all are counted. The settle report found 9; the skeptic found a 10th (a Poetry Club S2 show on 22 Sep, on the 07-28, 08-25 and 09-15 listings but not on the 10-01 listing, which still lists 19-22 Sep shows), and its count is used. Without them: The Poetry Club 48 / 32, SWG3 Galvanizers 20 / 14, SWG3 Warehouse 23 / 7, SWG3 TV Studio 16 / 7, SWG3 Galvanizers Yard 7 / 7, building 120 / 69. Shows announced after the last copy before their date, and later cancellations, cannot be seen; the widest gap is 10 Jun to 30 Jul (no listing copy read between 06-09 and 07-28; the unread 07-25 copy can add only shows on 25-30 Jul). The skeptic recounted every row and matched all 253 rows (199 counted, 54 bingo) to their cited copies. Counted rows include non-music items that carry no Amendment 4 word (Meatopia food days, World Cup Scotland FanParks screenings, Belfast Brunch Co shows, a street-art tour), which count under the word rule; The Poetry Club's same-night pairs are a 19:00 gig and a 23:00 club night with different titles. Two sessions with one title on one day count as listed: Belfast Brunch Co at 14:00 and 19:00 (SWG3 Galvanizers, 19 Apr, 28 Jun and 23 Aug) and 'Dyke Just Do It' at 13:30 and 21:00 (SWG3 Warehouse, 6 Jun); in the run, Amendment 12 counts one unit per act and night.
- §5: Glasgow's other venue that meets §1-4 is Nice'n'Sleazy (`gla-nicensleazy-*`), "not used" on §5 alone in `candidates.md` and re-admitted on §5 on 18 Oct 2026 because SWG3 became eligible; SWG3 and Nice'n'Sleazy give each other §5, so it holds only if both are listed. They are two clusters: no saved Nice'n'Sleazy body names SWG3, the SWG3 copies name no Nice'n'Sleazy company, and no company named as presenter in the SWG3 copies is named as presenter in Nice'n'Sleazy's counted rows (the Nice'n'Sleazy re-admission check, 18 Oct). The other Glasgow venues are not used (The Garage: bot check; Òran Mór and Saint Luke's: terms; Hug & Pint: Music Glue; Mono, Broadcast and Stereo: §4; Barrowland Ballroom: no usable copies). Under the literal reading of Amendment 1, Live Nation (1 SWG3 Warehouse row) and AEG (1 SWG3 Galvanizers row) are operators of rooms of this building, so SWG3 drops on §1 there (descriptive only); if DF Concerts is read as Live Nation, it is an operator of four rooms as well.
- Requests during the eligibility check: swg3.tv 48 (requests.log: 4 on 15 Oct, 2 on 16 Oct at 21:29 UTC, then 32 by the check and 10 by its skeptic on 16 Oct 22:56-23:05 UTC; 2 of the 48 are robots.txt reads), web.archive.org 2 naming swg3.tv in scouting on 16 Oct UTC (a CDX query at 21:25 and the 15 May copy at 21:28), plus 1 CDX refusal logged at 20:17 UTC without sending, after the archive's 429; www.sec.gov 1 (the Exhibit 21.1 above, 16 Oct 23:00 UTC). Source: requests.log. Settle (17-18 Oct 2026): web.archive.org 133 naming swg3.tv, sent by the prefetch on 17 Oct 19:59-21:05 UTC (22:59 on 17 Oct to 00:05 on 18 Oct server time): 2 CDX queries, 11 listing copies and 120 event-page copies, all 200. The settle and its check sent no request.

### The Lexington (`lon-lexington`, London)
- Source: The venue's own HTML listing https://www.thelexington.co.uk/events.php on a custom PHP site: one grid-item block per show with a link to event.php?id=N, the title, the date ('Saturday, April 04, 19:30', no year), a GIG or CLUB label and a ticket link. The live page lists coming shows only (86 rows on 16 Oct 2026 UTC). The event.php?id=N pages stay online for past shows, but their ids are not in date order and an unknown id returns the listing, so an exact count from them would take several hundred reads. S1/S2 were therefore read from web.archive.org copies of the same own page. thelexington.co.uk and www.thelexington.co.uk serve the same page.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://www.thelexington.co.uk/events.php for ts 20260305124644, 20260516134732, 20260607132411, 20260807091649, and https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://thelexington.co.uk/events.php for ts 20260312000519, 20260418003644, 20260513190136, 20260715160009 (8 of the 11 captures, 5 Mar-7 Aug 2026, that the CDX index https://web.archive.org/cdx/search/cdx?url=thelexington.co.uk/events.php&from=20260301&to=20260930&filter=statuscode:200 lists). Also the live https://www.thelexington.co.uk/event.php?id=3800 (read 16 Oct 2026 23:12 UTC, 17 Oct server time; one S1 row the copies lack). The scouting check (16 Oct, live) also read /, /events.php, /bookings.php, 27 event.php?id= pages (every 16th id from 3300 to 3684, plus 3500 and 3685), 6 more event pages read by the record's skeptic (3700, 3730, 3760, 3800, 3840, 3870) and the venue's tech spec PDF. Apart from id 3800, none of the live event pages was used for counting.
- Dates checked: Event pages give show dates with set times: id=3500 'Saturday, April 04, 19:30' with set times 8.15pm/9.15pm and a dice link to '4th-apr'; id=3636 'Saturday, September 05, 19:30' with a dice link to '5th-sep'; id=3588 'Saturday, June 20, 19:30', set times 7.45pm/9pm; id=3685's page text says 'Saturday 17 October 2026', the same as its date line. These are show dates, not post dates. The listing gives no year: each row's year is the one year whose weekday matches the listed weekday near the capture date (all 894 listing rows of the 8 copies resolved; the settle skeptic got the same). The 3800 row (17 Jun 15:00) agrees with its page ('Wednesday, June 17, 15:00'), and 8 random rows checked by the skeptic agree with their copies on date, time and title. Starts run from 15:00 to 23:00 (club nights 23:00), so the 00:01-04:59 rule (Amendment 3) never applies. id=3396 shows 'Tuesday, November 30, 00:00', PHP's zero date, an undated template row outside S1/S2.
- robots.txt (15-16 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://thelexington.co.uk/robots.txt (15 Oct) and https://www.thelexington.co.uk/robots.txt (16 Oct) both answer 404, so every path is allowed; polite.py allowed every page read. web.archive.org allowed.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026 UTC; re-checked by the settle skeptic on the 8 saved copies): none-found. No terms, privacy or copyright text on any page and no '©' notice; the only footer links are Instagram and Facebook. No text forbids copying or limits use to personal viewing. The record's skeptic found no ©, copyright, terms, privacy or personal-use text in any saved body (home, events, bookings, the 404 pages and the saved event pages). Checked: https://www.thelexington.co.uk/ (footer); https://www.thelexington.co.uk/events.php; https://www.thelexington.co.uk/bookings.php; https://www.thelexington.co.uk/terms (404); https://www.thelexington.co.uk/terms-of-use (404); https://www.thelexington.co.uk/terms-of-service (404); https://www.thelexington.co.uk/terms-and-conditions (404); https://www.thelexington.co.uk/legal (404); https://www.thelexington.co.uk/privacy.php (404); https://www.thelexington.co.uk/about.php (404)
- Platform: custom PHP site (events.php, event.php?id=, bookings.php), not a site builder: no Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly markers, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer in any live page or archive copy. Fatsoma appears only as one row's outside ticket link. No bot check and no 403: every venue request answered 200 or 404 (an unknown event id returns the listing), and none of the 8 copies is a challenge or error page; each is a full events.php listing.
- Operator (§1): owner not identified as one company. Companies House lists two active pub and bar companies (SIC 56302) at the venue's address, 96-98 Pentonville Road, N1 9JB: AD ASTRA BARS LTD (13458253, incorporated 2021) and BOURBON LIMITED (05271413, incorporated 2004) (https://find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk/advanced-search/get-results?registeredOfficeAddress=N1+9JB&status=active). On both PSC pages (https://find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk/company/13458253/persons-with-significant-control and /company/05271413/persons-with-significant-control) the only active controller is a private owner (75% or more of 13458253; more than 50% but less than 75% of 05271413). Neither has a corporate PSC, so no Live Nation or AEG stake is disclosed. The records do not say which of the two runs the venue. One web search named no owner; no news search was made. House booker: the venue itself. Its bookings page sends hire and gig bookings to its own @thelexington.co.uk address ('The Lexington is a 200 capacity live music venue - if you'd like to book the space please get in touch for hire details and availability', https://www.thelexington.co.uk/bookings.php). Its tech spec (v2.9, Feb 2025, https://thelexington.co.uk/pdfs/Lexington%20Tech%20Spec%20v2.92.pdf) names an in-house Live Booking Manager and tells promoters to 'sort out your own ticketing', so there is no house ticketing. No company presents more than half of the counted rows: the only listing title with 'X present(s)' is Treacherous Cretins (1 of the 193 counted S1+S2 rows, 25 May). Ticket links go to many sellers, dice.fm most. One S1 row (18 Apr) links to axs.com (AEG's ticketing) and one S2 row (3 Sep) to ticketweb.uk (a Ticketmaster brand). Under Amendment 14 a ticket link names no presenter, and 2 rows of 193 decide nothing. The settle report said no S1/S2 row links to Live Nation or Ticketmaster; its skeptic corrected that, and the correction is used here. On the live listing of 16 Oct (after S2), 2 of the 86 coming rows link to livenation.co.uk event pages. Blackstar Promotions and DIY Magazine are named as presenters on two single event pages read while scouting; no S1/S2 title names Live Nation, Blackstar Promotions or DIY Magazine. So the operator is the venue, and it is its own cluster; §1 passes under reading 2. The eligibility record had left §1 'unclear'; its skeptic settled it from the company records and the tech spec, and that reading is used here. Under the literal reading of Amendment 1, Treacherous Cretins (`presented_by` on 1 row) is an operator of the room; it is neither Live Nation nor AEG and has no other room in `venues.csv`, so nothing changes.
- Rooms: one (the upstairs music room; the ground-floor bar is not a stage). The listing has no room field. Its only filter, ?type=all|gigs|clubs, and the GIG/CLUB label (grid-item / grid-item-club classes) give an event type, not a room, so every row goes to the one room (Amendment 12, one room when `building` is empty). The ground-floor bar rows are in the same building, so they are not off-site.
- Coverage: S1 103, S2 90, lower bounds, counted after Amendment 4. Settled on 17-18 Oct 2026 from saved copies after web.archive.org stopped the 16-17 Oct check. The archive's second 429 came at 16 Oct 21:38 UTC, before this venue's first read that night, so the scouting check could only sample live event pages. It verified 7 S1 / 1 S2 rows, and its skeptic 9 / 3, which left §4 open. The 8 copies were fetched on 17 Oct 21:05-21:09 UTC (00:05-00:09 on 18 Oct server time) and counted offline. Each copy lists from its capture date to Dec 2026 or later, so no night of 1 Apr-30 Sep lies outside every copy. Rows were joined by event id, the latest copy's fields winning (14 shows changed date or time between copies), with one more row from the saved event.php?id=3800 page (17 Jun, a ground-floor bar row, the only S1/S2 night the copies lack): 213 rows in `shows.csv`. Listed 111 / 102. Removed: in S1, 7 'quiz' rows (the weekly pop quiz) and 1 POSTPONED row (18 Jun, canceled=1); in S2, 11 'quiz' rows and 1 'market' row (a 20 Sep album launch with a label market). No row is off-site or untitled. Counted rows include club nights (CLUB label), 9 ground-floor bar and DJ opening-hours rows (S1 2, S2 7) and 2 World Cup football nights (15 and 19 Jul), which no Amendment 4 word removes; without them the room has 101 / 81. 5 counted rows (S1 3, S2 2: 20 Apr, 23 Apr, 9 May, 25 Jul, 30 Sep) are missing from a later copy dated before their night and carry no cancel mark; 2 of them look like second listings of another row's show (9 May, 25 Jul). They are kept; without them the room has 100 / 88. Not exact: shows announced after the last copy before their night are missed (nights 1-16 Apr, 19 Apr-12 May, 14-15 May, 17 May-6 Jun, 8 Jun-14 Jul, 16 Jul-6 Aug and above all 8 Aug-30 Sep, since there is no capture after 7 Aug), and later cancellations are not seen. The 3 unused captures (20260310153625, 20260607013739, 20260711105215) could only add rows. The settle skeptic's own parser gives the same 103 / 90 and the same 213 rows. The room is a test room (Amendment 12), and the eligibility record's sampled 7 / 1 is superseded.
- §5: London has one other eligible venue of another cluster, The Underworld (Glendola Leisure; S1 74, S2 60, not exact), which `candidates.md` had "not used" on §5 alone while The Lexington was undecided and which was re-admitted on §5 on 18 Oct 2026 (its section below). Each passes §5 only through the other, so The Underworld comes back with The Lexington (its section and row are written separately). §6: London is not in Study 1.
- Capacity: 200, published by the venue (https://www.thelexington.co.uk/bookings.php).
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.thelexington.co.uk 46 (39 in the 16 Oct scouting check, robots.txt included; the record's 38 leaves out the robots.txt read at 21:40 UTC; 7 by the record's skeptic: about.php and 6 event pages), thelexington.co.uk 5 (4 in the 15 Oct first sweep, 1 tech spec PDF by the skeptic), find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk 3 (the N1 9JB search and the two PSC pages), web.archive.org 9 (1 CDX query and 8 copies, 17 Oct 21:05-21:09 UTC, 30 s apart); the settle itself made none (requests.log)

### The Underworld (`lon-underworld`, London)
- Source: The venue's own WordPress site. Its Yoast sitemaps (https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/sitemap_index.xml -> event-sitemap.xml, event-sitemap2.xml, event-sitemap3.xml; 2,085 /event/<slug>/ URLs, each with a lastmod) list every event page, past ones included. Slugs carry the show's day and month (e.g. '-1st-apr-the-underworld-london-tickets'). Each event page has a date header ('Wednesday 1st April 2026'), the title, a 'Presented by' field and the doors time. The home page lists coming shows as cards with full dates. The one web.archive.org copy of the home page that was read (9 May 2026) is used for dates and cancellation marks of rows from 8 May on. Ticket buttons link to DICE, which is not a source. Re-admitted on §5 on 18 Oct 2026, after a second check under the 16 Oct readings, because The Lexington (London, another cluster) was settled eligible on 18 Oct; `candidates.md` had it "not used" on §5 alone.
- Read: https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/sitemap_index.xml, /event-sitemap.xml, /event-sitemap2.xml, /event-sitemap3.xml, /page-sitemap.xml, / (home), /about/, /privacy-policy/, /wp-json/wp/v2/types, the terms paths below, and 30 event pages (22 by the first check and 8 by its second check, all on 16 Oct 2026 UTC). Archive: https://web.archive.org/web/20260509044458id_/https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/ and two CDX lists (https://web.archive.org/cdx/search/cdx?url=theunderworldcamden.co.uk/&from=20260301&to=20261015&filter=statuscode:200, 11 home copies from 1 Mar to 31 Aug 2026; and the same with url=theunderworldcamden.co.uk/event/&matchType=prefix&collapse=urlkey, 77 rows). The other 10 home copies were not read; no archive request was made in this re-check.
- Dates checked: These are show dates. On all 27 opened event pages with a dated slug, the header date (all in 2026) matches the slug's day and month (e.g. 'Sunday 28th June 2026' for a '-28th-jun-' slug; 'Friday 25th September 2026' for '-25th-sep-'). The year is the one nearest to the lastmod; across the counted slug-dated rows the lastmod lies 23 days before to 29 days after the slug date, apart from the rows taken out under Coverage. Three undated slugs were dated from their own pages: Arch Enemy 'Monday 10th August 2026', Nothing But Thieves 'Saturday 19th September 2026' and Incineration Festival 'Saturday 2nd May 2026' (multi-venue, not counted). The 9 May copy moves five rows: the plain slugs 'camden-rocks-club-4th-jul', '-11th-jul' and '-18th-jul' (each also has a '-2' twin dated 4, 11 and 18 Jul) are the nights of 1, 8 and 15 Aug (cards 'Saturday 1st August 2026' etc.; lastmod 2, 9 and 16 Aug), so they are not duplicates; the ACAB x Haymaker row (slug 13 Jun) is 6 Jun; and the Scardust row, whose slug says 10 Mar, is 'Tuesday 26th May 2026' (lastmod 26 May). It is the only row with a lastmod in the periods and a slug date outside them. Times are local wall-clock values (Europe/London). The 28 opened pages of counted rows give a first time between 5:30 pm and 11:00 pm, the club nights 'Doors open 11:00 PM' with a 3:00 AM curfew, and no card in the 9 May copy or the 16 Oct home page gives a time from 00:01 to 04:59, so Amendment 3 moves no night. The club series fall on their weekdays: 26 Blackout Club rows, all Fridays, and 26 Camden Rocks Club rows, all Saturdays.
- robots.txt (15 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/robots.txt and https://theunderworldcamden.co.uk/robots.txt (200, 186 bytes each; the rules are kept in the fetcher's state): a Yoast block, 'User-agent: *', 'Disallow:' (empty) and a Sitemap line. No Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every URL read, and web.archive.org's robots.txt allows the snapshot and CDX paths used. No bot check and no 403 from the venue host: every reply from www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk was a 200 (40) or a 404 (5, the terms paths), and the one reply from theunderworldcamden.co.uk was a 200.
- Terms (15-16 Oct 2026 UTC; re-checked 18 Oct 2026 from the saved bodies): none found. No saved body from the venue host, nor the 9 May copy, has a terms, use-licence, '©', 'copyright' or 'All rights reserved' text, or any text that forbids copying, reproducing or republishing or limits use to personal viewing. The home page's footer element is empty; outside its event links, the home page links only to the about and search pages, social sites, desertfest.co.uk, the World's End pub and shop sites, DICE ticket links, a playlist short link and browsehappy.com. The about page also links to DICE's own contact and privacy pages; DICE sells the tickets but does not host the site, so its terms are not the site's terms. The privacy policy covers personal data and cookies; its one use of 'copy' is 'Request a copy of that personal data.' The page sitemap lists six pages (home, search-events, privacy-policy, live-streaming, about, production) and no terms page; live-streaming and production were not read. Checked: https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/ (footer); https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/about/; https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/privacy-policy/; https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/terms/ (404); https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/terms-and-conditions/ (404); https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/terms-of-use/ (404); https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/terms-of-service/ (404); https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/legal/ (404); https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/page-sitemap.xml
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress (generator 'WordPress 7.1.3' on the live pages, 'WordPress 6.9.4' in the 9 May copy) with the Yoast SEO plugin and the WordPress REST API. It is not a site-builder: no Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly marker in any of the 44 saved venue-host bodies or the 9 May copy, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer ('rockhouse' and 'etix' occur in none of them). Tickets go through DICE (about page, 'The Underworld Ticketing'), which does not host the site. The 9 May web.archive.org copy is the real home page (title 'The Underworld', 332,840 bytes, 118 distinct dated show cards in its 'All' tab, 8 May 2026 to 7 May 2027), not a bot-check or hosting-check page, so it counts.
- Operator: owned by Glendola Leisure, a private UK pub and bar company. The about page reads 'The venue is itself owned by Glendola Leisure.' The privacy page reads '500 capacity live music venue, and in-house promoters. A part of The Foundation Group Of Companies Limited'. Two press pages were seen as web search results in the first check, not read (no line in requests.log): https://morningadvertiser.co.uk/Article/2017/10/02/Glendola-Leisure-purchases-Handmade-Pubs (Glendola is a privately owned pub and bar company running the World's End and the Underworld) and https://www.hospitalitynet.org/organization/17021895.html (Foundation Group is the group support company for Glendola Leisure (Holdings) Limited). Booking is in-house: the venue names its own in-house promoters, and on the 28 opened pages of counted rows 'Presented by' names The Underworld on 15 (one jointly with 1664 Music; on one more, Gorilla Biscuits 2 Jun, the page text reads 'Smorgasbord NTL and The Underworld proudly present'), Live Nation on 4 (letlive 17 Jun, The Pretty Reckless 1 Jun, Asava 7 Sep, Split Chain 25 Sep), Camden Rocks Festival on 2 (both club nights opened), Gotobeat, Morbid Angle, The Gig Cartel, Heavy Metal Hippie Music Ltd. with Imperiet IV, Action! and North To South Festival on 1 each, and no presenter on 1. Of 134 counted rows, 52 are the two weekly club series and 82 are gigs; 22 of the 26 opened gig pages name a presenter other than Live Nation, so Live Nation can present at most 60 of 134 rows if no club night is its own, under half. Camden Rocks Festival presents both opened club nights (a 'Blackout Club – Camden Rocks Takeover' and a plain 'Camden Rocks Club'); 12 of the 52 club rows are 'Camden Rocks (Fest) Takeover' nights by title, no plain 'Blackout Club' page (20 rows) was opened, and if Camden Rocks Festival presented every club row that would be 52 of 134 (39%). It presents none of the 26 opened gig pages. No company is shown to present more than half of the counted rows, so the house booker is the venue's own (Amendment 14) and Live Nation is an outside minority presenter that joins no cluster. No public source read or seen names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake or house booker for The Underworld, Glendola Leisure or The Foundation Group; the venue's own contact addresses and its ticketing (DICE) show none. §1 passes under Amendment 14. Under the literal reading, Live Nation and every other named presenter become operators of this room, and §1 fails there.
- Rooms: one. The site has no room or stage field and no room filter; the 'Club Nights' and 'Gigs' tabs are event types. The World's End pub upstairs has rows under the same /event/ path (slug tail '-the-worlds-end-camden-london-tickets'), but none is dated in S1 or S2; every counted slug ends '-the-underworld-london-tickets'. Capacity: 500 (https://www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk/about/, 'Capacity: 500'; also the privacy page).
- Coverage: S1 74, S2 60 (S1 48 gigs and 26 club nights; S2 34 gigs and 26 club nights). The three event sitemaps give 81 S1 and 60 S2 slug-dated rows. Taken out: 7 S1 rows that the 16 Oct home page lists as 2027 shows under the same slug (Mr. Punch 24 Apr, three Booze and Glory rows 1-2 May, London Rock Festival 2027, Krawallbrüder 7 May, Britallica 18 Jun; all with lastmod 12 Oct 2026); Atlas (slug 11 Sep), postponed to 'Friday 28th January 2028' on the home page; Cro-Mags 7 Aug, whose live page reads 'CRO-MAGS | CANCELLED'; and Downloaded 30 May, marked 'DOWNLOADED | CANCELLED' in the 9 May copy. Added: Scardust 26 May (S1, from the 9 May copy), Arch Enemy 10 Aug and Nothing But Thieves 19 Sep (S2, from their pages). No slug title and no opened page title has an Amendment 4 word; the undated 'creeper-karaoke' (lastmod 9 Oct) is out by its word. Multi-venue festival rows are not counted: Incineration Festival 2 May (its page lists the Roundhouse, Electric Ballroom, Underworld, Black Heart and The Dev) and Desertfest 2026 days 1-3 (15-17 May, cards in the 9 May copy, pages not read); counted, they would add 4 to S1. Cosmic Void Festival 2026 (18 Sep; its slug names the Underworld, Electric Ballroom and The Black Heart as multiple venues) has a card in the 9 May copy but is no longer in the sitemaps, and is not counted either. Drain (11 Jun) and Immortal Suffering (17 Jul) are on the 9 May copy but no longer in the sitemaps, and are not counted. The counts are not exact, and they are not lower bounds: 106 of the 134 counted rows were not opened after their night, and a show cancelled after the 9 May copy, or before its night in April and early May, would lower them (cancellation marks were seen on 1 of the 30 opened pages (Cro-Mags) and on 2 of the 118 cards of the 9 May copy (Downloaded and Cro-Mags)). Only the opened pages give strict lower bounds, S1 >=15 and S2 >=13. The two weekly club series alone fill every Friday and Saturday of both periods (26 and 26), and no club row was marked canceled anywhere. Corrections to the earlier record: S1 stays 74 but now leaves out Downloaded and takes in Scardust, and lists ACAB x Haymaker on 6 Jun; S2 is 60, not 61 (Cro-Mags), and not about 57, since the three plain Camden Rocks Club slugs of 4, 11 and 18 Jul are the nights of 1, 8 and 15 Aug; Atlas was postponed, not a 2027 show on the same day and month.
- Also checked: The London metro (OECD-EU functional urban area, core city London) has one eligible venue of another cluster, The Lexington (settled eligible on 18 Oct 2026; its companies are controlled by a private owner, and no saved source links them to Glendola Leisure or The Foundation Group), so §5 passes, and the pass is symmetric. London is not in the Minneapolis-St Paul metro, and The Underworld is neither Hideout nor Black Cat (§6, Amendment 11). No bot-check copy was counted: the only archive copy read is a real page.
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.theunderworldcamden.co.uk 45 (3 on 15 Oct 2026 between 19:03 and 19:04 UTC: robots.txt, the page sitemap and the privacy page; 42 on 16 Oct between 21:58 and 22:06 UTC, 17 Oct 00:58-01:06 server time: 34 by the first check (12 site pages and sitemaps and 22 event pages) and 8 event pages by its second check); theunderworldcamden.co.uk 1 (robots.txt, 15 Oct 19:00 UTC); web.archive.org 3 (2 CDX lists and the 9 May home copy, 15 Oct 2026 between 19:01 and 19:03 UTC, before the archive stop). Of these 49 requests, 44 answers were 200 and 5 were 404 (the five terms paths). The 18 Oct re-check sent no request. Source: requests.log.

### Hotel Cafe (`lax-hotelcafe-*`, Los Angeles)
- Source: The venue's own WordPress site (new.hotelcafe.com, with The Events Calendar and Events Calendar Pro). For S2 from 14 Jul, its own events REST feed (/wp-json/tribe/events/v1/events), which gives each row's local start, time zone, venue field and categories. The site took down every event before 14 Jul 2026: the feed returns 0 rows for 1 Apr-30 Jun, and the live past-events list for June shows no events. So S1 and 1-13 Jul come from web.archive.org copies of the venue's own home page. Its month view lists each day's events, and each row carries the post id, title, show time and the event's categories. www.hotelcafe.com is now a splash page that links to new.hotelcafe.com. Its old /tickets/ page says 'THERE ARE NO CURRENT EVENTS'. Neither was used for rows.
- Read: https://new.hotelcafe.com/wp-json/tribe/events/v1/events?start_date=2026-04-01 00:00:00&end_date=2026-06-30 23:59:59 (0 rows) and ?start_date=2026-07-01 00:00:00&end_date=2026-09-30 23:59:59, pages 1 and 2 (69 rows, 14 Jul to 22 Aug); https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://new.hotelcafe.com/ for ts 20260404073627, 20260520032740, 20260613210315 and 20260711165821 (month views of April, May, June and July 2026); https://web.archive.org/web/20260407213312id_/https://www.hotelcafe.com/ (the splash page, not counted); 3 web.archive.org CDX queries that answered, plus 1 that timed out; live https://new.hotelcafe.com/, /info/, /contact/, /faq/, /privacy-policy, /events/list/?eventDisplay=past&tribe-bar-date=2026-06-30, /wp-json/wp/v2/pages and the terms paths below; https://www.hotelcafe.com/ and https://www.hotelcafe.com/tickets/.
- Dates checked: These are show dates. A month-view row sits in a day cell (id 'tribe-events-calendar-day-YYYY-MM-DD') with its show time in `<time datetime="HH:MM">`. The tooltip date of each of the 283 month-view rows in the four copies matches its day cell. Feed rows give start_date in local time with timezone 'America/Los_Angeles' and a matching utc_start_date: the first row starts '2026-07-14 19:00:00', with utc_start_date '2026-07-15 02:00:00'. No feed row is all-day. Of the 36 rows that the 11 Jul copy shows for 14 Jul-1 Aug, 35 have the same date and title in the feed. The 36th (17 Jul, 21:30, Main Stage) is the same night, which the feed lists under a longer title. This corrects the 15 Oct record, which says one row moved from 25 Jul to 20 Aug. In fact the 25 Jul row is in the feed on 25 Jul, and the feed's 20 Aug row with the same billing is a second show. Counted starts run from 18:45 to 22:30, and none falls between 00:01 and 04:59 (Amendment 3).
- robots.txt (14 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://new.hotelcafe.com/robots.txt (200, 219 bytes; the body was not saved, and the rules are as the 15 Oct record gives them) disallows only MJ12bot, Baiduspider and similar named bots. It has nothing for `*` or for an Anthropic agent. https://www.hotelcafe.com/robots.txt answers 404 (allow). polite.py allowed every URL read, and web.archive.org's robots.txt allows the snapshot and CDX paths used. The venue hosts answered only with 200 or 404 (404 for the five terms paths and www's robots.txt). There was no 403 and no bot check. The only 'captcha' string in the saved pages is the validation script of an iContact newsletter form. The Cloudflare strings are the cdnjs.cloudflare.com library links (in 15 of the 26 bodies), Cloudflare's email-obfuscation links and script on /contact/ and /faq/ (/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection), and the static.cloudflareinsights.com analytics beacon (in the 4 archive copies). None is a challenge page; there is no 'Just a moment', cf-chl, Turnstile, reCAPTCHA or hCaptcha string. Every saved HTML page of new.hotelcafe.com (11 live pages and the 4 archive copies) carries `<meta name='robots' content='noindex, nofollow' />`, WordPress's site-wide setting that asks search engines not to index the pages. It is not robots.txt, not terms and not a bot check, and it says nothing about reading or reuse, so it does not decide §3; it is recorded here so that the lead can weigh it.
- Terms (14 Oct 2026 UTC; re-checked 18 Oct 2026 from the saved bodies): none-found. The footer on every page reads only '© 2026 The Hotel Cafe', a bare notice and not terms (Amendment 14). The site's page list (/wp-json/wp/v2/pages) has eight pages: Main Stage Specs, Second Stage Specs, About, CONTACT, PRIVATE EVENTS AND FILMING, ABOUT (/info/), FAQ and Privacy Policy. None is a terms page. The privacy policy is WordPress's default text on cookies, comments and personal data. The FAQ's 'No' to filming is about filming shows in the room, not about the website. None of the 26 saved bodies has text that forbids copying, reproducing or republishing, or that limits use to personal viewing, and none says 'All rights reserved'. Checked: https://new.hotelcafe.com/ (footer); https://new.hotelcafe.com/info/; https://new.hotelcafe.com/contact/; https://new.hotelcafe.com/faq/; https://new.hotelcafe.com/privacy-policy; https://new.hotelcafe.com/wp-json/wp/v2/pages; https://new.hotelcafe.com/terms (404); https://new.hotelcafe.com/terms-of-use (404); https://new.hotelcafe.com/terms-of-service (404); https://new.hotelcafe.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://new.hotelcafe.com/legal (404). The Specs and Private Events pages were not fetched.
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress with the venue's own theme ('hotelcafe' under wp-content/themes) and The Events Calendar (Pro) plugins. It is not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly. 'ROCKHOUSE' and 'etix' occur in none of the 26 saved bodies, so there is no Rockhouse Partners/Etix footer. Tickets go through the venue's own www.hotelcafe.com/tickets/?s=events_view&id=N links: 157 of them in the 20 May copy, and the feed's website field uses the same form. All 4 web.archive.org copies of new.hotelcafe.com/ are the full events page, titled 'Events from <dates> – Hotel Cafe', with 73, 93, 57 and 60 month-view rows. The www.hotelcafe.com copy is the 3,377-byte splash page, the same size as the live one. None is a bot-check page.
- Operator (§1): The Hotel Cafe, run by private owners (two co-owners are named in https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hotel_Caf%C3%A9 and in a Pollstar interview, https://news.pollstar.com/?p=1213540, as cited from search results by the 15 Oct record; neither page was fetched or saved, and requests.log has no line for them). No company record was found, and none was searched. The venue books its own shows. /contact/ says 'We do all of our music Booking through email only.' with in-house addresses, and the FAQ answers 'Can I book bands for you?' with 'No.' Tickets are sold through its own ticket pages. /info/ thanks NIVA and The Live Music Society for support during the 2020-21 closure. The strings 'Live Nation', 'AEG', 'Goldenvoice', 'Bowery', 'Ticketmaster', 'TicketWeb' and 'AXS' occur in none of the 26 saved bodies. The 15 Oct skeptic's searches (Pollstar, Time Out, KTLA, Wikipedia; not saved) found no Live Nation, AEG or Goldenvoice link, and no promoter partner for the planned 2027 move. The titles name these presenters ('X Presents'): a private person's showcase series on 16 of the Main Stage's 102 counted rows; Netflix Is A Joke, Netflix's comedy festival, on 10 Main Stage and 10 Second Stage rows; Sunset Eclectico on 4 Main Stage rows; and other presenters on 1 to 5 rows each, among them LA Playground and the venue itself ('The Hotel Cafe Presents', 22 Aug). No company presents more than half of either room's counted rows, so there is no outside house booker (Amendment 14). The house is the operator, and both stages form one cluster (`lax-hotelcafe`). §1 passes under Amendment 14. Under the literal reading, the named presenters become operators of their rooms. None of them is a Live Nation or AEG company. None is among the presenters recorded for Whisky a Go Go (the venue itself, a music festival and a sponsor pairing), so Los Angeles keeps two clusters.
- Rooms: two test rooms and one pool room, by the event category field (Amendment 1; Amendment 14, rooms). Every month-view row carries its categories as tribe_events_cat-<slug> classes, and every feed row as a categories list. The stage values are 'Main Stage' and 'Second Stage', beside 'Just Announced'. The site's menu has filters for them (All Shows / Main Stage / Second Stage). One counted row has both stages (10 Aug, 'The Last Monday Monday at the Hotel Cafe'). It goes to the Main Stage, which the site lists first (alphabetical). Two counted rows have no stage category: an S1 row on 8 Jun (13 Jun copy) and 'That’s All Folks Fest' on 31 Jul (feed). They go to `lax-hotelcafe-house`, the pool room named after the building, which stays pool whatever it counts. The 15 Oct record put the 31 Jul row on the Second Stage (60), which is why the Second Stage is now 59. Capacity: Main Stage 215 ('Main Stage has a capacity of 215 with seating for around 40 people', https://new.hotelcafe.com/info/; the FAQ says '~200', and the larger published figure is used, Amendment 8); Second Stage 75 ('Second Stage is a more intimate room that holds 75 people', same page); the house room has no figure.
- Coverage: Main Stage S1 >=74, S2 >=28; Second Stage S1 >=114, S2 >=59; house room 1 / 1; building S1 >=189, S2 >=88. Rows are counted at the local date, after the Amendment 4 rules. S1 is the union of the four copies, one row per post id. When an id appears in two copies with different dates, the latest copy's date is used: one row moved from 1 May to 13 Jun and stays in S1, and one moved from 24 Jun to 9 Jul and is counted in S2. No row that an earlier copy shows is missing from a later copy that shows its date. Raw S1 is 197 (Main 75, Second 121, no stage 1). Amendment 4 removes 8 rows, all for 'comedy' (Main 1, Second 7). Netflix Is A Joke rows without the word count, as the scope note in "Notes for compiling" says. Raw S2 is 88 (Main 28, Second 59, no stage 1), and the words remove 0. S2 is 69 feed rows (Main 23, Second 45, no stage 1) for 14 Jul-22 Aug, plus 19 rows for 1-13 Jul from the 11 Jul copy (Main 5, Second 14; no shows on 1-5 Jul). No row is untitled. No row is marked canceled or postponed: the feed status is 'publish' on all 69 rows, no description mentions a cancellation, every schema.org eventStatus in the copies (15 per copy) is EventScheduled, and no title says so. No row is off-site. The feed's venue field is 'Hotel Cafe' on 68 rows and empty on 1 (7 Aug, Main Stage), which counts because it names no other building. The month views have no venue field, and no title names another building. The S1 counts are lower bounds. April rests on the 4 Apr copy, which misses shows added later for 5-25 Apr. On 2 Apr that copy hides 1 of 6 events behind a 'more' link (found_events 6, 5 shown), and it is not counted. May rests on the 20 May copy, which misses later additions for 20-30 May (31 May is also in the 13 Jun copy's grid), and June rests on the 13 Jun copy (28-30 Jun also on the 11 Jul copy). The 69 feed rows are what the site still lists for 14 Jul-30 Sep. The 7 copy rows for 11-13 Jul are advance listings, so a later cancellation would not show in them. The Main Stage reaches 23 in S2 from the feed alone. The venue closed its Cahuenga room after 'The Hotel Cafe Presents: Curtain Call on Cahuenga' on 22 Aug 2026, so no rows fall after 22 Aug. Each stage meets coverage by itself, so both are test rooms of one cluster. `shows.csv` confirms or corrects this (Amendment 12).
- Also checked: Hollywood is in the city of Los Angeles, in the Los Angeles-Long Beach-Anaheim MSA (OMB Bulletin 23-01). That metro now has one eligible venue of another cluster, Whisky a Go Go (West Hollywood, the same MSA; one room, S1 20, S2 61; settled eligible on 18 Oct 2026), so §5 passes. Whisky a Go Go's S1 of 20 is a lower bound at the line. If that venue leaves, Hotel Cafe fails §5 again. Los Angeles is not in the Minneapolis-St Paul metro, and Hotel Cafe is neither Hideout nor Black Cat (§6, Amendment 11). On 15 Oct 2026 the venue was "not used" on §5 alone. A second check under the 16 Oct readings, made from the saved pages with no new request, re-admitted it on §5 on 18 Oct 2026 because Whisky a Go Go became eligible. A refutation check the same day, again from the saved bodies with no new request, recounted every room with its own parser (same counts), confirmed the quotes, the ticket links, the absence of Rockhouse/Etix, Live Nation, AEG and platform strings and of any bot check, and upheld the re-admission.
- Requests during the eligibility check: new.hotelcafe.com 16 (1 of them robots.txt), www.hotelcafe.com 3 (1 of them robots.txt), web.archive.org 9 (4 CDX queries, one of which timed out with no status, and 5 copies). All were made on 14 Oct 2026 between 20:21 and 22:06 UTC. Source: requests.log, 28 lines. The 15 Oct record counts 25; the other 3 are the 15 Oct skeptic's reads of /faq/, www.hotelcafe.com/tickets/ and the WordPress page list. The 18 Oct re-check made no request.

### Whisky a Go Go (`lax-whisky`, Los Angeles)
- Source: The venue's own self-hosted WordPress site. On the live site each show has one dated event page, /tm-event/<slug>/ (the Ticketmaster/TicketWeb events plugin, post type tm_event), listed in the venue's own https://www.whiskyagogo.com/tm_events-sitemap.xml (194 URLs on 16 Oct 2026: 101 coming shows and 93 others, the earliest a 4 Jun 2026 show). The live listing pages (/, /page/2/, /page/3/, /tm-venue/, /tm-attraction/) show only coming shows, and tm_event has no route in /wp-json/. Before that, the site's listing was a WordPress install under /calendar/ with the TicketWeb plugin (/calendar/events/<id>/ pages, which now redirect to the home page). So S1 comes from the live event pages, and S2 from the live event pages plus web.archive.org copies of the venue's own /calendar/ listing (page 1 of 3, 44 rows each). Settled on 17-18 Oct 2026 from saved copies (the settle itself made no request; the archive copies were fetched before it, by the settle prefetch), after web.archive.org stopped the 16-17 Oct check.
- Read: https://www.whiskyagogo.com/tm-event/<slug>/ (35 own event pages, read on 16 Oct 2026 UTC and saved; 34 of them are S1 or S2 shows), https://www.whiskyagogo.com/tm_events-sitemap.xml, the home page and /page/2/, /page/3/, and the pages under Terms; https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://whiskyagogo.com/calendar/ for ts 20260712153655, 20260815073731, 20260819191146 and 20260919031219 (fetched 17 Oct 2026, 21:22-21:23 UTC, 00:22-00:23 on 18 Oct server time, and saved; the settle read the saved bodies). Not used: five copies of https://whiskyagogo.com/ (20260710230132, 20260712144649, 20260802013708, 20260831124343, 20260911172909) are identical 1,314-byte meta-refresh shells pointing to /calendar/, not listings; the scouting copy 20260515065934 of the same page is also 1,314 bytes; the live /calendar/ page read in scouting on 15 Oct (coming shows only). The settle CDX list also has a /calendar/ capture of 20260811193316, and the 15 Oct CDX survey lists S1 /calendar/ captures of 12 Apr, 4 May, 16 May and 18 May 2026; none of these was read.
- Dates checked: These are show dates. Live pages print the date with zone and doors: King's X 'Sat June 13, 2026 PDT (Doors: 6:00 PM)', Dokken 'Sat June 20, 2026 PDT (Doors: 7:00 PM)', Primer 55 'Mon September 28, 2026 PDT (Doors: 6:00 PM)'. The sitemap lastmod is not a show date (King's X lastmod 2026-06-04, show 13 Jun). Six S2 shows are both on a live page and in an archived listing, at the same date: 12, 20, 22 and 28 Jul, 6 Aug and 28 Sep (Primer 55 is in the 15 Aug, 19 Aug and 19 Sep listings at 28 Sep). All 81 counted rows start between 16:30 and 20:00 local time (live pages with -07:00, archive listings in local time with no zone), so Amendment 3 moves no date. Each night has at most one show.
- robots.txt (15 Oct 2026, applied by polite.py on 16 and 17 Oct): allowed. https://www.whiskyagogo.com/robots.txt (the same text at whiskyagogo.com): 'User-agent: * / Disallow: /wp-admin/ / Allow: /wp-admin/admin-ajax.php', a group for AlliAI ('Allow: /') and four Sitemap lines; no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every page read, and web.archive.org's robots.txt allows the snapshot and CDX paths used. No bot check and no 403: every venue-host reply was a 200 or a 404, and no saved live page or archive copy is a check page (the skeptic's 'captcha|challenge' hits are a band name, 'Vertical Challenges').
- Terms (16 Oct 2026; archive copies re-checked 18 Oct 2026 from the saved bodies): copyright-only. The only rights text on the live site is the footer 'Whisky a Go Go © 2026. All Rights Reserved.', a bare notice (Amendment 14); the archived /calendar/ copies have only '© 2026 Whisky a Go Go | West Hollywood. All Rights Reserved.' The footer links no terms or privacy page, and the page sitemap lists none. A keyword scan of the saved home, about, faqs, booking, venue-specs, 404 and event pages found no text that forbids copying, reproducing, republishing or automated reading, or limits use to personal viewing. The archived copies carry an SMS sign-up line whose 'Terms and Privacy Policy' link goes to eepurl.com (Mailchimp's SMS-programme terms, not site terms; not read). Checked: https://www.whiskyagogo.com/ (footer: no terms or privacy link); https://www.whiskyagogo.com/page-sitemap.xml (pages: home, about, booking, contact, employment, faqs, happy-hour, venue-specs; no terms, legal or privacy page); https://www.whiskyagogo.com/terms-of-use/ (404); https://www.whiskyagogo.com/privacy-policy/ (404); https://www.whiskyagogo.com/cookie-policy/ (404); https://www.whiskyagogo.com/faqs/ (no use or copying clause)
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress (Porto theme, WPBakery, All in One SEO Pro) with the Ticketmaster/TicketWeb events plugin; the archived /calendar/ pages are the older TicketWeb plugin on the same site. Footer credit 'SEO for Restaurants by Bar Marketing Basics'. Not Fatsoma, Squarespace, Wix, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer on the live pages or in the archive copies. Tickets are sold through TicketWeb (a Ticketmaster company) and, on one row, eventcartel; they sell tickets only and do not host the listings.
- Operator: owned by private owners, a family (named in a 2023 obituary of the late owner, https://beverlypress.com/2023/11/legendary-club-owner-on-sunset-strip-remembered/, and in other press; the obituary calls him owner of the Sunset Strip's Rainbow Bar & Grill and Whisky a Go Go, and the LA Times, as quoted in search snippets, expected his son to carry on the business). No public source names a current company owner. Booking is in-house: https://www.whiskyagogo.com/booking/ gives only the venue's own office@ and booking@whiskyagogo.com addresses, and the FAQ sends bands there ('I'd like to play at your venue! How do I book my band?', https://www.whiskyagogo.com/faqs/); no outside booker is named. Outside presenters: the live pages' presenter field names the venue itself ('Whisky A Go Go') on 8 counted rows, 'GIRLACTIC MUSIC FEST' on 1 (6 Aug), and the 16 Jun Ultimate Jam Night page reads 'Presented by Red Rock Lager & Hal Sparks' (1); the archive listings name no presenter. No company presents more than half of the counted rows, so there is no outside house booker (Amendment 14), and the venue is its own cluster. Live Nation appears only in the description of an embedded YouTube video (a Live Nation channel stream of a past performance), not as owner, booker or presenter. Web searches in the first check (Live Nation or AEG booking or acquisition) and in the review (Live Nation, AEG or Goldenvoice acquisition or booking; a new owner or sale in 2025-2026; a talent buyer or outside promoter in 2026) found no Live Nation or AEG owner, stake, house booker or talent-buying deal. Under the literal reading (Amendment 14, last item), GIRLACTIC MUSIC FEST and Red Rock Lager & Hal Sparks go into `presented_by`; neither is Live Nation or AEG, and neither presents a row at a room in `venues.csv`.
- Rooms: one. The site has one venue term (/tm-venue/whisky-a-go-go/ in the tm_venues sitemap), the venue-details field is empty on every live page, the archive listings have no venue-name field, and there is no room filter. The first and second floors are one room. The FAQ mentions 'a small room for meetings ... which holds about 25 people' for rentals; it has no listed shows. The settle's rows, venue_id `whiskyagogo-com:house`, all go to `lax-whisky`; the venue is not split, so there is no house pool room. Capacity: no figure published (https://www.whiskyagogo.com/venue-specs/ gives audio and backline only; the 25-person meeting room is not the show room), so the room is "size unknown" (Amendment 8).
- Coverage: S1 >=20, S2 >=61 (one room; lower bounds), settled on 17-18 Oct 2026 from saved copies after web.archive.org stopped the 16-17 Oct check. S1: 20 live event pages on distinct nights from 4 to 28 Jun (4-7, 11-21 and 24-28 Jun). The live sitemap keeps past pages only from 4 Jun, so 1 Apr to 3 Jun is not covered, and June shows without a saved page could be missing. S2: 14 live event pages (2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 20, 22 and 28 Jul, 6 Aug, 28 Sep) plus 47 more rows from the union of the four archived /calendar/ listings (12 Jul: 12 Jul-20 Sep; 15 Aug: 15 Aug-18 Oct; 19 Aug: 20 Aug-22 Oct; 19 Sep: 19 Sep-14 Nov), unioned by archive event id and across sources by night plus slug or title, the latest copy winning. 1-11 Jul is covered only by the live pages, and a show added after 12 Jul for a night from 12 Jul to 14 Aug and gone again by 15 Aug would be missed. Removed: 0 by the Amendment 4 words, 0 canceled, 0 off-site (every live page gives 8901 Sunset Blvd, West Hollywood), 0 untitled, 0 moved by Amendment 3. The independent skeptic recounted the saved bodies with its own parsers and got the same 81 rows (S1 20, S2 61) and 8 of 8 sampled rows matching their sources; it changed no count. Earlier, the 16 Oct review corrected the first check's arithmetic (24 sitemap URLs with June lastmod, not 23; 19 of the 23 past ones read, not 21) and read the 4 June pages left unread, which brought S1 from 16 to 20; its figures are the ones used. S1 meets 20 with no margin, so the role rests on all 20 rows; the compile confirms it (Amendment 12). One of them is the free 16 Jun Ultimate Jam Night, which carries no Amendment 4 word and counts; without it S1 is 19. Its page text describes a 26 May edition, but its date field says 16 Jun; either date is in S1 and no other row is on 26 May, so it counts once. One S2 row (20 Sep) is the second date of a 'ROLLING QUARTZ [MY TURN] 2026 TOUR in Los Angeles' sequential-dates block in the 12 Jul and 15 Aug listings; its own ticket link names a different eventcartel show, so its title is the block's title, and it is gone from the 19 Aug and 19 Sep listings with no cancel mark. It counts as listed and not canceled; without it S2 is 60. The compile rule written for Horseshoe Tavern and The Pearl ("Notes for compiling": a row that vanished from a later copy before its date is left out) would drop it; either way the room meets S2. The 19 Sep listing marks the 4 Nov Dead Boys show 'Cancelled' (outside S2). Programme: almost every night is a rock, metal or punk bill of 3-8 acts, many of them tribute nights, with free Ultimate Jam Night sessions.
- §5: Los Angeles is the Los Angeles-Long Beach-Anaheim MSA (OMB Bulletin 23-01), which takes in West Hollywood. No other Los Angeles venue is in `venues.csv`: Lodge Room is out on §3 (reading 5), Troubadour on §3 (reading 3), The Roxy on §1 (Goldenvoice, AEG, is its house booker), and Hotel Cafe (another cluster), which `candidates.md` had "not used" on §5 alone, was re-admitted on §5 on 18 Oct 2026 through this venue (its section above). So §5 passes, and the pass is symmetric: each passes only through the other. §6: Los Angeles is not in the Minneapolis-St Paul metro.
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.whiskyagogo.com 56 (on 15 Oct UTC 1 page and robots.txt; on 16 Oct UTC, 22:12-22:24, 54: 40 in the first check and 14 in the review, 51 at 200 and 3 at 404), whiskyagogo.com 1 (robots.txt, 15 Oct), web.archive.org 17 (4 in scouting on 15 Oct: three CDX lines, one of them a 503 and its retry, and the 15 May home-page copy; 13 on 17 Oct UTC, 21:21-21:28, by the settle prefetch: 4 CDX queries and the 9 copies under Read). These 13 come after the last line counted in the 6,577 requests under "Eligibility check" (16 Oct 23:25 UTC). Source: requests.log. The record's own count is 40 (the first check alone).

### Ardmore Music Hall (`phl-ardmore`, Philadelphia)
- Source: The venue's own WordPress home page (https://ardmoremusichall.com/; /all-events/ and /calendar/ hold the same list).
