- Operator (§1, Amendment 14): Owner: Good George LLC, owned by Mississippi Productions Inc., the owners of Mississippi Studios (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolution_Hall, search snippet; the privacy policy gives Mississippi Studios' address and mail). The record's skeptic corrected the owner field, and its correction is used here: the Mississippi Studios owners own Revolution Hall 'with True West' (https://www.portlandmercury.com/music/2017/07/28/19196631/the-owners-of-mississippi-studios-just-bought-the-north-star-ballroom), so True West is a co-owner; 2014 press calls the venue a partnership between promoter True West and the Mississippi Studios owners (https://www.wweek.com/portland/blog-32443-details-of-new-music-venue-at-washington-high-school-released.html, search snippet). House booker: True West (True West LLC), named by its own site as producing the concerts ('True West is one of the leading independent concert promoters and event producers in the Pacific Northwest, with concerts at Aladdin Theater, Revolution Hall ...', https://truewestpresents.com/about-us/; the tech pack's production contacts use @truewestpresents.com addresses, https://www.revolutionhall.com/rev-hall-tech-pack/). The house labels 'Revolution Hall Presents', 'REVOLUTION HALL PRESENTS' and 'True West Presents' present 69 of the 99 counted main-hall rows (70%; S1 36 of 54, S2 33 of 45), and 'Show Bar Presents' 37 of the 50 counted Show Bar rows (74%; S1 19 of 27, S2 18 of 23). Every counted row names a presenter. Outside presenters on counted main-hall rows: 'Revolution Hall and Dayglo Presents' 4 (S2, with the house), Live Nation ('LIVE NATION PRESENTS') 3 (all S1), Adventures Without Limit 3 (S1), Outback Presents 3 (2 + 1), Soul'd Out 3 (2 + 1), Powell's Books 3 (1 + 2), Monqui 2 (S1), and Willamette Week, OBSESSED, Doe Records, Show and Tell, Telos Onstage (S1), Reggae Rise Up, Floydian Slips, Showbox ('SHOWBOX PRESENTS', which the settle report reads as AEG's Showbox) and Mammoth NW (S2) 1 each. On counted Show Bar rows: Low Bar Chorale 9 (6 + 3), Video Dance Attack 2 (S1) and Montavilla Jazz Festival 2 (S2). None of them presents more than half of a room's rows, so none is a house booker and none joins a cluster. Web searches in the check and its skeptic found no Live Nation or AEG deal, stake or acquisition involving True West, Mississippi Studios or Revolution Hall; Portland press ties AEG to Monqui Presents and Live Nation to another venue, not to True West or Mississippi Studios. Ticketing is Etix, not Ticketmaster or AXS. Owner and house booker are independent, so §1 passes, and the operator is written as True West LLC, as for the Aladdin. Under the literal reading of Amendment 1 (Amendment 14, last item), Live Nation (3 counted S1 rows) and Showbox (1 counted S2 row) are operators of the main hall, so the building would fail §1 in that report; Monqui's 2 rows are to be checked against `ln-aeg.csv` as well.
- Rooms: two, each from a field of its own, never from the title: data-venue-id 16897 ('Revolution Hall', class revolution-hall, logo 'Revolution Hall Logo') and 21322 ('Show Bar at Revolution Hall', class show-bar-at-revolution-hall, logo 'Showbar Logo'; called 'Show Bar', class show-bar, in the March copies, same venue id), and the venue-name span of the calendar cells. Every row in the periods carries one of the two ids, so no row goes to a house room. The source names the main-hall room 'Revolution Hall', the building's own name; since it is a real room (its own venue id), not rows with no room, it is listed as 'Revolution Hall Auditorium', the private-events page's name for it ('The Revolution Hall Auditorium', https://www.revolutionhall.com/private-events-revolution-hall/; the about page calls it 'the historic auditorium'), so that it is not read as the building's room for rows with no room. The Roof Deck and Martha's are bars with no show rows. Both rooms are in one building at 1300 SE Stark St.
- Coverage: Revolution Hall Auditorium S1 >=54, S2 >=45; Show Bar at Revolution Hall S1 >=27, S2 >=23; building S1 >=81, S2 >=68; all lower bounds. Settled on 17-18 Oct 2026 from saved copies (fetched 18 Oct 03:29-03:41 UTC and read offline in settle batch 5), after web.archive.org's second 429 stopped the 16-17 Oct check, when the scouting record could not count S1 or S2. The count is the union of the 18 copies keyed by Etix event id (latest copy wins): 162 rows in the periods (main hall 54 / 49, Show Bar 31 / 28), of which 1 is canceled (main hall, 23 Sep, title prefix 'CANCELED:') and 12 are removed by the Amendment 4 words (S1 4: Show Bar open mic 3, karaoke 1; S2 8: main hall comedy 3, Show Bar comedy 2, open mic 3); none is `off-site` and none is untitled. Counted rows by month, main hall: Apr 21, May 18, Jun 15, Jul 15, Aug 14, Sep 16; Show Bar: Apr 8, May 8, Jun 11, Jul 6, Aug 7, Sep 10. An independent skeptic re-parsed the 18 saved bodies with its own script and got the same 162 rows, flags and counts, found 8 sampled rows in their cited copies, and upheld the verdict; it corrected no count. Its notes: 5 rows cite a ?style=list copy as source_url, but their clock time and presenter come from a home-page copy (dates agree in every copy, so counts and nights are unchanged); 4 main-hall nights carry two rows (12 Apr, 28 Jun, 19 Jul, 26 Sep), and counted once per night both rooms stay above 20. Windows: the home-page feed (30 shows from the capture date, both rooms) covers 03-13..04-12, 04-11..05-10, 05-09..06-11, 06-06..07-18, 06-25..08-02, 07-06..08-14, 07-29..09-03, 08-12..09-12 and 09-21..10-12, the list copies 05-09..06-08 and 08-02..09-05, the calendar copy of 2 Aug 08-02..08-31, the Show Bar page feed 05-09..12-02, 07-13..11-04, 07-26..11-15 and 08-02..12-02, with Show Bar month grids on every page. S1 has no uncovered day for either room. In S2 no capture covers the main hall on 13-20 Sep (8 nights), and Show Bar's 13-20 Sep is covered only by the 2 Aug /show-bar/ copy; the CDX files show no capture of /, ?style=list, ?style=calendar or /show-bar/ between 12 Aug and 22 Sep, so the gap cannot be filled from these URLs. Each window is what was announced on its date and the last date of a 30-show page may be cut, so the counts are lower bounds. Both rooms meet coverage by themselves, so both are test rooms of one cluster; `shows.csv` confirms or corrects this (Amendment 12). Programme: mostly concerts in the main hall, with talks, podcasts, film-with-orchestra and drag nights that pass the Amendment 4 words and count; small band nights in the Show Bar (Notes for compiling, scope).
- §5 and §6: cluster `true-west`, shared with the Aladdin Theater (`pdx-aladdin`): True West is the house booker of both and a co-owner here. Mississippi Studios and Polaris Hall, settled eligible in the same batch, are linked with Revolution Hall and TrueWest in a 'family of music venues' footer, and Revolution Hall's owners are Mississippi Studios' owners, so they would join this cluster (Amendment 1: the owner joins rooms); their sections keep their own cluster `pdx-mississippi`, so if the shared owner is taken as shown, the Aladdin Theater, Revolution Hall, Mississippi Studios and Polaris Hall get one cluster id. No eligible Portland venue of another cluster is in `venues.csv` yet, so Portland passes §5 through another venue there: Wonder Ballroom, settled eligible on 18 Oct 2026 as a cluster of its own (see its section below); §5 is left to the lead. §6: Portland (the Portland-Vancouver-Hillsboro, OR-WA MSA) is not Minneapolis-St Paul, and the venue is neither Hideout nor Black Cat (Amendment 11).
- Capacity: no room figure. The information page says 'Capacity varies per configuration. We have 5 configurations' and gives no number, and the tech pack gives no audience figure. The private-events page's 'events of all kinds for 20 to 1200 guests' covers the whole building, so it is not used (Amendment 8: size unknown).
- Requests during the eligibility check: revolutionhall.com 2 on 15 Oct (robots.txt and the redirect to www), www.revolutionhall.com 17 (3 on 15 Oct with robots.txt; 14 on 16 Oct, 13 by the check and 1 home-page read by its skeptic; 12 answered 200, 5 answered 404 (the terms paths); the record counts 13), shoprevolutionhall.com 2 on 16 Oct by the skeptic (robots.txt and the terms path, 404), truewestpresents.com 6 on 16 Oct by the record's count (requests.log holds 16 requests to truewestpresents.com and www.truewestpresents.com that day, shared with the checks of the other True West venues; 5 of them, at 22:40-22:41 UTC, are the pages the record lists: the home page in its www and bare forms, about-us, partners and the April calendar), web.archive.org 0 during the check and 26 on 18 Oct 2026 UTC (03:29-03:41: 8 CDX queries and the 18 copies, all 200). The settle and its skeptic sent no request. Source: requests.log.
- Note for compiling: the main-hall rows are `revolutionhall-com:Revolution Hall` in the settle `shows.csv` and go to `pdx-revhall-auditorium`; the Show Bar rows go to `pdx-revhall-showbar`. Comedy Bang! Bang! has early and late sets on 11 Aug as two rows, both out of coverage by the word 'comedy'. The 5 rows the skeptic noted keep a ?style=list source_url with a time and presenter read from a home-page copy, for the 10% second pass.

### Wonder Ballroom (`pdx-wonder`, Portland)
- Source: The venue's own WordPress site with the RHP (Rockhouse) events plugin: its /events/ listing (rhpSingleEvent blocks with the date, a tagline field that names the presenter, the title and the show time) and one page per show at /event/<slug>/wonder-ballroom/portland-oregon/ (a schema.org JSON-LD startDate with its offset and the name, and an RHP header with the title, the tagline presenter and a venue element). The live listing shows coming shows only, and past event pages return the home page or a 410, so S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of the own listing and event pages. Settled eligible on 18 Oct 2026 from the prefetched copies; `candidates.md` had it undecided at S1 15.
- Read: 67 web.archive.org copies, 61 of them used: 60 event-page copies (Mar-Jun 2026 captures, one show each) and https://web.archive.org/web/20260613213714id_/https://wonderballroom.com/events/ (54 shows from 18 Jun 2026 to 31 Jan 2027). The six home-page copies (20260303061134, 20260307202745, 20260412185759, 20260501164719, 20260523220032, 20260613205729) are empty shells with no event rows (the show list is not in the server HTML) and are not used. Live during the check (16 Oct 2026 UTC): https://wonderballroom.com/, /events/, /privacy-policy/, /contact/, /faq/, /tickets/, /rental-info/, /tech-specs/, /wp-json/wp/v2/types, the sitemap index, the five rhp_events sitemaps, 13 event pages and the terms paths below, for date, status and terms checks only; the saved live sitemap is used only as an indicator and for one cancellation (see Coverage). No live row is counted.
- Dates checked: These are show dates. Die Spitz page (capture 20260307215750): 'Tuesday, April 21 Show : 8 pm', JSON-LD startDate 2026-04-21T20:00:00-0700; Dry Cleaning (20260307213124): 'Sunday, May 17 Show : 8 pm', startDate 2026-05-17T20:00:00-0700; 3BALLMTY (20260412202240): 'Wednesday, June 10 Show : 8 pm', startDate 2026-06-10T20:00:00-0700. The 13 Jun listing rows carry full dates such as 'Sat, Jun 27, 2026 | Monqui Presents | Searows'. All starts lie between 11:00 and 20:30 local, so Amendment 3 moves no date.
- robots.txt (15 and 16 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://wonderballroom.com/robots.txt: 'User-agent: * / Disallow: /wp-admin/ / Allow: /wp-admin/admin-ajax.php' and a sitemap line; no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every page read; web.archive.org allowed the snapshot and CDX paths.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026 UTC; the 67 copies re-checked 18 Oct): none-found. There are no terms of use on the site: the five terms paths return 404, and the footer links only Food & Beverage, Contact, ADA, Jobs, Tech Specs, Tickets, FAQ, Rental Info, Events and Privacy Policy. The privacy policy covers data collection and cookies only and has no use-licence or copying clause. No '©' or 'All rights reserved' line and no copying or personal-use text on any saved page (the record's skeptic re-scanned the 34 saved venue bodies) or in any of the 67 copies (the settle skeptic). Checked: https://wonderballroom.com/ (footer); https://wonderballroom.com/events/; https://wonderballroom.com/privacy-policy/; https://wonderballroom.com/contact/; https://wonderballroom.com/faq/; https://wonderballroom.com/tickets/; https://wonderballroom.com/rental-info/; https://wonderballroom.com/tech-specs/; https://wonderballroom.com/terms/ (404); https://wonderballroom.com/terms-of-use/ (404); https://wonderballroom.com/terms-of-service/ (404); https://wonderballroom.com/terms-and-conditions/ (404); https://wonderballroom.com/legal/ (404)
- Platform: WordPress with Elementor and the RHP (Rockhouse) events plugin (rhp_events sitemaps, RHP script variables); tickets are sold through Etix. No saved page and none of the 67 copies carries the 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer, so the plugin is noted only (reading 4), unlike the Rockhouse Partners sites under "Platform and archive terms". Not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma or Music Glue. No bot check, challenge, 403 or Wayback error page in any copy; the 3 live event pages that answered 410 are past shows, which is how the plugin treats past events (the record's skeptic), not a refusal.
- Operator (§1, Amendment 14): private owners (private persons named in https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wonder_Ballroom and https://www.portlandmercury.com/news/2005/06/23/33887/this-old-ballroom; by the 2005 article, one of them is also a partner in Monqui Presents). No company owner is named. House booker: Monqui Presents, a Portland independent promoter founded in 1983, which the venue names as its music booker ('BOOKING INQUIRIES For Serious Music Related Events Monqui Presents', https://wonderballroom.com/contact/) and which presents 41 of the 47 counted rows (S1 21 of 21, S2 20 of 26); cluster `monqui-presents`. Other presenters on counted rows: Showbox Presents (AEG's Seattle promoter brand) 4 (S2: 25 Aug, 9, 11 and 17 Sep), Minty Boi Presents 1, SINCE PRESENTS 1, and Blisspop Presents in the title of one row whose tagline is Monqui Presents (1 Aug). The AEG tie is the 2024 'strategic alliance' of AEG Presents and Monqui to build a new Lloyd District venue (https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20240709071936/en, https://news.pollstar.com/2024/07/25/monqui-aeg-presents-developing-new-portland-venue/; AEG calls Monqui a Portland-based independent promoter): a partnership on another venue, not a stake in Monqui or in the Wonder Ballroom. The record's skeptic found no sale of the venue, no Live Nation or AEG owner and no AEG stake in Monqui, disclosed or 50%+, and Monqui is not in `ln-aeg.csv`. So §1 passes on the record's evidence; if the lead holds evidence that AEG holds 50%+ of Monqui or books for it, §1 fails. Ticketing is Etix, not Ticketmaster or AXS. Under the literal reading of Amendment 1 (Amendment 14, last item), Showbox Presents (AEG) is an operator of the room through its 4 counted S2 rows, so the room would fail §1 in that report.
- Rooms: one. The source has no room field or filter ('rhpRoomContent' appears only in CSS), and every event page's venue element and JSON-LD location name only 'Wonder Ballroom'; Wonder Bar is the food and drink side, not a listed stage. Rows moved to other buildings are marked only in the title ('MOVED TO THE CRYSTAL BALLROOM', 'MOVED TO ROSELAND THEATER') and are `off-site`. The settle wrote the rows as `wonderballroom-com:house`; that is the whole venue, not a split venue's pool room, so it is the venue's one room and a test room, as for Martyrs', Mississippi Studios and Polaris Hall.
- Capacity: 778 ('Capacity: 778 open floor (450 if chairs are used)', https://wonderballroom.com/contact/ and /rental-info/; the saved /tech-specs/ page gives the same 778 open floor but '380 if chairs are used').
- Coverage: S1 >=21, S2 >=26 (lower bounds; settle of 18 Oct 2026, upheld by its skeptic, whose own parser and union give the same 51 rows by date and title). S1 has 22 rows in the window, minus 1 off-site (underscores, 24 Jun, moved to the Crystal Ballroom). S2 has 29, minus 1 canceled ('*CANCELLED* 3QUENCY', 7 Jul), 1 off-site (Slayyyter, 5 Sep, moved to the Crystal Ballroom) and deca joins (26 Sep), which the copies show with no mark but the venue's own live sitemap renamed cancelled-deca-joins (lastmod 27 Sep); on the copies alone S2 is 27. 0 untitled, 0 removed by the Amendment 4 words. Of the six event-page captures the 16 Oct check could not read once the archive was stopped, five are now saved and read (the sixth, captured 7 Mar, was not picked); with them and the 'RESCHEDULED Claire Rosinkranz' page (2 Jun, its new date), S1 went from 15 to 21. Gaps: no listing copy covers 1 Apr to 12 Jun, so those weeks rest on the 15 S1 rows known only from dated event-page copies; the live sitemap (lastmod = show date + 1 day) suggests about 39 S1 shows, so S1 is a lower bound with a margin of 1, and it would still be 20 if the Claire Rosinkranz row were read as postponed. 13 Jun to 30 Sep is covered only by the 13 Jun listing copy, so shows announced later are missing (the sitemap suggests about 32 in S2). The CDX has no /events/ capture after 19 Jun 2026 and no event-page capture after 13 Jun; the unpicked captures (one event page captured 7 Mar, /events/ and /events/page/2/ of 19 Jun) could only raise the counts. All 15 page-only S1 rows keep their slugs in the live sitemap and none is renamed cancelled- or postponed-; TRICKY (28 Sep) stays counted (its sitemap slug is unchanged).
- §5: Portland (Portland-Vancouver-Hillsboro, OR-WA MSA, OMB Bulletin 23-01). Cluster `monqui-presents`, a cluster of its own: no section above names Monqui Presents as owner or house booker of the Aladdin Theater, Revolution Hall, Mississippi Studios or Polaris Hall (Monqui presents 2 counted Revolution Hall rows, under half), so Portland passes §5 through it whether those four are one cluster or two (the open point at the head of this part), and they give it §5 in turn. Portland sorts after the cut, so the venue is not in `venues.csv` and was not compiled. §6: Portland is not in the Minneapolis-St Paul metro, and the venue is neither Hideout nor Black Cat (Amendment 11).
- Requests during the eligibility check: wonderballroom.com 36 (requests.log: robots.txt in scouting on 15 Oct 2026 UTC; 35 on 16 Oct UTC, 34 of them at 20:21-20:26 and /wp-json/wp/v2/types by the record's skeptic at 21:49; 28 answered 200, 5 answered 404 (the terms paths) and 3 answered 410 (past event pages)), web.archive.org 5 in scouting on 15 Oct UTC (2 CDX queries, the 17 May home-page copy and the 13 Jun listing copy, and one copy request with a malformed URL that got no answer), 9 copies on 16 Oct UTC, 21:34-21:38 (all 200; the record counts 10), and 71 on 18 Oct 2026 UTC by the prefetch (04:38-04:45 and 18:50-19:18: 4 CDX queries and 67 copies, all 200). Six more archive requests of 16 Oct (one CDX query at 20:23 and five copies at 21:38 UTC) were refused by polite.py ('web.archive.org stopped after a 403/429') and not sent. The settle and its skeptic sent no request. Source: requests.log.
- Note for compiling: not compiled. The settle `shows.csv` leaves `performers` empty on every row: an RHP accordion typed 'performer' on 5 of the 60 event pages is neither schema.org nor a feed field, and the JSON-LD has no performer. Its skeptic suggests that a `venues.csv` row, if one is ever written, follow `chi-martyrs` (role test, empty building) rather than the `:house` suffix. `pdx-wonder` is the id used here only; no row is written.

### Brick & Mortar Music Hall (`sf-brickmortar`, San Francisco)
- Source: The venue's own WordPress pages, where TicketWeb's 'event-discovery' plugin renders the show rows server-side: the homepage list (/, /page/2/, /page/3/; each row gives weekday and M.D date, a presenter prefix, the title with its /tm-event/<slug>/ link, doors and show time and a venue field), the same list at /calendar/ and /event-details/, a 'Just announced' sidebar widget with full dates, a JS 'all_events' array on /calendar/ with ISO dates for about 50-60 coming shows, and one /tm-event/<slug>/ page per show with the full date. The live site lists only current and future shows (all 107 entries of tm_events-sitemap.xml are current or future), so S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of these own pages. Settled on 18 Oct 2026 from saved copies, after web.archive.org stopped the 16-17 Oct check: the copies were fetched on 18 Oct 2026 (01:10-01:41 UTC) and counted offline by the settle and an independent check, both without network.
- Read: 53 web.archive.org id_ copies, all status 200 and all real listing or event pages: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://www.brickandmortarmusic.com/ for ts 20260306152404, 20260307213156, 20260328085510, 20260414003052, 20260511162557, 20260523224437, 20260611002110, 20260629204424, 20260703171747, 20260821222139; /page/2/ 20260414001307; /page/3/ 20260609151457, 20260703171743; /calendar/ 20260521105908, 20260703171747, 20260821222139; /calendar/page/2/ 20260413102328; /calendar/page/3/ 20260413233546; /event-details/ 20260511132614; /event-details/page/2/ 20260306155147, 20260613003135; /event-details/page/3/ 20260609154945; and 31 /tm-event/<slug>/ pages (24 captured on 3 Jul, 6 on 21 Aug and 1 on 3 Oct 2026), picked from 11 CDX queries (one per listing URL, and a prefix and an exact query for /tm-event/ from 1 Jul). Live (16 Oct 2026 UTC): https://www.brickandmortarmusic.com/, /page/2/, /page/3/, /calendar/, /tm-event/natanya/, /tm-event/father-of-peace/, sitemap_index.xml and tm_events-sitemap.xml, for date and platform checks only.
- Dates checked: These are show dates. The 3 Jul 2026 homepage copy lists 'Wed 8.12 Goldenvoice Presents YELLOW Doors: 7:00PM Show: 9:00PM', and the 3 Jul copy of /tm-event/yellow/ reads 'August 12, 2026 9:00 pm PDT (Doors: 7:00 pm ) Brick and Mortar Music Hall , 1710 Mission Street'. The 28 Mar 2026 homepage copy lists 'Wed 4.1 Popscene + Brick & Mortar Music Hall presents Chloe Qisha Doors: 7:00PM Show: 8:00PM' (row 1 Apr, 20:00). Live: /tm-event/natanya/ 'November 12, 2026 8:00 pm PST (Doors: 7:00 pm)' and /tm-event/father-of-peace/ 'December 9, 2026 8:00 pm PST (Doors: 7:00 pm)'. List rows give M.D only, so the year is taken from the capture date. The sitemap lastmod values are creation dates, not show dates, and were not used. Every counted start lies between 14:00 and 21:00, so Amendment 3 moves no date.
- robots.txt (15 and 16 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://www.brickandmortarmusic.com/robots.txt: 'User-agent: * / Disallow:' (Yoast block, empty, allows everything), Crawl-delay: 10 and a sitemap_index.xml line; no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every venue page requested; web.archive.org allowed the snapshot and CDX paths.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026 UTC): silent. The Terms of Use (last updated 3 Feb 2020) say 'The Service and its original content, features and functionality are and will remain the exclusive property of Brick & Mortar and its licensors.', which states ownership only; the rest are links, termination, disclaimer, governing-law, changes and contact clauses. There is no ban on copying, reproducing, republishing, scraping or automated access and no personal-use licence. The footer 'Copyright 2009 - Brick & Mortar. All Rights Reserved.' is a bare notice (Amendment 14). The privacy policy (also 3 Feb 2020) has no use-licence section. The terms page was read live twice (first and second check), and its saved body was scanned again for this section; it is not among the 53 archive copies. Checked: https://www.brickandmortarmusic.com/terms-of-use; https://www.brickandmortarmusic.com/privacy-policy; https://www.brickandmortarmusic.com/ (footer, which also links /purchase-policy, not read); https://www.brickandmortarmusic.com/robots.txt
- Platform: Self-run WordPress (Tuto theme by MH Themes, footer 'Proudly powered by Tuto WordPress theme from MH Themes') with TicketWeb's 'event-discovery' plugin (/tm-event/, /tm-venue/, /tm-attraction/; links carry REFID=clientsitewp; the accessibility note points to info@ticketweb.com), the same plugin as The Pearl. TicketWeb, a Live Nation/Ticketmaster company, is the ticket vendor; it does not host the site and no source names it as owner or booker, so neither §1 nor the platform-terms reading is triggered (the check's note; TicketWeb's own terms were not read, as polite.py refuses ticketweb.com). Not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue or Webflow, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer in any live body or any of the 53 copies. No bot check and no 403: every venue-host request returned 200, and none of the 53 copies is a captcha, challenge or access-denied page (the only 'cloudflare' strings are cdnjs asset links).
- Operator: a private owner group (named in https://kqed.org/arts/13874540/brick-mortar-music-hall-ousts-controversial-co-owner-signs-ten-year-lease, which reports a ten-year lease); no company owner is identified, and the About page says the venue 'stands as one of the few remaining independently owned music venues in San Francisco'. House booker: the venue itself. Its Booking link is its own address (mailto:booking@brickandmortarmusic.org), and Brick & Mortar is named as presenter on 58 of the 107 counted S1+S2 rows (S1 28, S2 30), alone (36 as 'Brick & Mortar Music Hall') or with Popscene (22). Outside promoters on counted rows: Goldenvoice (AEG) 28 of 107 (26%; S1 19 of 58, S2 9 of 49), all as 'Goldenvoice Presents' alone, under half, so it is not the house booker and does not decide §1 (Amendment 14); Popscene 24 (22 with the venue, 1 alone, 1 with Leisure); Moms Feelin Themselves 2; Dancing Ghosts, Leisure, Golden Hour Sound & Studios and one row presented by 415Fest, Bayzlive and L7CK Entertainment, 1 each. 11 rows name a person's name + 'Presents' and are withheld here and counted (the lead may decide whether one of them is a promoter's trading name), and 3 name no presenter; even if all 14 of these rows were Goldenvoice's, it would present 42 of 107, under half (unlike Great American Music Hall, where 35 of 72 counted rows named no presenter). No public source read names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake, house booker or booking deal: one search for a Goldenvoice booking deal found only Goldenvoice-presented shows (for example https://missionlocal.org/event/brick-mortar-music-hall-goldenvoice-presents-yellow/), the KQED piece and Goldenvoice's 2018 booking partnership with Slim's and Great American Music Hall (other venues); a second search, for 2025-2026 ownership news, could not be run (search budget used up). Under the literal reading of Amendment 1 (Amendment 14, last item), Goldenvoice (AEG) is an operator of this room through its 28 counted rows, so the room fails §1 in that report.
- Rooms: one. tw-venue-details and the /tm-event/ venue name give only the building ('Brick and Mortar Music Hall, 1710 Mission Street'); there is no room field or room filter. The settle files call it `house`; it is the room itself, not the pool room of a split venue, so it meets coverage by itself (Amendment 12). The 15-17 Oct record called it 'pool room of the building'; the check corrects that reading, and its correction is used here.
- Coverage: S1 >=58, S2 >=49 (union by /tm-event/ slug of the 53 copies, the latest capture winning; by month Apr 20, May 19, Jun 19, Jul 15, Aug 17, Sep 17). The independent check recounted the same 107 shows (no row in only one count). 0 rows marked canceled, 0 off-site (every venue field names Brick and Mortar Music Hall; the Hotel Utah Saloon rows the scouting expected do not appear), 0 untitled, 0 removed by the Amendment 4 words. 7 shows were dropped from a later listing that covered their date, with no cancel mark (S1: 24 May, 26 May, 3 Jun, 7 Jun, 17 Jun; S2: 12 Sep, 20 Sep); they stay in `shows.csv` with canceled=0, and without them the room has 53 / 47. If they are left out at compile, as for Horseshoe Tavern and The Pearl (Notes for compiling), the room is still a test room. Two shows changed date across captures and the latest capture's date is used: one moved from 17 to 18 Jun (stays in S1) and one from 16 Jun to 20 Sep (S1 to S2). The 3 Apr row's slug is 'six-sex-postponed-to-2026', read as a show postponed into 2026 and held, so it counts; without it S1 is 57 (52 conservative), as the check notes. One S2 row, 'The Digilogue Bay Area 10 Year Celebration (Networking & Panels)' (11 Jul, 14:00), is not a concert but carries no Amendment 4 word, so it counts; without it S2 is 48. No date in 1 Apr to 30 Sep lies outside every listing window, but each window holds only shows announced by its capture date, so late additions can be missing for 6-10 May (latest covering capture 14 Apr), 25 Jul to 20 Aug (latest 3 Jul) and 12-30 Sep (latest 21 Aug); these are lower bounds. The CDX files list no unread capture inside those ranges (the next homepage capture after 21 Aug is 3 Oct). Programme: concerts, apart from the one networking row.
- Capacity: no figure published by the venue (About, General Info, Private Events and History pages read); the 250 in the KQED piece is press, so the field is empty.
- §5: San Francisco also has Bottom of the Hill (`sf-bottom`), Make-Out Room (`sf-makeout`) and Yoshi's (`sf-yoshis`), other clusters, so §5 passes. §6: San Francisco is not in Study 1.
- Note: the 15-17 Oct record gives the cluster as `brick-and-mortar-music-hall-sf`; the row uses `sf-brickmortar`, a single-venue cluster either way.
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.brickandmortarmusic.com 21 lines in requests.log, all 200 (2 in scouting on 15 Oct UTC, robots.txt and the homepage; 15 in the first check and 4 in the second check on 16 Oct UTC; 2 of the 21 are robots.txt reads), brickandmortarmusic.com 1 (robots.txt, 15 Oct UTC), web.archive.org 3 in scouting on 15 Oct UTC (two CDX queries and the 11 May homepage copy). For the settle: web.archive.org 64 on 18 Oct 2026 UTC (11 CDX queries and 53 copies, all 200, 01:10-01:41 UTC; the 11 May homepage copy was fetched again); the settle and its check made no request (see requests.log)

### The Chapel (`sf-chapel-*`, San Francisco)
- Source: The venue's own server-rendered HTML listing on its self-hosted WordPress site, made by the SeeTickets WordPress plugin in the page itself: list rows on https://thechapelsf.com/ and https://thechapelsf.com/music/ (date without year, a presenter 'header' line, title, headliners, show and doors time and a separate venue field, `<p class='venue'>at X</p>`) and month grids on https://thechapelsf.com/calendar/ (month and year from the grid heading, day from the cell, title and times; no venue and no presenter field). The live pages list only coming shows (from 16 Oct 2026); further calendar pages load through admin-ajax POST, which was not used; WordPress REST has no event type (/wp-json/wp/v2/types lists posts, pages and popups only), and the events sitemap was last modified in 2023. S1 and S2 therefore come from web.archive.org copies of the same three own pages. Ticket links go to wl.seetickets.us (and tixr.com for KQED shows), ticketing sites that are never a source; rows are counted only from the venue's own pages. The scouting host table's 'listings only on SeeTickets' was wrong on the source question, and `candidates.md` had listed The Chapel only as looked at during scouting.

Not usable: /calendar/ 20260309071828 (a 28-byte body, 'The read operation timed out') and /calendar/ 20260630124654 (byte-identical to the 20260407021613 copy, months from April 2026, so a misfiled duplicate, not the 30 Jun capture and not a challenge page). Not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer or RHP/Etix plugin on the live site or in any copy (the two 'wix' matches are base64 image-URL noise). No bot check and no 403 in front of the listings: every venue-host request answered 200 or the site's own 404, and Cloudflare Turnstile is loaded only for the Contact Form 7 form. No used copy is a bot-check, error or 403 page.
- Operator (§1, Amendment 14): Owner: a private owner, a restaurateur (named in https://www.mixonline.com/?p=40841, https://www.cbsnews.com/sanfrancisco/news/beloved-san-francisco-venue-the-chapel-marks-10-anniversary-with-special-shows and https://www.7x7.com/the-chapel-the-missions-most-ambitious-music-venue-1783805346.html); no company owner is named. House booker of the room The Chapel: (((folkYEAH!))), an independent Bay Area production company whose founder is the venue's talent buyer and 'has booked nearly every show in the Chapel's history' (https://www.sfexaminer.com/lifestyles/entertainment/in-its-second-decade-the-chapel-keeps-things-fresh/article_1e736714-b850-11ed-936d-cbcb748610c7.html, search snippet; also https://48hills.org/?p=160501, search snippet, and the CBS piece: shows 'primarily booked by (((folkYEAH!)))'). The venue's own pages do not name it as booker (they give the in-house address booking@thechapelsf.com), but '(((folkYEAH!))) Presents' heads 53 of the room's 86 counted S1 and S2 rows (S1 29 of 43, S2 24 of 43), more than half, so it is that room's house booker. It presents no counted row in the other rooms (Main Bar 0 of 4, Curio 0 of 4, Outdoor Stage 0 of 2; the house room's 22 counted rows come from calendar grids, which carry no presenter field), so they are booked in house. In the whole building it presents 53 of the 118 counted rows and 53 of the 96 counted list rows (55%); 80 of those 96 carry a header line, 59 of them a presenter line ('... Present' or '... Presents'), and (((folkYEAH!))) is named on 53 of the 59. Outside presenters on counted rows, all in the room The Chapel unless noted: KALW 91.7 FM 3, and Value Culture (Curio), Slumberland Records, popscene, buildOn, Desert Daze, The Allen Ginsberg Estate, KALX 90.7 FM, KQED Live, Alt.Latino, Sinister Sounds Records and GLIDE 1 each; radio stations, labels, promoters and nonprofits, none above half of a room's rows. One more header names only a private person; the row is counted and the name is not written (`presented_by` leaves it out). No row names Live Nation, AEG, Another Planet or Goldenvoice, and no saved body has a Live Nation, AEG, Another Planet, Goldenvoice, Rockhouse/Etix, Ticketmaster or AXS string. Web searches in the 16-17 Oct check found no report of a sale of the venue or a Live Nation, AEG or Another Planet takeover of 777 Valencia in 2025 or 2026, and no Live Nation, AEG or Goldenvoice tie for (((folkYEAH!))); the search budget then ran out, so this rests on absence of evidence, the venue's own booking contact and SeeTickets ticketing. §1 passes under Amendment 14. (((folkYEAH!))) presents no row at any other venue in `venues.csv` or in the other settled venues' `shows.csv`, so the building is one cluster (`sf-chapel`). Under the literal reading of Amendment 1, popscene (1 counted row, 24 Apr, the room The Chapel) is also named in `presented_by` on 24 counted rows of Brick & Mortar Music Hall's settle `shows.csv` (S1 16, S2 8), so that room and Brick & Mortar would join one cluster in the sensitivity analysis.
- Rooms: the venue field of each list row, separate from the title (Amendment 14, rooms). Values: 'at The Chapel', 'at The Chapel Main Bar', 'at Curio', 'at The Chapel Outdoor Stage' and 'at Curio & Main Bar'; the one 'Curio & Main Bar' row (5 Jul) goes to Curio, the first listed. Title text ('in Curio') is not used as a room. 'The Chapel' is the source's label for the music room (https://thechapelsf.com/rental-spaces/ names 'The Chapel Music Room', the 'Music Room With Mezzanine'), so that room is `sf-chapel-musicroom` and the room for rows with no room carries the building name. Curio is in the same building: the footer of every page reads 'The Chapel / Curio Bar SF / 777 Valencia Street', so Curio rows are not off-site. The calendar grid has no venue field, so rows seen only in calendar grids go to the house room `sf-chapel-house`, a pool room of the same building; they could belong to any room. Room for a row: the latest capture with a venue field.
- Coverage: settled on 17-18 Oct 2026 from saved copies, after web.archive.org was stopped for good in the fetcher during the 16-17 Oct check (second 429, 00:38 on 17 Oct server time), which had left the venue undecided. The copies were fetched on 18 Oct under the 30 s archive rule and read offline; an independent check recounted them with its own parser and upheld the report. Lower bounds: The Chapel (music room) S1 >=43, S2 >=43; The Chapel Main Bar >=4 / 0; Curio 0 / >=4; The Chapel Outdoor Stage 0 / >=2; house room >=6 / >=16; building S1 >=53, S2 >=65. Shows were unioned by SeeTickets event id (date and title for 'Private Event' rows with no link), the latest capture giving date, title and time; 131 shows fall on local nights from 1 Apr to 30 Sep 2026 (2 Apr to 30 Sep), S1 60 and S2 71, and the check matched all 131 to `shows.csv` one to one by date and title, with the same rooms. Removed: 11 'Private Event' rows by the Amendment 4 words (S1 7: The Chapel 5, house 2; S2 4: The Chapel 2, house 2), 1 canceled ('CANCELED: Sophie Truax', 29 Sep) and 1 off-site ('MOVED TO KILOWATT ON TUESDAY 9/15: Magic Sword', the 10 Sep home copy), both in the room The Chapel in S2; no untitled row. The check's first pass missed the off-site row and gave The Chapel S2 44; after it, its counts equal the report's, so no count is corrected. World Cup screenings ('Watch ...', free admission) carry no Amendment 4 word and count: 6 in The Chapel (1 / 5), all 4 Main Bar rows and 3 of the 4 Curio rows; without them The Chapel would have 42 / 38. Gaps (the report's, borne out by the check against the CDX lists): every day from 1 Apr to 30 Sep lies within some used copy's horizon (calendar grids run months ahead; the home page and /music/ list about two weeks), but shows announced late can be missing where the newest covering copy is more than 14 days older than the show: 1-5 Apr (12 Mar calendar), 23-28 Jun (8 Jun), 19-21 Jul (3 Jul), 17 Aug to 8 Sep (23 Jul calendar; the 2 Aug home copy reaches only 16 Aug) and 21 Sep (10 Sep). The CDX has no /calendar/ capture between 20260723 and 20260923, no home capture between 20260802 and 20260910 and no /music/ capture after 20260703. Unpicked captures (calendar 7 Apr twice, 20260606170310 and 20260629213840; home 10 Mar, 2 Apr, 7 Apr twice, 8 Apr, 12 May, 13 May, 29 Jun five times, 20260630035546, 3 Jul, 10 Oct and 11 Oct; /music/ 7 Apr twice and 20260703171630; the 67 /event/ pages) lie outside 17 Aug to 8 Sep and could only raise the counts; the two October home captures come after 30 Sep. The record's second check counted 68 event URLs in the prefix list; 67 are /event/ pages and the 68th is the events sitemap. S2 counts include rows listed in advance; a cancellation made after the last covering copy would not show. The Chapel meets coverage by itself and is the one test room; Main Bar, Curio and Outdoor Stage cannot reach 20 in S1 from any capture (all 6 unroomed S1 rows added to any of them would still leave it under 20), and the house room holds the rows whose room is not given, so all four are pool (Amendments 12 and 14). `shows.csv` confirms or corrects this (Amendment 12).
- §5 and §6: the metro is the San Francisco-Oakland-Fremont, CA MSA (OMB Bulletin 23-01). It has eligible venues of other clusters: Bottom of the Hill (`sf-bottom`), Make-Out Room (`sf-makeout`) and Yoshi's (`sf-yoshis`), and Hotel Utah Saloon and Brick & Mortar Music Hall, settled the same night, so §5 passes. San Francisco is not in Study 1, and The Chapel is neither Hideout nor Black Cat (Amendment 11).
- Capacity: no room figure. The venue publishes only 'options for guests from 20 to 1,000' (https://thechapelsf.com/private-events/), which covers the music room with the bar, restaurant and outdoor patio; a third-party listing (Tripleseat) gives seated 500 for the music room, which is not the venue's own figure and is not used (Amendment 8: size unknown).
- Programme: mostly music (indie, folk, rock and touring acts), with private events, World Cup screenings, day parties on the Outdoor Stage and a film and music tour night. This is only a description; nothing was filtered by it.
- Requests during the eligibility check: thechapelsf.com 20 (requests.log: 2 in scouting on 15 Oct 2026 UTC, the robots.txt read and the home page; 18 in the check on 16 Oct UTC, 11 at 200 and 7 at 404, the terms paths), web.archive.org 30: 3 in scouting on 15 Oct UTC (2 CDX queries and the 14 May home copy), then 3 CDX queries and the 24 copies fetched by the prefetch on 18 Oct 2026, 03:48-04:01 UTC, about 30 s apart (26 at 200; the 9 Mar /calendar/ copy timed out). The 16-17 Oct check made none, as the archive was stopped; the settle and its check made no request (see requests.log)
- Note for compiling: `shows.csv` leaves `drop_reason` empty on the 11 'Private Event' rows. The words 'private event' take them out of coverage either way (Amendment 12), which gives the counts above; a count by `drop_reason` alone gives The Chapel 48 / 45 and the house room 8 / 18. In the run they are dropped as `non-music` only if the words lie outside the resolved name (Amendment 4).

### Hotel Utah Saloon (`sf-hotelutah`, San Francisco)
- Source: The venue's own server-rendered HTML listing on https://hotelutah.com/ and https://hotelutah.com/calendar/, made by the SeeTickets WordPress plugin in the page itself (no iframe). Show 4:00PM' falls on Halloween; 'Sun Oct 25, Free All-Ages 3PM Matinee!, Show 3:00PM' has a header that matches its time; the REST post of an 'OPEN MIC NIGHT' row was created on 2026-05-01 but is listed for Mon Oct 19, so the listed date is the event date, not the publish date. - Terms (16 Oct 2026 UTC; the archive copies read 18 Oct): silent. The footer 'Privacy Policy' link (https://hotelutah.com/terms-and-conditions/) holds only the stock WordPress privacy text ('PRIVACY POLICY / Who we are / Our website address is: https://hotelutah.com.'), with no clause on copying, reproduction, republishing, personal use or automated access. The footer reads 'Copyright 2024 | The Hotel Utah Saloon | Privacy Policy', a bare notice (Amendment 14), on the live site and in all 16 copies. A text search of every page fetched from the venue host found no 'reproduc', 'republish', 'personal use', 'scrap' or 'crawl'; a second check re-read the terms page and the homepage live and found none either. Checked: https://hotelutah.com/ (footer); https://hotelutah.com/calendar/; https://hotelutah.com/terms-and-conditions/; https://hotelutah.com/utah-booking-request/
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress (7.1.3, Avada theme) on GoDaddy Managed WordPress hosting; GoDaddy is a host, whose terms bind its account holder, not visitors. The listing is the SeeTickets plugin's HTML; tickets are sold on wl.seetickets.us (afflky=HotelUtahSaloon), the ticketing vendor only, and no SeeTickets page was read. Not Fatsoma, Squarespace, Wix, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer on the live site or in any copy (the one 'wix' match is inside a base64 image URL). No bot check and no 403 in front of the listings: every venue-host request was a 200, and the Cloudflare Turnstile and reCAPTCHA strings belong to the contact form plugin. All 16 web.archive.org copies are real SeeTickets listings with HTTP 200, none a challenge, error or empty page.
- Operator: the owner is not identified (Amendment 14): the site's history section names only past owners, private persons, and the footer names only 'The Hotel Utah Saloon'; no company owner is named. Booking is in-house: the booking page (https://hotelutah.com/utah-booking-request/) asks acts to 'fill out the form below' to 'play our stage', refers to 'our booking team' and gives the venue's own booking address (play@hotelutah.com). No page on the live site or in the 16 copies names Live Nation, AEG, Goldenvoice, Another Planet or an outside booker, and ticketing is SeeTickets, not Ticketmaster or AXS. Press and company records were not checked (the scouting and second-check sessions had no web search left), so §1 rests on the venue's own pages, booking contact and ticketing. Outside presenters named on counted rows (event-header field): 'CORE Presents' (16 Apr) and 'HHV presents' (24 Apr), 1 row each of the 69 counted S1 and S2 rows, far below half; neither is identified further. A third 'HHV presents' row is in March, outside the periods. No company presents more than half, so there is no outside house booker.
- Rooms: one. The venue field on all 111 distinct listed events in the copies, inside and outside the periods, reads 'at The Hotel Utah Saloon', the building's own name, and the page has no room filter; 'The loft' is named only for private hire. It is read as the single room of a one-room venue (as Martyrs'), not as a split building's pool room. Capacity 79: 'The Utah has a capacity of 79.' (https://hotelutah.com/utah-booking-request/).
- Coverage: S1 >=44, S2 25 (by month Apr 15, May 13, Jun 16; Jul 11, Aug 5, Sep 9; union by SeeTickets event id of the 16 web.archive.org copies, the latest copy that shows a row winning; settled on 18 Oct 2026 from saved copies after web.archive.org stopped the 16-17 Oct check, with 0 requests by the settle or its check). Listed rows: S1 52, S2 38. Removed by Amendment 4 words: 21 (S1 8, S2 13): 20 weekly open-mic rows ('Open Mic at Hotel Utah' 18 May to 29 Jun, 'OPEN MIC NIGHT' 6 Jul to 28 Sep) and 1 S1 row (22 May) whose band name holds the word 'class'. No row is canceled, off-site or untitled. The independent check corrected the settle report on one point: the 19 May /calendar/?sepage=2 copy has a month grid with an 'Open Mic at Hotel Utah' entry on 18 May that the settle missed, so S1 listed is 52, not 51, removed 21, not 20, and the S1 gap is 15-17 May, not 15-18 May; the counts do not change. Gaps: no copy covers 15-17 May; 1-7 Apr is seen only in the 10-16 Mar copies, and 30 Jun to 24 Sep only in copies dated 29 Jun or earlier (the CDX lists no capture of any of the six pages between 20260629230033 and 20260925090432), so shows added later are not seen in those stretches. S1 is a lower bound. S2 is not a strict lower bound (the check's reading, which wins over the settle report's): a cancellation made after 29 Jun would not show, the margin is 5 and no copy lies in between, so 25 is the best available count, not proven >= 20. 13 of the 25 counted S2 rows are the weekly 'Open Bluegrass Jam' (no performers, no time), which no Amendment 4 word removes; without them S2 would be 12. The 25 Sep copy and the live October list still carry the weekly jam and open mic. Three counted rows are stand-up comedy by their header or subtitle, not their title (19 Apr fundraiser; 15 Jul 'LAUGHS ON THE ROCKS' bill; 25 Sep 'ALIENS EXIST Film Premiere', subtitle 'Stand Up Comedy Sets'); Amendment 4 is a word rule on the title, so they count, and with header and subtitle words S1 would be 43 and S2 24. Copies of 10 Mar to 22 Apr list 9-14 rows (to 14 May); copies of 19 May to 8 Jun list 50 rows a page, with 5 more on ?sepage=2; the 29 Jun copy lists 45 rows (to 3 Nov) and the 25 Sep copy 18. The six unpicked captures of / sit within hours of picked copies, so no further capture is needed.
- §5: San Francisco also has Bottom of the Hill (`sf-bottom`), Make-Out Room (`sf-makeout`) and Yoshi's (`sf-yoshis`), other clusters, so §5 passes. §6: San Francisco is not in Study 1.
