### The Lost Church (`sf-lostchurch`, San Francisco)
- Source: The venue's own WordPress HTML listing pages: https://thelostchurch.org/san-francisco/ for the San Francisco building (988 Columbus Ave), https://thelostchurch.org/santa-rosa/ for its second building in Santa Rosa (see the last item), and the homepage, whose headed blocks ('UPCOMING SHOWS - SAN FRANCISCO', then 'SANTA ROSA') list each building's coming shows (9 per building in the copies from 15 Apr on). Each row is a 'performances_performance' block with a title (h1), a ticket link carrying a Salesforce event-instance id (ticket#/instances/<id>) and a month/day/year calendar block. The live pages list coming shows only (on 16 Oct: San Francisco 32 rows, 16 Oct 2026 to Mar 2027), and the site has no events post type, REST feed or iCal (its WP types are only post, page, attachment and ai_playlist), so S1/S2 rows come from web.archive.org copies of the own pages.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://thelostchurch.org/san-francisco/ for ts 20260309054551, 20260406220414, 20260611130931, 20260630103904, 20260820091549; .../https://thelostchurch.org/ for 20260311032330, 20260401175509, 20260415030937, 20260526224847, 20260611124408, 20260629170946, 20260820091530; .../https://thelostchurch.org/santa-rosa/ for 20260401175339, 20260406012631, 20260629152824, 20260820091549 (16 copies, all HTTP 200, gzip-encoded bodies; the 12 San Francisco page and homepage copies give the San Francisco rows); the CDX lists of /san-francisco/ (9 captures, 5 picked), / (11 captures, 7 picked) and /santa-rosa/ (4 captures, all picked), 1 Mar to 15 Oct 2026; live (16 Oct 2026 UTC, for dates, terms and operator only, no S1/S2 row): https://thelostchurch.org/ , /san-francisco/ , /santa-rosa/ , /about/ , /book-a-show/ , /faq/ , /srmove/ , /terms/ , /sitemap_index.xml , /page-sitemap.xml , /wp-json/wp/v2/types , /wp-json/wp/v2/pages?slug=terms-and-privacy
- Dates checked: These are show dates. Each row carries its own month/day/year block, and the union by instance id followed five re-datings between captures (the latest capture wins), four of them in San Francisco: one show moved from 1 May to 17 Oct ('NEW DATE'), one listed 'CANCELED' for 27 May was re-listed under the same id on 13 Jul, one moved from 27 Sep to 1 Nov and one from 4 Jul to 13 Jun; the ones moved out of 1 Apr-30 Sep leave the window. On the live lists (16 Oct) the Litquake literary nights fall on 16-23 Oct, inside that festival's dates, and one Santa Rosa row of 25 Oct is marked 'CANCELED', so each row is a dated performance. The FAQ gives '7:30PM DOORS OPEN' for evening shows. The listings give a date and no time, so Amendment 3 keeps the listed date. The independent check found 8 random rows in the copies their source_url names, with the same date, title and room; 5 are San Francisco rows (2 May from the 6 Apr page, 4 Jun from the 26 May homepage block, 13 Jul and 21 Jul from the 30 Jun page, 19 Sep from the 20 Aug page).
- robots.txt (16 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://thelostchurch.org/robots.txt: 'User-agent: * / Disallow: /wp/wp-admin/ / Allow: /wp/wp-admin/admin-ajax.php', plus a Sitemap line. No Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every venue URL read; web.archive.org allowed the snapshot and CDX paths.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026 UTC; the archive copies read 18 Oct): none-found. /terms/ leads to /terms-and-privacy/, a page with a heading and no text (https://thelostchurch.org/wp-json/wp/v2/pages?slug=terms-and-privacy, modified 2021-07-20; 394 characters of HTML). The footer reads '501(c) 3 Non-Profit Arts Organization ©2021 The Lost Church', a bare notice (Amendment 14), on the live site and in all 16 copies; the independent check found no terms text in any copy. Checked: https://thelostchurch.org/terms/ ; https://thelostchurch.org/terms-of-use/ (404); https://thelostchurch.org/terms-of-service/ (404); https://thelostchurch.org/terms-and-conditions/ (404); https://thelostchurch.org/legal/ (404); https://thelostchurch.org/privacy/ (404); https://thelostchurch.org/ (footer); https://thelostchurch.org/faq/
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress 6.4.2 (custom theme, Yoast sitemaps). Not Fatsoma, Squarespace, Wix, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer on the live site or in any copy. Tickets are sold on the venue's own Salesforce site (thelostchurch.my.salesforce-sites.com), which is not a source and was not read; there is no Ticketmaster or AXS link. No bot check and no 403 in front of the listings: every venue-host request answered 200 or 404 (the terms paths), and all 16 copies are real listing pages ('San Francisco - The Lost Church', 'Home - The Lost Church', 'Santa Rosa Re-opening April 2026' / 'Now Open - TLC Santa Rosa') with no captcha, challenge or 403 marker. The scouting host table marks thelostchurch.org 'unclear', not bot-check.
- Operator (§1, Amendment 14): Owner: THELOSTCHURCH.ORG, INC, a 501(c)(3) nonprofit arts organisation ('THELOSTCHURCH.ORG, INC is a tax exempt 501(c) 3 Non-Profit Arts Organization', homepage footer; 'We are a 501 (c)(3) nonprofit arts organization that serves the community by creating, sustaining, and defending spaces for live performance.', https://thelostchurch.org/about/), run by its co-founders, private owners. House booker: the venue itself. https://thelostchurch.org/book-a-show/: 'Interested in booking a show? ... read through this entire page', 'The Lost Church books shows approx. 3 months in advance. Email booking@thelostchurch.org', 'Our Booking Agent will get back to you', door deal '70% Artists / 30% TLC'. No outside booker is named, and ticketing is the venue's own Salesforce site, not Ticketmaster or AXS. No page on the live site or in the 16 copies names Live Nation or AEG, and the press seen in scouting (SF Chronicle datebook, ebar.com, a 48hills archive) describes a founder-run nonprofit. The second check's web search ran out after one search (SF Chronicle datebook, KRCB/NorCal Public Media, SF Station, Bohemian, Creative Sonoma), which named no Live Nation, AEG or Another Planet link, so a recent-news check for a booking deal is incomplete; §1 rests on the venue's own pages, booking contact and ticketing and on that press (Amendment 14, public evidence). Outside presenters named on counted San Francisco rows: SongRise Studios 1 (S1) and ENORCHESTRA 1 (S2), 2 of the 77 counted rows; one more S1 row is presented by a name that is only a private person's name (counted, not written; `presented_by` leaves it out). None is Live Nation or AEG and none presents more than half of the room's rows, so there is no outside house booker and none joins a cluster.
- Rooms: one. The source splits the two buildings by page (/san-francisco/, /santa-rosa/) and by headed homepage block, not into rooms; each building has a single stage. No row is given both buildings, no instance id moves between them, and the title suffixes ('SF', 'San Francisco') agree with the assigned building in every row (independent check). Under Amendments 1 and 12 each building is a venue of its own, and the two are one cluster (same owner and booker). The San Francisco room is the single room of a one-room venue (as Martyrs' and Hotel Utah Saloon), so it is a test room, not a split building's pool room; no row lacks a building. Capacity 132: 'Capacity: 132 (125 seated + 7 standing)' (https://thelostchurch.org/book-a-show/, San Francisco section).
- Coverage: S1 >=40, S2 >=37 (lower bounds; by month Apr 14, May 9, Jun 17; Jul 15, Aug 10, Sep 12). Settled on 18 Oct 2026 from saved copies, after web.archive.org was stopped for good in the fetcher during the 16-17 Oct check (second 429, 00:38 on 17 Oct server time), which had left the venue undecided; the copies were fetched by the prefetch on 18 Oct under the 30 s archive rule and read offline, and the settle and its check made 0 requests. Rows are the union by Salesforce instance id of the 12 copies that hold San Francisco rows, the latest capture winning. Listed rows in the window: S1 46, S2 43 (2 Apr to 27 Sep). Removed by the Amendment 4 words: 12 (S1 6: comedy 5, private event 1; S2 6: comedy 4, private event 2). After the union no row is canceled, off-site or untitled (the 27 May 'CANCELED' row is the show re-listed on 13 Jul). Several counted rows are non-music (burlesque, recitals, magic, improv, readings) with no Amendment 4 word; the rule keeps them. No San Francisco night has two counted rows. The independent check recounted 40 / 37 with its own parser, matched `shows.csv` row for row (three titles differ only by a double space that `shows.csv` keeps) and upheld the verdict. Its corrections, which win over the settle report, leave these counts unchanged: the report's statement that no row listed in one capture is missing from a later capture covering its date is false for one Santa Rosa row (see the last item), and besides the 20260608015501 page capture and the 20260517203441 homepage capture that the report names as unpicked, the 20260406012631, 20260413114934 and 20260602071937 homepage captures were not picked either (the other unpicked San Francisco page captures are 20260406012547, 20260406012631 and 20260629152830). Gaps: every date from 1 Apr to 30 Sep lies inside some used capture's window (capture date to its last listed show), but the pages list only shows on sale at capture time, so shows booked later can be missing where the newest covering capture is more than 4 weeks older than the show: 5-25 May and 7-10 Jun (newest page copy 6 Apr; the 26 May homepage block lists only 9 shows), 29 Jul-19 Aug (30 Jun page) and 18-30 Sep (20 Aug page). The CDX lists no capture of any of the three pages between 30 Jun and 20 Aug or after 20 Aug 2026. S1 rows come from the 1 Apr, 15 Apr and 26 May homepage blocks (3, 9 and 8 listed rows) and the 6 Apr and 11 Jun pages (14 and 12); S2 rows from the 30 Jun and 20 Aug pages (23 and 20).
- §5 and §6: the San Francisco building is in the San Francisco-Oakland-Fremont MSA (OMB Bulletin 23-01; the record gives the earlier name San Francisco-Oakland-Berkeley). Bottom of the Hill (`sf-bottom`), Make-Out Room (`sf-makeout`) and Yoshi's (`sf-yoshis`) are other clusters in that metro, so §5 passes. §6: San Francisco is not in Study 1, and the room is not an Amendment 11 room.
- Programme: a mixed nonprofit performance space; the booking page names 'music, comedy, theater, film, literary events, improv, cabaret, spoken word, dance and more'. This is only a description; nothing was filtered by it.
- Requests during the eligibility check: thelostchurch.org 24 (requests.log, 16 Oct 2026 UTC, 23:16-23:21: 19 in scouting, where the record gives 18, and 5 in the second check; 2 of them robots.txt reads; 19 at 200 and 5 at 404, the terms paths), web.archive.org 19 on 18 Oct 2026 UTC (04:01-04:10: 3 CDX queries and the 16 copies, fetched by the prefetch 30 s apart, all 200); the 16-17 Oct check sent none to the archive, which was stopped. The settle and its check made no request.
- Santa Rosa building (not in `venues.csv`): The Lost Church's second building, 545 Mendocino Ave, Santa Rosa (it moved there from the old Ross Street site and opened on 10 Apr 2026, https://thelostchurch.org/srmove/; the 1 Apr copy of /santa-rosa/ already gives the new address, so no row is off-site), was settled in the same run: S1 >=26, S2 >=34 (lower bounds; 11 rows removed by the Amendment 4 words: open mic 5, comedy 5, karaoke 1). The independent check found that the 26 May row 'A Benefit Concert for Ukraine: Kommuna Lux' appears under one id on the 1 Apr and 6 Apr copies and under a new id on the 26 May homepage, one show issued again and kept as two rows; merged, S1 is 25, still above 20. Capacity 75 (https://thelostchurch.org/book-a-show/). It has the same owner and booker as the San Francisco building, so it is the same cluster, and it lies in the Santa Rosa-Petaluma MSA, a metro of its own with no eligible venue of another cluster, so it fails §5. It is not used, is not in `venues.csv` and feeds no pool.
- Note for compiling: rows come from the settle rows with venue_id 'thelostchurch-org:San Francisco' renamed to `sf-lostchurch`; the Santa Rosa rows are left out. `drop_reason` is empty on all 12 San Francisco rows that carry an Amendment 4 word, so the words must be applied at compile (Amendment 12 counts rows with none of the words, which gives 40 / 37; a count by `drop_reason` alone gives 46 / 43). `fetched_at` holds the capture date, not the request date (independent check); counts are not affected.

### The Garrison (`tor-garrison-*`, Toronto)
- Source: Static hand-coded HTML calendar on the venue's own site: https://garrisontoronto.com/listings.html, and the same calendar_wrap markup on the homepage. Each row has an h1 date block (weekday, month, day; no year), a headliner div (the title), a support div and a separate doors/cover span (start time, price and, on some rows, the room). The live page lists only coming rows (60 rows, 14 Oct 2026 to 29 Mar 2027), so S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of that same own page and of the homepage. The year is taken from the copy's date.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/ copies of http(s)://(www.)garrisontoronto.com/listings.html for ts 20260301063037, 20260413231652, 20260526133815, 20260608023550, 20260701205758, 20260715103846, 20260815132637, 20260915205426, and of the homepage for ts 20260316053207, 20260318180211, 20260413232222, 20260424120655, 20260531072503, 20260608170415, 20260717201031 (15 copies, 52 to 74 rows each); the CDX lists of garrisontoronto.com/listings.html and garrisontoronto.com/; live https://garrisontoronto.com/listings.html, /, /info.html, /menu.html and /contact.html for date, terms and operator checks only. The 20260523222503 listings copy, saved during scouting on 16 Oct, was not used for counting.
- Dates checked: The h1 block carries the show date and the doors/cover span its start time. TRAMPOLINE HALL: 'MONDAY MAY 11', '7:PM' (24 Apr homepage copy). 4am LAST CALL rows: '12:AM ~ 4:AM', a start of exactly 00:00, so the listed date is used (Amendment 3); 64 counted rows start at 00:00. Three Front Room rows (10, 17 and 24 Jun) read 'After the show ~ 4:AM' or '~ 4:AM', no clock time, and keep the listed date. SECOND SUMMER FESTIVAL is headed 'THURSDAY SEPTEMBER 24 - SATURDAY SEPTEMBER 26' with 'TBD doors' in the copies up to 15 Aug; the 15 Sep copy lists it as three day rows ('6:PM doors', '8:PM', '7:PM'), counted as three nights. The live page (16 Oct) gives its first rows (14, 16 and 18 Oct) the same way, each with its start time. Thumbnail file names carry stale date prefixes (some 2025), so dates come from the h1 block only; day numbers are sometimes typed with a letter o ('o5'). Of the 719 row copies dated in S1/S2 across the 15 copies, the weekday disagrees with 2026 only on 'MONDAY MAY o5' (5 May 2026 is a Tuesday; either date is in S1), 'SATURDAY JUNE 16' (the duplicate row the review took out, see Coverage) and the festival range heading; 'SATUDAY JUNE o6' is a misspelt Saturday. These are show dates.
- robots.txt (15-16 Oct 2026): allowed. https://garrisontoronto.com/robots.txt returns 404, so every path is allowed; there is no Anthropic group. polite.py allowed every venue page read and the web.archive.org copies.
- Terms (16 Oct 2026): none-found. There is no terms, legal or privacy page and no copyright notice: the homepage, listings, info, menu and contact pages carry no ©, 'rights reserved', terms link or copy, republish or personal-use text, and none of the 15 archive copies has such text either (checked by the settle and again by its review). Checked: https://garrisontoronto.com/; https://garrisontoronto.com/listings.html; https://garrisontoronto.com/info.html; https://garrisontoronto.com/menu.html; https://garrisontoronto.com/contact.html; https://garrisontoronto.com/terms (404); https://garrisontoronto.com/terms-of-use (404); https://garrisontoronto.com/terms-of-service (404); https://garrisontoronto.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://garrisontoronto.com/legal (404); https://garrisontoronto.com/privacy (404); https://garrisontoronto.com/privacy-policy (404); https://garrisontoronto.com/sitemap.xml (404)
- Platform: hand-coded static HTML on Apache (prototype.js, scriptaculous, lightbox; no generator tag). No site builder: no Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Weebly, Webflow or WordPress marker and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer, live or in any copy, so no platform terms apply. No bot check and no 403: every venue-host request returned 200 or 404, and all 15 archive bodies are the venue's own calendar (status 200), none a check, challenge or error page. Tickets link out to DICE, Ticketmaster, TicketWeb, AdmitOne, Eventbrite, Universe and others.
- Operator (§1): owner: a private owner-operator (named in https://nowtoronto.com/music/the-baby-g-opens-on-dundas-west/, NOW, 31 Aug 2016, which says the owner opened the Garrison in 2009; a blogTO article, refused to us by its robots.txt, is known only from a search snippet calling the owner 'owner operator ... having founded the venue in 2009'). The same owner runs the sister club The Baby G, which is not in `venues.csv` (no usable copies), so it joins no cluster here. House booker: none outside the house. The venue names only its own booking contact ('Booking inquiries booking@garrisontoronto.com', https://garrisontoronto.com/contact.html), and no company presents more than half of either room's counted rows. Outside presenters named in titles (the review's count, which corrects the settle's): DEPARTURE 2026 with RANGE MAGAZINE, 1 house row (7 May); NXNE with Billboard Canada LIVE, 1 house row (11 Jun, 'NXNE x Billboard Canada LIVE present', which the settle left without `presented_by`); TRANSMIT, 3 rows (5-7 Jun, off-site, not counted). That is 2 of the house room's 161 counted rows and none of the Front Room's. Ticket links are not presenters: ticketmaster.ca 24 and ticketweb.ca 21 of the settle's 182 counted rows, together under half. No public source read names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake, house booker or sale; the ownership evidence dates from 2016 and 2021, and the eligibility review could not run a news search for later changes. The owner is the operator.
- Rooms: the doors/cover span is a field of its own, apart from the headliner div, so a room it names is a room (Amendment 14). It names 'front room' ('No cover in front room') on the 12:AM 4am LAST CALL rows in the copies up to 31 May (last such row 31 May) and on the three Wednesday 'GARRIOKE' after-show rows of 10, 17 and 24 Jun; every other 4am LAST CALL row from 4 Jun on reads only 'No cover', and no S2 row in any copy names a room. Rows with no room, which are all the main-room concerts, go to the house room `tor-garrison-house`, a pool room of the same building. NOW 2016 says The Baby G, 'like the Garrison, will have a bar in the front and a live music space in the back'.
- Coverage: S1 102, S2 78 for the building, lower bounds; by room Front Room 19 / 0 and house room 83 / 78. Settled on 18 Oct 2026 from saved web.archive.org copies, after web.archive.org stopped the 16-17 Oct check (the eligibility record had S1 and S2 unknown). The settle wrote 107 S1 and 80 S2 rows (union of the 15 copies by date and show URL, the latest copy giving the fields); 3 S1 rows are off-site (TRANSMIT PRESENTS @ DO WEST FEST, 5-7 Jun, 'Free across from The Garrison') and 2 S2 rows are marked ***POSTPONED*** (17 and 22 Sep), giving 104 / 78 (house 85 / 78). The independent review corrected this to 102 / 78 (house 83 / 78), and its count is used: two S1 house rows are one show listed under another date when the same show URL is followed across copies (a show first listed for 30 Apr and moved to 27 Aug; a 16 Jun row in one 18 Mar homepage copy that every later copy lists as 13 Jun). No title has an Amendment 4 word ('GARRIOKE' is support text on a 4am LAST CALL row, not the word karaoke); TRAMPOLINE HALL, a talk night, counts under the literal rule. The no-cover 4am LAST CALL DJ nights count: Front Room 19 / 0, house 20 / 30; without them the house room has 63 / 48. Front Room's 19 are 16-19 and 23-26 Apr, 21-24 and 28-31 May, and 10, 17 and 24 Jun. Not exact: 4am LAST CALL rows and other short-notice additions are listed only 1-3 weeks ahead, and no used copy covers at that horizon 1-12 Apr, 27 Apr-20 May, 1-3 Jun, 6-13 Jul, 20 Jul-12 Aug, 31 Aug-10 Sep and 21-30 Sep. The listings captures 20260402155635, 20260517204718, 20260523222503 (saved in scouting, not used), 20260806192017 and 20260904083705 would fill part of this; the other captures not used (listings 20260915031811, a same-day twin of 20260915205426, and homepage 20260605013749 and 20260318132218) add no gap day, as the review found; 27 Apr-16 May, 6-13 Jul, 20 Jul-5 Aug, 31 Aug-3 Sep and 21-30 Sep have no capture at all. Seven S1/S2 rows (2 May, 27 May, 17 Jun, 19 Jun, 5 Aug, 21 Aug, 5 Sep) are missing, with no cancel mark, from later copies that cover their date; they are kept under the union rule, and without them the house room has 79 / 75 and the building 98 / 75. Role: the house room meets 20 / 20 but holds the rows whose room is not given, so it is a pool room (Amendment 14); Front Room fails S2 and cannot reach 20 from the unread S2 captures (they sit between copies that name no room). The venue is eligible with role `pool` and no test room. The programme is mostly music (indie, rock, punk and touring acts), with the late DJ nights and a few party, talk and community nights.
- §5: Toronto (Statistics Canada CMA) has eligible venues in other clusters (`tor-horseshoe`, `tor-tranzac`), so §5 holds. §6: Toronto is not in Study 1.
- Capacity: none published by the venue. The owner is quoted in press as saying 'The Garrison holds 300' (NOW 2016), and exclaim.ca gives about 270 (not fetched); neither is the venue's or operator's own figure, so the rooms are "size unknown" (Amendment 8).
- Requests during the eligibility check: garrisontoronto.com 23 lines in requests.log, 2 of them robots.txt reads (3 on 15 Oct UTC and 2 on 16 Oct before the check; 14 in the check and 4 in its review, 16 Oct 22:51-22:55 UTC); nowtoronto.com 2 (robots.txt and the 2016 article); www.blogto.com 1 robots.txt read and 1 refusal logged without a request; web.archive.org 3 during scouting on 16 Oct UTC (two CDX lists and the 23 May listings copy, not used for counting) and 17 on 18 Oct 01:54-02:02 UTC, when the saved copies were fetched (two CDX lists and the 15 copies read). The settle and its review read only those saved copies and made no request.

### Lee's Palace and Dance Cave (`tor-leespalace-*`, Toronto)
- Source: HTML of the venue's own Webflow pages. The home page carries an 'Upcoming Shows' collection list: each row has an /event/<slug> link, the title, a weekday date and a separate 'Venue:' field (Lee's Palace or Dance Cave). Every /event/ page carries an 'Other Events' list in the same row format, so its copies act as listing snapshots too, plus an own-event block with date, time and room. The live site and the venue's S3 feed (https://leesplaceeventsbucket.s3.amazonaws.com/events.json, a Webflow CMS export with a matching 'venue-name' field) hold only coming shows (65 rows from 16 Oct 2026); past event pages are deleted (/event/black-marble-lees answers 404) and the sitemap lists only 68 coming /event/ pages. So S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of the own home page and /event/ pages.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://www.leespalace.com/ for ts 20260301235439, 20260315043820, 20260401163828, 20260415165555, 20260501163705, 20260515163829, 20260523201213, 20260815040417, 20260831055726, 20260912163722, 20260924163848 (11 copies); 60 copies of /event/<slug> pages captured 5 Mar to 6 Jun 2026 (60 slugs on 5 base slugs: 36 'a-wilhelm-scream' and 21 'angelmaker-canadian-heritage' variants, and one page each of three other shows), picked for their 'Other Events' lists from the CDX prefix list of www.leespalace.com/event/ (2,085 rows); CDX lists of www.leespalace.com/ (195 rows, 11 picked) and www.leespalace.com/event/ (prefix), and the earlier whole-site CDX listing of leespalace.com/ (prefix) and the CDX list of leespalace.com/lees-calendar from scouting. All 71 copies are real Webflow pages with listings of 48 to 100 rows; two of them (the 15 Aug home copy and one 26 Apr event page) are gzip bodies that decode to full pages. Live pages (15-16 Oct 2026), for date, terms and operator checks only: https://www.leespalace.com/, /lees-calendar, /the-dance-cave-calendar, /events, /sitemap.xml, /faqs, /booking, /contact, and the S3 events.json.
- Dates checked: These are show start dates, local. All 65 rows of the live S3 feed, with event-starts converted to America/Toronto, give the date shown on the live home page row (0 mismatches); for example witch-please-a-spooky-rave 2026-10-17T01:00Z = 'Friday, October 16, 2026', pounds-of-sin-2026 2026-10-18T00:00Z = 'Saturday, October 17, 2026'. In the copies, the weekday of every counted row fits its 2026 date (0 mismatches, the independent check). The own-event block of the 26 Apr 2026 copy of an A Wilhelm Scream page reads 'Saturday, April 25, 2026 | 8:00 pm | 19+ | Dance Cave', the date and room its row gives. Listing rows give the date only; event-page times are 6 to 9 pm, so Amendment 3 moves no date.
- robots.txt (15-16 Oct 2026): allowed. https://www.leespalace.com/robots.txt: 'User-Agent: * / Disallow: (empty) / Disallow: /talent/ / Disallow: /talent/*' with a sitemap line; there is no Anthropic group, and polite.py allowed every page used. The bare leespalace.com host's robots.txt was unreachable, so polite.py treated that host as disallowed and it was not used. The S3 feed host answered robots.txt with S3's 403 AccessDenied for a missing object, which polite.py reads as allow-all; events.json then answered 200 (see Platform). web.archive.org allowed the CDX and copy paths (16 and 18 Oct 2026).
- Terms (15-16 Oct 2026): none-found. The venue site has no terms, legal or privacy page and no copyright line; its footer reads only 'Powered by [Webflow link] [Patron Technology logo]'. The independent check of the settle found no terms, copyright or personal-use text in any of the 71 copies. The operator's site carries a stock WordPress privacy text with no use licence and a bare '© 2025 Modo Live, All rights reserved.' (Amendment 14: not terms). Checked: https://www.leespalace.com/ (footer); https://www.leespalace.com/terms (404); https://www.leespalace.com/terms-of-use (404); https://www.leespalace.com/terms-of-service (404); https://www.leespalace.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://www.leespalace.com/legal (404); https://www.leespalace.com/privacy (404); https://www.leespalace.com/privacy-policy (404); https://www.leespalace.com/faqs, /booking, /contact, /events, /lees-calendar, /the-dance-cave-calendar (no copying, reuse or personal-use text); https://modo-live.com/privacy-policy/; https://leapevent.tech/legal/privacy-policy/ (data privacy only); https://leapevent.tech/legal/terms-of-use/ (404); https://leapevent.tech/legal/terms-of-service/ (404); https://events.leapevents.com/terms (404)
- Platform: Webflow (data-wf-site markup, data-wf-domain=www.leespalace.com, assets on cdn.prod.website-files.com, footer 'Powered by' linking to webflow.com), which passes as read for Horseshoe Tavern (reading 4). The same footer carries a Patron Technology logo image, not a link; Patron Technology is now Leap Event Technology (patrontechnology.com redirects to leapevent.tech), and tickets are sold on events.leapevents.com, a ShowClix whitelabel. Leap's privacy policy refers to 'our Terms' for its Services, but Leap publishes no terms of use (the three terms URLs above answer 404). Leap does not host the site, so under Amendment 14 its terms would not be the site's terms; it is not the 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' line of reading 4, which is in no copy. Recorded as a note, as decided at the settle. No bot check and no 403 from www.leespalace.com, live (only 200 and 404) or in any copy. The only 403 came from the S3 feed host's robots.txt (a missing key with bucket listing off); it is not the venue host and the feed is not used for any S1/S2 row, so reading 5 does not apply.
- Operator (§1, Amendment 14): Owner: MODO-LIVE (Modo Live), a Canadian independent promoter and venue operator, since December 2023 (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee%27s_Palace: owner 'Collective Concerts (2006-2023) / Modo-Live (2023-present)'; https://ca.billboard.com/music/concerts/lees-palace-40th-anniversary: 'Lee's Palace was bought by independent Canadian promoters/venue operators MODO-LIVE last year'). House booker: MODO-LIVE, named by the venue: https://www.leespalace.com/booking ('For all booking inquiries email' a modo-live.com address of a MODO-LIVE staff member) and https://www.leespalace.com/contact ('Bookings:' the same address); MODO-LIVE's own site (https://modo-live.com/) lists the Lee's Palace and Dance Cave shows. So the operator is MODO-LIVE for both rooms, and the rooms join MODO-LIVE's cluster `modo-live` with The Pearl (`van-pearl`, Vancouver). Ticketing is Leap/ShowClix, not Ticketmaster or AXS, and no copy has any Live Nation, AEG, Rockhouse, Etix, Ticketmaster or AXS text. No public source read names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake or house booker; the eligibility check could not run a further search on MODO-LIVE's investors (search budget used up). Outside presenters on counted rows: Tawoos 1 (Lee's Palace, S2, 22 Aug, 'Tawoos Presents: LMNL'), 1 of the room's 103 counted rows; the only other one named, QuotedTO, presents one 22 Aug row that the Amendment 4 word 'market' removes. Dance Cave has none. None presents more than half of a room's rows, so none is a house booker and none joins a cluster. Both go into `presented_by`; under the literal reading of Amendment 1 (Amendment 14, last item) Tawoos is an operator of the Lee's Palace room in that report, and neither is Live Nation or AEG. 'Collective Concerts', the former owner, appears only in a stale site-wide refund notice.
- Rooms: two, from the separate 'Venue:' field of each listing row, outside the title (the S3 feed's 'venue-name' field and the separate calendar pages per room agree): Lee's Palace (`tor-leespalace-main`) and Dance Cave (`tor-leespalace-dancecave`). Their building in the proposed rows (`venues-b5-rows.csv`; not in `venues.csv`) is 'Lee's Palace and Dance Cave', as the venue's FAQ names the pair ('Tickets to events at Lee's Palace and Dance Cave'), not 'Lee's Palace': a room named after its building is the house room (Amendment 14), and the main room is a test room. Undated sidebar rows with no room all map to a main-list show, so no row is left without a room and there is no house room. No Venue field names another building, so nothing is off-site. Second Summer Festival (24-26 Sep) is six items, one per night per room, so 3 rows per room.
- Coverage: Lee's Palace S1 >=54, S2 >=49; Dance Cave S1 >=31, S2 >=27; building S1 >=85, S2 >=76; lower bounds (the settle report and the settle `shows.csv`, 162 rows: 104 at `leespalace-com:Lee's Palace`, 58 at `leespalace-com:Dance Cave`). Settled on 18 Oct 2026 from saved web.archive.org copies (fetched on 18 Oct, 02:19-02:55 UTC, and read offline), after web.archive.org stopped the 16-17 Oct check, which had only the one 15 May copy (Lee's Palace >=26 / >=24, Dance Cave >=9 / >=7) and left the venue undecided with Dance Cave's role open. Rows are unioned by base slug and listed date, the latest copy that lists a show giving its fields: the Webflow CMS re-creates items under new slugs with a 5-hex suffix (up to about 40 variants of one show across the April copies), and no base slug has two dates. Removed by the Amendment 4 words: 1 (Lee's Palace, S2, 22 Aug 'QuotedTO Presents: SOUNDCLASH + Vintage Market', market). No row is marked canceled or postponed, none is off-site and none is untitled. An independent check recounted all 71 copies with its own scripts and got the same counts (its row set equals the 162 rows; a second key on date, title and room gives the same numbers); it corrected no count and upheld both verdicts. Nine counted rows were listed and later dropped, with no cancel mark, from a copy taken before their date: Dance Cave S1 1 (15 May); Lee's Palace S1 3 (4 Jun twice, 19 Jun) and S2 5 (22 Aug, 9, 18, 20 and 30 Sep). They are kept here (canceled 0), as for The Garrison; the compile note for Horseshoe Tavern and The Pearl leaves such rows out, and without them the rooms have Lee's Palace 51 / 44 and Dance Cave 30 / 27, so coverage and both roles hold either way. Not exact: each copy shows only what was announced by its date. Picked copies fall on 1, 5 and 15 Mar, 1 Apr, 15-26 Apr (daily), 1-18 May (near daily), 23 May, 6 Jun, 15 and 31 Aug, 12 and 24 Sep. The settle report says no capture of any kind exists from 7 Jun to 14 Aug; the independent check corrected this, and its reading is used: the whole-site CDX holds 3 captures in that window (/event/the-zolas 1 Jul, /the-dance-cave-calendar 14 Jul and 3 Aug; the calendar pages may hold no rows, as they draw them by JavaScript), none of them picked. The smaller lags (up to 14-16 days: 2-14 Apr, 24 May-5 Jun, 16-30 Aug, 1-11, 13-23 and 25-30 Sep) are lags of the picked copies only: the home-page CDX holds unpicked captures in 2-14 Apr and 5 in 16-23 Aug. Rows that no later copy can confirm: 28 S2 rows dated 1 Jul-14 Aug were last seen in the 6 Jun copies (Lee's Palace 20, Dance Cave 8; the independent check's 30, with Lee's Palace 22, also takes in the 22 Aug and 20 Sep Lee's Palace rows that vanished from the 15 Aug copy); Dance Cave S2 would fall below 20 only if all 8 of its rows were canceled, against 9 of 162 rows seen to vanish. The CDX holds a 6 Jun capture of /?ca28b0e4_page=2, but no copy shows a pagination link and every home list is under 100 items; two 15 Apr event-page lists are capped at 100 items and repeat rows (used for the union, not for the vanish test). Both rooms meet coverage by themselves, so both are test rooms in the `modo-live` cluster (Amendment 12).
- §5 and §6: Toronto (Statistics Canada CMA) has eligible venues in other clusters (`tor-horseshoe`, `tor-tranzac`), so §5 holds. Toronto is not in Study 1 and neither room is an Amendment 11 room. Toronto sorts after the cut, so the rooms are not in `venues.csv`, and the `modo-live` cluster there holds The Pearl alone (as "Operators, clusters and presenters" says). If they were listed, they would share a cluster with The Pearl, so neither could be The Pearl's placebo nor take it as theirs.
- Capacity: none read. The venue's own 'Venue Pack' is a Google Drive PDF ('LEE'S PALACE - DECK.pdf'); its view page is drawn by JavaScript and robots.txt bars the download URL, so no figure could be read. Wikipedia's '450-600' is not the venue's own figure, so the rooms are "size unknown" (Amendment 8).
- Programme: mostly live band shows in both rooms, plus some themed DJ, party, tribute and rave nights; the FAQ also mentions occasional seated comedy and burlesque events. This is only a description; nothing was filtered by it.
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.leespalace.com 19 lines in requests.log (11 at 200, 8 at 404): 3 on 15 Oct UTC (robots.txt, the home page, /terms), /faqs on 16 Oct 21:22 UTC and 15 in the check, 16 Oct 22:53-22:57 UTC; leespalace.com 2 lines with no answer (robots.txt unreachable, so the home page was refused unread; 15 Oct); leesplaceeventsbucket.s3.amazonaws.com 2 (robots.txt 403, events.json 200; 16 Oct); modo-live.com 6 (robots.txt and the home page on 15 Oct, the home page and /privacy-policy/ in the check on 16 Oct, the home page and /about-modo-live/ on 17 Oct 02:19 UTC); ca.billboard.com 1 (the anniversary article); en.wikipedia.org 1; drive.google.com 2 (robots.txt and the view page) and 1 refusal of the download URL by robots.txt; leapevent.tech 5 (robots.txt, the home page, the privacy policy and /legal/terms-of-use/ in the check, /legal/terms-of-service/ in its review); leapevents.com 1 robots.txt read and events.leapevents.com 2 (robots.txt and /terms, 404) in the review; web.archive.org 3 during scouting on 16 Oct 21:19-21:22 UTC (two CDX lists and the 15 May home copy) and 73 on 18 Oct 02:19-02:55 UTC, when the saved copies were fetched (two CDX lists and the 71 copies read), all at 200. The settle and its check read only those saved copies and made no request (all times UTC).

### The Atlantis (`was-atlantis`, Washington)
- Source: The venue's own HTML listing on its WordPress home page (https://theatlantis.com/). Each `event-list-item` row carries the show date, an optional presented-by field (`topline-info presented-by`), the title, a supporting line, the doors time, an info line and a status button (on sale, sold out, a custom status such as 'MOVED TO 9:30 CLUB', or canceled). The live site keeps only coming shows: the WordPress REST type `atlantis_event` returns [], the Yoast sitemap index has no event sitemap, and the past page /e/buck-meek/ now serves the current home page. S1 and S2 therefore come from a web.archive.org copy of the same own page. No feed was used. Re-admitted on §5 on 18 Oct 2026, after a second check under the 16 Oct readings, because The Pocket inside 7DrumCity became eligible; `candidates.md` had it "not used" on §5 alone.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/20260519030102id_/https://theatlantis.com/ (63 rows, 18 May to 10 Nov 2026; title 'The Atlantis'); the web.archive.org CDX list of the home page (26 status-200 copies, 1 Mar to 2 Oct 2026) and of /e/ pages (95 URLs, collapsed by URL). Live, for date, status, operator and terms checks only: https://theatlantis.com/ (15 Oct), /e/buck-meek/ (16 Oct), /history/, /frequently-asked-questions-2/, /privacy-policy/, /tickets/, /friends-with-benefits/, /mailing-list/, /wp-json/wp/v2/pages, /wp-json/wp/v2/types, /sitemap_index.xml; https://impconcerts.com/ and https://impconcerts.com/history/ for the operator. The second check (18 Oct 2026) re-read every saved body; no request was sent. A refutation check the same night re-read them again with its own parser and scans; it sent no request either.
- Dates checked: These are show dates. For 61 of the 63 rows the date printed on the row equals the show date in the row's own Ticketmaster link (read as URL text, never fetched), e.g. Claire Rosinkranz 18 May 2026 -> '...-05-18-2026', Deer Tick 12 Jun 2026 -> '...-06-12-2026', Edgehill 22 Sep 2026 -> '...-09-22-2026'; the other 2 are the two moved rows, whose links carry no date. 62 of the 63 rows give a doors time only ('Doors 6:30 PM', 7:00 PM, 7:30 PM, and 11:30 AM for one family matinee) and the moved 27 Aug row gives no time; none is between 00:01 and 04:59, so Amendment 3 moves no date. The venue's live listings of 15 and 16 Oct 2026 UTC match I.M.P.'s own listing (16 Oct UTC) night for night from 16 Oct to 10 Nov (27 nights in each), so the venue's list is the operator's full list for the room.
- robots.txt (15 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://theatlantis.com/robots.txt (200, Yoast block): 'User-agent: *', 'Disallow: /wp-admin/', 'Allow: /wp-admin/admin-ajax.php', 'Crawl-delay: 10' (honoured), and Sitemap lines for the I.M.P. sites; no Anthropic group. https://impconcerts.com/robots.txt has the same block. web.archive.org's robots.txt answered 404 to the fetcher, which allows the snapshot and CDX paths. polite.py allowed every URL read. No bot check and no 403 from the venue host: every theatlantis.com answer was 200 or 404, the 404s are the site's own 'Page not found - The Atlantis' page, and no saved body has 'challenge-platform' or 'cf-chl' (Cloudflare serves e-mail protection only). The host atlantisdc.com, tried in scouting, is not the venue's site: its robots.txt did not answer, so the fetcher refused it.
- Terms (15-16 Oct 2026 UTC; re-checked 18 Oct 2026 from the saved bodies): copyright-only. The footer of every saved page body that reaches it reads 'Privacy Policy 2020-2026 All rights reserved © The Atlantis Site by WLCR', a bare notice (Amendment 14); the two live home-page bodies and the 19 May copy are cut off before the footer. There is no terms page (the REST page list has nine pages, none of them terms). The privacy policy covers visitors' own data and has no use licence and no clause on copying, reuse or automated access. A scan of the visible text of all 18 saved theatlantis.com bodies, the 2 impconcerts.com bodies and the 19 May copy found no text that forbids copying, reproducing, republishing or automated reading, or limits use to personal viewing (the hits for 'automated' and 'terms of service' are the SMS consent line of the mailing-list form). That line links 'Terms of Service' to Hive (https://www.hive.co/terms-and-conditions), an e-mail and SMS marketing vendor; the saved Hive page (title 'Terms and Conditions') is headed 'Hive Enterprise Terms and Conditions' and its readable text is navigation only. Hive does not host the site, so under the platform reading its terms are not the site's terms. Checked: https://theatlantis.com/ (footer); https://theatlantis.com/terms (404); https://theatlantis.com/terms-of-use/ (404); https://theatlantis.com/terms-of-service/ (404); https://theatlantis.com/terms-and-conditions/ (404); https://theatlantis.com/legal/ (404); https://theatlantis.com/privacy-policy/; https://theatlantis.com/frequently-asked-questions-2/; https://theatlantis.com/history/; https://theatlantis.com/tickets/; https://theatlantis.com/friends-with-benefits/; https://theatlantis.com/mailing-list/; https://theatlantis.com/wp-json/wp/v2/pages?search=terms (empty); https://theatlantis.com/wp-json/wp/v2/pages; https://impconcerts.com/ (footer: no terms link); https://www.hive.co/terms-and-conditions
- Platform: Self-hosted WordPress (wp-content, Yoast SEO; 'Site by WLCR', a web agency, not a platform with terms), served through Cloudflare. Not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue, Webflow or Weebly, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer: 'rockhouse', 'etix', 'squarespace', 'wix.com', 'wixstatic', 'fatsoma', 'musicglue' and 'music glue' occur in none of the 18 theatlantis.com bodies, the 2 impconcerts.com bodies or the 19 May copy. The 19 May copy is the real listing page (63 rows), not a bot-check or hosting-check page, so it counts. Tickets are sold through Ticketmaster and the I.M.P. box offices; Ticketmaster does not host the site.
- Operator: I.M.P., owner and house booker. 'I.M.P. is an independent concert promotion and production company' and 'On May 30, 2023 I.M.P. unveiled its smallest room, The Atlantis' (https://impconcerts.com/history/); 'In 2023, I.M.P. opened its newest venue, The Atlantis' (the venue's Zine Archive page, https://theatlantis.com/zine-archive/, in the saved REST page list). The venue's FAQ and tickets pages sell tickets 'online through Ticketmaster or in person at the Box Offices at The Anthem, 9:30 Club, Lincoln Theatre, The Atlantis, and Merriweather Post Pavilion' and say 'I.M.P. does not endorse using any secondary ticketing sources'. The site names no booker by that word; no outside company presents more than half of the counted rows (see below), so the house booker is I.M.P., the company that owns and runs the room (Amendment 14). I.M.P.'s chairman, a private owner named on the history page, is reported in press as its owner. Web searches in the 15 Oct check (Pollstar 2019; dcist and fox5dc 2023 opening coverage naming I.M.P. as owner and booker; Digital Music News, Feb 2026, on the US v. Live Nation witness list, where I.M.P.'s chairman is a plaintiff-side witness; Retail Technology Innovation Hub, Apr 2026; businesswire 2025; none saved, no request in requests.log) found no Live Nation or AEG owner, stake of any size, joint venture or booking deal. On I.M.P.'s own listing (16 Oct 2026 UTC), 'Live Nation Presents...' and 'AEG Presents...' nights are at The Anthem and Merriweather Post Pavilion, and one 'I.M.P and AEG Present...' night is at Capital One Arena; none is at The Atlantis. §1 passes under Amendment 14. Ticketing is Ticketmaster, a Live Nation Entertainment company, used as a ticket vendor; it is not an owner or a booker. This is the reading `candidates.md` already applies to Antone's Nightclub ('Ticketmaster is the ticket vendor only').
- Presenters: the presented-by field holds a company on 5 of the 63 rows, all 'All Good Presents...'. 4 of them are counted rows (27 May, 28 May and 25 Jun in S1; 24 Jul in S2), 4 of 52, an outside promoter on a minority of rows, so not a house booker (Amendment 14). One more S2 row names its presenter in the title only: 'Brooklyn Vegan Presents: Swervedriver raise at 35' (26 Aug). Under the literal reading of Amendment 1 they go into `presented_by`; neither is named on any saved row of The Pocket inside 7DrumCity, so Washington keeps two clusters as far as the saved pages show.
- Rooms: one. The rows have no room or venue field and the page has no room filter. Shows moved to another building are marked in the title and in the status button ('MOVED TO 9:30 CLUB') and are off-site rows (Amendment 1).
- Coverage: S1 >=27, S2 25 (counted on the one web.archive.org copy read, 19 May 2026, the 15 Oct record's second check and this check, each with its own parser, give the same rows). S1: 28 rows from 18 May to 25 Jun minus 1 off-site ('Trinix - Moved to 9:30 Club', 6 Jun) = 27, a lower bound, because 1 Apr to 17 May is not in that copy. S2: 26 rows (Jul 6, Aug 9, Sep 11) minus 1 off-site ('Tiffany Day - HALO TOUR - Moved to 9:30 Club', 27 Aug) = 25, as listed in advance on 19 May; it is not a strict lower bound, because a later cancellation or move does not show (the site marks cancellations with a 'Canceled' button, and no row of the 19 May copy has one). No title is empty, no title has an Amendment 4 word (0 removed in either period), and no two rows share a night and an act ('Wild Child' 1 Aug and 'Wild Child For Kids' 2 Aug are different nights), so Amendment 12 drops no duplicate. The saved copy is cut at exactly 5 MiB inside the 10 Nov row, after every S1 and S2 row. Not read, because web.archive.org was stopped in polite.py after its second 429 (00:38 on 17 Oct 2026, server time) and no request was sent on 18 Oct: the 7 home-page copies from 1 Mar to 1 May (any of them could raise S1: a March copy lists coming shows, so it can carry April nights) and the 18 copies after 19 May (23 May; 1 Jun twice, 10 and 14 Jun; 26 Jun five times; 4 Jul three times, 5 and 21 Jul; 22 Aug twice; 2 Oct). The /e/ CDX list holds 26 event pages first captured on or after 19 May that are not rows of the 19 May copy: 14 of them are dated after 30 Sep by the 15-17 Oct listings, and the other 12 have no date in any saved body; any of these in S1 or S2 would only raise the counts. S2 falls under 20 only if 6 or more of the 25 were later canceled or moved. The only advance rows that can be followed to a later listing are the 6 rows of the 19 May copy dated 16 Oct to 10 Nov: the 15 Oct live listing still lists all 6 on the same nights and marks 1 of them canceled (10 Nov). Test room on these counts; it becomes pool if `shows.csv` gives under 20 in either period, and the venue then leaves, since it has no other room (Amendment 12).
- Capacity: 450 ('the brand new 450-capacity venue', https://impconcerts.com/history/, the operator's figure; the venue's own page says only 'less than half the capacity of the current 9:30 Club').
- §5: The Washington metro (Washington-Arlington-Alexandria, DC-VA-MD-WV MSA, OMB Bulletin 23-01) now has an eligible venue of another cluster, The Pocket inside 7DrumCity (settled eligible on 18 Oct 2026, booked in house, not by I.M.P.), so §5 passes; Pie Shop, another cluster, was settled eligible on 18 Oct 2026 (batch 5) as well. I.M.P.'s other rooms (9:30 Club, The Anthem, Lincoln Theatre) are the same cluster and are not in `venues.csv`; Merriweather Post Pavilion lies in Columbia, MD, in the Baltimore MSA. Black Cat is excluded (Amendment 11). §6: Washington is not in Study 1's metro, and The Atlantis is neither Hideout nor Black Cat.
- Requests during the eligibility check: theatlantis.com 18 plus robots.txt (19 lines in requests.log: 3 on 15 Oct 2026 UTC, 16 on 16 Oct UTC), impconcerts.com 2 plus robots.txt (3 lines, 16 Oct UTC), web.archive.org 3 (the home-page CDX list, the /e/ CDX list and the 19 May copy, 16 Oct UTC; 4 more requests for the /e/ list and the copy were refused locally by polite.py after a 429 or a 503 on another venue and never sent), www.hive.co 1 plus robots.txt (2 lines, 16 Oct UTC); atlantisdc.com and www.atlantisdc.com 4 lines (2 robots.txt reads that did not answer, 2 home pages refused locally). None on 18 Oct 2026: the second check used only saved bodies (see requests.log)

### Pie Shop (`was-pieshop`, Washington)
- Source: The venue's own Webflow show list at https://www.pieshopdc.com/shows, with one page per show at /shows/<slug> (date, doors and show time). The page holds two Webflow collection lists: a calendar list capped at 100 items (slug, title and full date; the newest items, unsorted, so it keeps some past shows) and an upcoming grid that is not capped (slug, month, day, time and title, no year). The homepage shows the next 5 shows, with a featured one that names its presenter. The live site has no past listing and no sitemap, and the live calendar list (1 Jul 2026 to 23 Jan 2027) misses shows because of its cap, so S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of the own /shows page and homepage, joined with the live /shows list.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://www.pieshopdc.com/shows for ts 20260310214422, 20260605222814 and 20260630235712 (the 30 Jun body stored gzip-compressed); .../https://www.pieshopdc.com/ for ts 20260303144259, 20260430181248, 20260514171420, 20260518190851, 20260605231429 and 20260630235711 (the 30 Jun body gzip) (9 copies, all used as listings); the CDX lists of www.pieshopdc.com/shows, www.pieshopdc.com/ and pieshopdc.com/ (1 Mar to 1 Jul 2026, status 200); live https://www.pieshopdc.com/shows (16 Oct 2026 UTC, the source of 44 S2 rows), the live homepage, and the live show pages /shows/keaton-schiller-w-birthday-girl-massie-01-jul and /shows/jake-blount-17-sep for date and presenter checks
- Dates checked: These are show dates. Live show pages: Keaton Schiller, 'July 1, 2026', 'Doors 7:30 pm', 'Show 8:00 pm'; Jake Blount, 'September 17, 2026', 'Show 8:00 pm'; the live homepage grid gives 16-21 Oct at 8:00 pm, matching the slugs. Calendar items carry a full date; grid items carry no year, so the year comes from the capture year and goes up when the month goes down. The independent check found no slug whose calendar date and grid date disagree. One slug, 'divorce-19-sep', is listed with the date 3 Apr 2026 in the 10 Mar grid and calendar; the listed date was used. Every listed start lies between 13:30 and 20:30 (34 rows give a date only), so Amendment 3 moves no date.
- robots.txt (15 and 16 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://www.pieshopdc.com/robots.txt returned 200 with an empty body (0 bytes), which allows every path; no Anthropic group. The apex pieshopdc.com redirects to www. polite.py allowed every venue URL read; web.archive.org allowed the snapshot and CDX paths.
- Terms (15-16 Oct 2026 UTC; the archive copies read 18 Oct): none-found. Every footer reads only '© , Pie Shop', 'Privacy', 'Accessibility' and 'Powered by Opendate'. The privacy policy (an Opendate-style template ending '© 2024 Pie Shop. All rights reserved.', a bare notice under Amendment 14) covers visitors' own data; it mentions 'our website terms', but no such page exists, and it has no use licence, copying, reproduction, personal-use or scraping clause. A second check re-read the saved bodies, read /page/press, /order-online and /menu, and found no page that links to any terms. Checked: https://www.pieshopdc.com/ (footer); https://www.pieshopdc.com/privacy; https://www.pieshopdc.com/accessibility; https://www.pieshopdc.com/about; https://www.pieshopdc.com/booking; https://www.pieshopdc.com/contact; https://www.pieshopdc.com/page/press; https://www.pieshopdc.com/order-online; https://www.pieshopdc.com/menu; https://www.pieshopdc.com/terms (404); https://www.pieshopdc.com/terms-of-use (404); https://www.pieshopdc.com/terms-of-service (404); https://www.pieshopdc.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://www.pieshopdc.com/legal (404); https://www.pieshopdc.com/sitemap.xml (404)
- Platform: Webflow (data-wf-site on every page, assets on cdn.prod.website-files.com), which passes as for Horseshoe Tavern and Off Broadway (reading 4); the footer reads 'Powered by Opendate', and tickets are sold through Opendate, whose terms bind organizers, not the people who view their listings (see Off Broadway); no Opendate page was read for this venue. Not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma, Music Glue or Weebly, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' or Etix footer on the live site or in any copy. No bot check and no 403 in front of the listings: every venue-host request was a 200 or a 404 (or an apex redirect to www); the 'captcha' strings are Google reCAPTCHA on the newsletter form only, and the 'cloudflare' strings are the cdnjs FullCalendar library. The wf3 host table lists pieshopdc.com as 'unclear', bot check false. All 9 web.archive.org copies are real Webflow pages with show data (HTTP 200), none a challenge, error or empty page.
- Operator: private owners (an owner and a co-owner, private persons, named in 2018 press found by web search: https://dcist.com/story/18/08/01/team-behind-dangerously-delicious-p/ and https://washingtonian.com/2018/09/07/dangerously-delicious-pies-concert-venue/; no press page was saved). The venue's about page reads 'Pie Shop is an independent woman owned Washington D.C. legacy small business ... Pie Shop is also an independent live music venue'; https://independentvenueweek.com/us/venues-us/pie-shop-2/ and https://www.livemusicsociety.org/music-in-action-2025/pie-shop list it as independent and locally owned. No company owner is identified. Booking is in-house: show pages read 'Pie Shop Presents' above the title, the booking form on https://www.pieshopdc.com/booking is the venue's own, and the 2018 press says a co-owner does most of the booking. Tickets are sold on Opendate, not Ticketmaster or AXS. No public source names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake, booking deal or sale, and no 2025-2026 sale or new-owner news was found; no copy has a Live Nation, AEG, Ticketmaster, AXS or Goldenvoice mark. A LinkedIn company page lists an employee who is head talent buyer at Union Stage Presents, an independent DC promoter that is not Live Nation or AEG; nothing shows that this person books Pie Shop. Presenters: the listings name a presenter only on the homepage's featured show and on show pages, so 157 of the 163 rows name none. 'Pie Shop Presents' is on 5 rows and one outside presenter, Ripping Headaches Presents, on 1 row (5 Jun); all 6 are among the 162 counted rows, far below half. No company presents more than half, so there is no outside house booker.
- Rooms: one, the single music room upstairs at 1339 H St NE. The listings and show pages have no room field or filter, and the show pages' venue field reads only 'Pie Shop'. It is read as the single room of a one-room venue (as Night & Day Cafe, Martyrs' and Hotel Utah Saloon), not as a split building's pool room, so it meets coverage by itself and is a test room (Amendment 12). Capacity: no figure published by the venue (press gives about 60-70, which is not a venue figure).
- Coverage: S1 >=79, S2 >=83 (one room, written as `pieshopdc-com:house` in the settle `shows.csv`, `was-pieshop` when compiled; union by slug of the 3 /shows copies, the 6 homepage copies and the live 16 Oct /shows list, oldest first, so the latest copy that lists a show sets its fields; settled on 18 Oct 2026 from the copies the prefetch saved earlier that day, after web.archive.org had stopped the 16-17 Oct check, with 0 requests by the settle or its check). Listed rows: S1 80 (Apr 29, May 28, Jun 23), S2 83 (Jul 29, Aug 27, Sep 27). Removed by Amendment 4 words: 1 S1 row (4 Jun, a band name that holds the whole word 'class'); it does not count for coverage, and it stays in `shows.csv` with an empty drop_reason, as the run drops it as non-music only if the word lies outside the name the headliner rule resolves. No row is canceled, off-site or untitled. S1 rows come from the /shows copies (75) and the homepage copies (5); 44 S2 rows are on the live list and 39 rest only on the 5 Jun or 30 Jun copy. The independent check upheld both counts and corrected nothing: its own parser gave the same 163 (date, title) pairs. It gives robust floors of S1 55 (rows listed in a copy taken on or after the show date) and S2 44 (the live list alone). The scouting record's S2 of 44 is superseded: the live calendar list is capped at 100 newest items, and the 5 Jun and 30 Jun copies add 39 S2 rows (38 last listed on 30 Jun, 1 on 5 Jun). The scouting check's note that the 8 Sep row (slug the-4411) has a placeholder title, which would give 43, is a parse artefact: the list title is 'The 4411 w/ Juniper' and the placeholder sits in other fields, so no title is empty. Gaps, so the counts are lower bounds: (a) shows dated 11 Mar to 4 Jun that were first listed after 10 Mar and had left the 100-item calendar by 5 Jun (the homepage copies of 30 Apr to 5 Jun show 5 shows each, and every one is in the union); (b) shows dated Jul to Sep that were first listed after 30 Jun and pushed out of the live 100-item calendar (an aggregator snippet, not a source, mentions a 25 Sep show missing from the list); (c) the source has no cancel marker, so a show canceled and deleted after the last copy that lists it still counts. One case of (c): an 18 Jul burlesque night is in the uncapped grid on 10 Mar and 5 Jun but gone from both lists on 30 Jun, probably canceled; under the rules it counts (S2 would be 82 without it). Rows that are not concerts but carry no Amendment 4 word stay counted: a 4 Apr fundraiser speed-dating night, burlesque nights on 1 Apr and 18 Jul, two World Cup semi-final screenings (14-15 Jul), 'H St. Festival - BAR OPEN' (19 Sep) and 'Drag Talk' (24 Sep). Programme: almost all local and touring band bills (indie, punk, metal, hip-hop, folk). The CDX lists 3 /shows captures from 1 Mar to 1 Jul 2026 (all read) and 8 homepage captures (6 read); the 2 May and 26 Jun homepage captures were not read and could only add rows, and the check found no further capture needed.
- §5: The Washington metro (Washington-Arlington-Alexandria, DC-VA-MD-WV MSA) also has The Pocket (`was-pocket`, 7DrumCity, booked in house), another cluster, settled eligible on 18 Oct 2026 with a test room, so §5 passes for both; The Atlantis (`was-atlantis`, I.M.P.), re-admitted on §5 on 18 Oct 2026, is a third cluster. Black Cat is excluded (Amendment 11). Washington sorts after the cut, so Pie Shop is not added to `venues.csv` and is not compiled (Amendment 12, cap). §6: Washington is not the Minneapolis-St Paul metro, and Pie Shop is not Black Cat, the Study 1 room excluded by Amendment 11.
- Requests during the eligibility check: www.pieshopdc.com 23 without robots.txt reads (4 in scouting on 15 Oct UTC, 1 on 16 Oct at 20:30 UTC, 15 in the check on 16 Oct 21:50-21:52 UTC and 3 in the second check at 21:55 UTC; 16 at 200, 7 at 404); with its 2 robots.txt reads, requests.log has 25 lines. pieshopdc.com 4, each a redirect to www, plus 2 robots.txt reads. web.archive.org 18: 3 CDX queries and 15 copy requests fetched by the prefetch on 18 Oct 2026, 03:16-03:24 UTC, about 30 s apart (the 3 /shows copies once and the 6 homepage copies twice each); the check of 16-17 Oct made none, as the archive was stopped. Source: requests.log. The record counts 17 venue-host requests for its check; the log has 18 lines for 16 Oct 21:50-21:52 UTC (15 www pages, 2 robots.txt reads, 1 apex redirect).

### The Pocket (`was-pocket`, Washington)
- Source: The venue's own Webflow pages on thepocket.7drumcity.com, the listings subdomain of 7DrumCity, the music school and rehearsal business that houses the room. /shows and the homepage / carry two lists: a CMS calendar list (all items in date order, Webflow's 100-item cap, no pagination) and an upcoming-shows grid (month, day, time and title of each show from the capture day on). /shows-preview carries the grid only. Each show has an own page /events/<slug> (title, location line, date, time, 'Presented by' line). There is no sitemap.xml (404), RSS (/events/rss.xml, 404) or site search (/search, 404), and slugs carry no year. The live list stops at 21 Apr 2026 (the 100-item cap) and the live grid starts in Oct 2026, so S1/S2 come from web.archive.org copies of these own pages, united by slug. The main site www.7drumcity.com (Weebly) holds no show rows: its /the-pocket.html page only embeds an iframe of /shows-preview.
- Read: https://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/shows for ts 20260308155200, 20260602175647, 20260618132506, 20260724003947, 20260925210656 (the copy 20260715023931 was not used: its saved body is byte-identical to the 24 Jul copy, and its grid starts 23 Jul); .../https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/ for ts 20260303224711, 20260506150037, 20260521194131, 20260618135654; .../https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/shows-preview for ts 20260521203753, 20260626125739; 60 copies of /events/<slug> pages (59 slugs, one captured twice; captured 3 Mar to 25 Sep 2026), picked from the CDX prefix list of thepocket.7drumcity.com/events/ (73 rows); 14 copies of https://www.7drumcity.com/the-pocket.html (1 Mar to 1 Oct 2026, iframe only, no show rows). CDX lists: /shows (6 rows), / (4), /shows-preview (2), /events/ by prefix (73), www.7drumcity.com/the-pocket.html (193). Every /shows, / and /shows-preview capture in the CDX lists was read. Live pages (16 Oct 2026) for date, terms and operator checks only: https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/shows, /, /shows-preview, three April event pages and the 7 Jul open mic page (/events/the-pocket-open-mic-07-jul, 'July 7, 2026', read in the first check to see that the venue was active in S2); https://www.7drumcity.com/about-our-venue.html, /info.html, /privacy-policy.html, /safety--reporting.html.
- Dates checked: These are show dates. Live /events/monoceans-11-apr: 'April 11, 2026 • 8:00 pm, Doors 7:30 PM | Show 8:00 PM', the date its calendar row gives ('April 11, 2026'; grid 'Apr 11', '8:00 pm'). The first check found the same on two more April pages. Noble Dust + Griefcat: grid 'Aug 09', '8:00 pm' in four copies (2 Jun to 24 Jul) = event page copy 20260618140109 'August 9, 2026', '8:00 pm', location 'The Pocket'. Foot Traffic: grid 'Jun 25', '8:00 pm' = event page copy 20260618143417 'June 25, 2026', '8:00 pm'. The grid gives month and day only, so the year is the capture's; no slug carries two dates across copies. One show whose slug ends '-17-apr' is listed for 7 May and is written at 7 May. Counted starts run 19:00 to 20:30 (4 April calendar rows give no time), so Amendment 3 moves no date.
- robots.txt (15-16 Oct 2026 UTC): allowed. https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/robots.txt answers 200 with an empty body (0 bytes), which allows every path; no Anthropic group. https://www.7drumcity.com/robots.txt and https://7drumcity.com/robots.txt answered 200, and polite.py allowed every venue-host page read. web.archive.org allowed the CDX and copy paths (18 Oct 2026).
- Terms (15-16 Oct 2026 UTC): silent. No terms page on either host. The only notice is the bare footer 'The Pocket © 2010-2026. All Rights Reserved.' on www.7drumcity.com (Amendment 14: not terms). The privacy policy (HTML page and a March 2025 PDF) covers personal data and SMS only, with no clause on use or licence of content. A second check re-read the www.7drumcity.com bodies (home, the-pocket, about-our-venue, info, privacy-policy, safety--reporting) and found no copy, reproduce, personal-use or scraping clause; the settle check found no terms text, 'reproduce', 'personal use' or noindex in the Webflow copies. Checked: https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/shows (footer); https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/terms (404); https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/terms-of-use (404); https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/terms-of-service (404); https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/terms-and-conditions (404); https://thepocket.7drumcity.com/legal (404); https://www.7drumcity.com/terms (404); https://www.7drumcity.com/terms.html (404); https://www.7drumcity.com/terms-of-use.html (404); https://www.7drumcity.com/privacy-policy.html; https://www.7drumcity.com/uploads/4/4/2/7/4427384/7dc_privacy_policy_v2__march_18_2025_.pdf; https://www.7drumcity.com/info.html; https://www.7drumcity.com/about-our-venue.html
- Platform: the listings are Webflow (thepocket.7drumcity.com), which passes as read for Horseshoe Tavern (reading 4). The main site www.7drumcity.com is Weebly, whose terms leave hosted sites to their own terms and do not count (as for Make-Out Room). Ticket buttons use an Opendate tag (app.opendate.io); Opendate is a ticket platform that does not host the site, and its terms were read for Off Broadway (they bind organizers, not viewers). The about page names Eventbrite as 'our ticketing partner'. Not Squarespace, Wix, Fatsoma or Music Glue, and no 'Powered by ROCKHOUSE PARTNERS an ETIX company' footer in any body ('rhp' hits are inside an integrity hash). No bot check and no 403: all 86 web.archive.org copies are 200 with full content, and every venue-host request was a 200 or a 404. The 'captcha' strings are a reCAPTCHA form script on the Weebly pages, and the 'cloudflare' strings are email obfuscation and a cdnjs link.
- Operator (§1, Amendment 14): owner 7DrumCity, a DC music school and rehearsal business; its owner and founder is a private person, named in https://dcist.com/story/19/10/04/7drumcity-is-opening-a-new-concert-venue-in-truxton-circle ('It's almost like a supplement to our core business.'). House booker: in-house, named by the venue. The staff list on https://www.7drumcity.com/info.html includes the role 'The Pocket Booker' (a 7DrumCity staff member), and https://www.7drumcity.com/about-our-venue.html sends performers to the venue's own thepocket@ address ('DO NOT CALL THE FRONT DESK LINE OR DM US ON SOCIALS REGARDING BOOKING.'). That rests on the live pages of 16 Oct, not on the archive copies; nothing in the copies contradicts it. So the operator is 7DrumCity. Outside presenters, of the 78 counted S1 + S2 rows: Open Gem 5 (3 S1, 2 S2; 'Open Gem Presents Fresh Produce'), The Garage 2 (1 S1, 1 S2; 'The Garage Presents: ...') and AYA Collective 1 (S1, 20 Jun; its co-presenter on that row is a private person, counted, not written). 'Presented by 7DrumCity', the owner, appears only on open mic event pages (26 page copies, 16 of them for S1 or S2 nights), rows the words remove. No company presents more than half of the counted rows, so none is a house booker and none joins a cluster. No Live Nation, AEG, Ticketmaster, AXS or Etix marker in any body, and the first check found no public source that names a Live Nation or AEG owner, stake or booker. The second check of 16 Oct could not run its own recent-news search (search budget used up).
- Rooms: one. The listings have no room field or filter, and every event page copy gives the location 'The Pocket', the venue itself. All rows go to the one room; nothing is off-site.
- Coverage: S1 >=42, S2 >=36, lower bounds (the settle report and the settle `shows.csv`, 106 rows, all at `7drumcity-com:house`, the settle's name for the venue's one room, which is its test room `was-pocket`, not a pool room). Settled on 17-18 Oct 2026 from saved web.archive.org copies (read on 18 Oct, 00:04-00:49 UTC, and counted offline), after web.archive.org stopped the 16-17 Oct check, which had S1 >=10 (1-21 Apr only) and S2 unknown and left the venue undecided. Rows listed in the periods: 56 S1, 50 S2. 28 removed by Amendment 4 words: 26 'open mic' rows (13 in each period, the weekly Tuesday open mic), 'Live Karaoke' (24 May) and 'Something Like The Apollo: Performance Workshop' (5 Jul). No row is marked canceled or postponed, none is off-site and none is untitled. An independent second check recounted from the same bodies with its own scripts and got the same numbers (union of 228 shows by slug over all dates, the same 106 rows); it corrected no count or reading and upheld both verdicts. Not exact, but no night is wholly unseen: 1-21 Apr is complete as the CMS kept it (every /shows and / copy carries the calendar list from 2 Oct 2025 to 21 Apr 2026, and the 25 Sep copy still lists 1-21 Apr). After that only the grid helps, so shows announced after one copy and played before the next are missed: 22 Apr-6 May rest only on the 3 and 8 Mar copies, 7-20 May on 6 May, 21 May-1 Jun on 21 May, 2-17 Jun on 2 Jun, 18-25 Jun on 18 Jun, 26 Jun-19 Jul on the 26 Jun /shows-preview (16 items, to 19 Jul), 20-22 Jul only on the 18 Jun copies, 23 Jul-24 Sep only on the 24 Jul copy (two months) and 25-30 Sep on 25 Sep. One counted row (14 Jun) comes only from its event page copy. The 13 /events/ captures not picked are shows already in the union. A vanish test (a show listed earlier and missing from a later copy whose window covers its date) finds only Live Karaoke (24 May), which the words remove anyway. The house room meets coverage by itself, so it is a test room (Amendment 12). Note: 11 Jul 'Yacht Rock Flashband Showcase' is a showcase of the owner's student-band programme; no Amendment 4 word catches it and it counts (S2 35 without it).
- §5: The Washington metro (Washington-Arlington-Alexandria, DC-VA-MD-WV MSA) has two eligible venues of other clusters: Pie Shop (booked in house as 'Pie Shop Presents', private owners; settled eligible on 18 Oct 2026 with a test room, S1 >=79, S2 >=83) and The Atlantis (I.M.P., S1 >=27, S2 25), which `candidates.md` had "not used" on §5 alone and which was re-admitted on §5 on 18 Oct 2026 together with this row. Either one gives The Pocket its §5 partner. Black Cat is excluded (Amendment 11), and Bossa is still undecided. Washington sorts after the cut, so The Pocket is not added to `venues.csv` and is not compiled (Amendment 12, cap). §6: Washington is not Minneapolis-St Paul, and The Pocket is not Black Cat (Amendment 11).
- Capacity: 75, the venue's own figure ('With 75 capacity, the space creates a special listening room', https://www.7drumcity.com/about-our-venue.html; the-pocket.html says '75-capacity venue').
- Requests during the eligibility check: thepocket.7drumcity.com 18 (10 at 200, 8 at 404; 16 Oct), www.7drumcity.com 13 (10 at 200, 3 at 404; 15-16 Oct), 7drumcity.com 3 (15-16 Oct), thepocketdc.com 2 (robots.txt, 404, and the home page; 15 Oct, a different host, not used), dcist.com 2 (robots.txt, 404, and the article; 16 Oct), web.archive.org 91 (5 CDX lists and 86 copies, 18 Oct 00:04-00:49 UTC). - The Night Cat (Melbourne): the only eligible venue in the metro. S1 >=61, S2 >=54 (settled 18 Oct 2026).
